  - Binding: Open MPI's default is kept (foam/parallel.DEFAULT_BINDING = "default"). The bound runs were faster at 2 and 4 ranks on one mesh and slower at 2 ranks on the other and at 8 ranks; the differences (4-15 %) are within what single runs on this desktop vary, so neither is clearly better. foam/parallel.BINDINGS keeps both placements and SolverRun takes a `binding` argument (used by the study).
  - Recommended cores, shown read-only on the Solvers node ("Recommended cores: N (C cells, 50,000 per core)", "-- (generate the mesh first)" without a mesh): min(physical cores, cells of the current mesh / 50,000 rounded down, 4), at least 1 (foam/parallel.recommended_cores). The cap of 4 is the adjustment from the study: on every mesh measured (192k, 565k, 990k cells) the parallel efficiency is at least 0.5 up to 4 ranks and 0.28-0.37 at 8, and it follows the number of ranks rather than the cells per rank, i.e. the solver is memory-bandwidth bound on this two-channel machine. The 50,000 cells per core lower bound of the request is kept (the 192k sphere still halves its time on 4 cores, 48k cells per core). It is a recommendation only: the cores spin box still goes up to the physical cores.
- Alternatives considered: `--bind-to core --map-by core` as the default (not clearly better); the formula without a cap (would recommend 12 cores for 1M cells, where 8 already give only 3.0); a threshold of 250,000 cells per core (would recommend serial runs for the 192k sphere, which runs twice as fast on 4 cores); a cap measured per machine at first start (more machinery; the cap is a constant to revisit on other hardware).
- Consequences: the recommendation is machine-specific (EFFICIENT_CORES = 4 is a constant in foam/parallel.py; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 69). Every real run above 60 s, including this study, carries the `long` marker (D-043).

## D-066 Mesh independence study with the Celik GCI (schema v7)
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (Task 6.4, [NUMERICS-REVIEW]). D-065 was not used: the freestream far field of Part B was not applied (docs/QUESTIONS.md item 71).
- Context: PLAN 6.4: judge grid convergence of chosen reports on a family of systematically refined meshes with the Celik et al. (2008) procedure already used by the flat plate study (D-036), without touching the user's case.
- Decision:
  - GCI (foam/gci.py, no Qt; the flat plate study now imports it): Celik, Ghia, Roache, Freitas, Coleman, Raad, J. Fluids Eng. 130 (2008) 078001: apparent order p by fixed-point iteration of p = |ln|eps32/eps21| + q(p)| / ln r21 with q(p) = ln((r21^p - s) / (r32^p - s)), phi_ext^21, e_a^21, e_ext^21, GCI_fine^21 = 1.25 e_a^21 / (r21^p - 1), GCI_medium^32, and the asymptotic-range ratio GCI_medium^32 / (r21^p GCI_fine^21) (in range within 0.1 of 1). Oscillatory convergence (eps21 and eps32 of opposite sign) gets no number: "oscillatory, GCI not applicable". A p > 0 that does not reproduce the data (the change grows with refinement: phi = phi0 + C h^p requires eps32 / eps21 = r21^p (r32^p - 1) / (r21^p - 1)) is "divergent", equal solutions on two grids "undefined"; none of them gets a GCI. Tested against Table 1, first column, of the paper (p 1.534, phi_ext 6.1685, GCI_fine 2.175 %, GCI_medium 4.113 %; the third, oscillatory column gets no number), exact power laws with equal and unequal ratios, and the divergent and undefined cases.
  - Model (schema v7, migration v6 -> v7 adds the defaults): `Simulation.studies.mesh` = `MeshStudySettings`: `ratio` r in linear cell size (default sqrt(2), at least 1.3, at most 4), `current_as` "fine" (default; the study adds two coarser levels) or "medium" (one coarser, one finer), `quantities` (report ids of core/reports.py, default ["Cd"]), `cores` (MPI ranks of each level's run, default 1).
  - Levels (foam/mesh_study.py): linear size factors f = r^2, r, 1 (current = fine) or r, 1, 1/r (current = medium); a level divides the background cell budget by f^3 (or multiplies an explicit base size by f) and multiplies the target surface size by f, so the surface refinement levels, which follow log2(base / target surface size), stay the same and every cell scales by f; explicit level overrides (mesh and per part) are kept. Prism layers: the SAME absolute stack on every level, the first-layer thickness, expansion ratio and number of layers of the current settings (relative sizing: first = final_layer_thickness x surface cell size / ER^(n - 1) at the current maximum surface level; absolute y+ sizing: the y+ first layer and the current computed count), written with relativeSizes false (MeshCaseInput.fixed_layers; the quality controls keep following the project's sizing, so a relative-sized project gets no relaxed set). Reason: the flat plate study (D-036) showed Cf changing 2.6 % between first-cell y+ 1 and 0.5 and then converging; with relative layers the first-cell y+ would change between levels and mix a wall-resolution change into the grid error. The representative cell size is h = (V_domain / N_cells)^(1/3) from the cell count of each level's checkMesh report (not the estimate).
  - Before launching, the Mesh Study node shows per level the estimated cells (background cells x the cells per background cell of the current mesh record, or 1.9 without a mesh, marked "assumed") and the solver time up to the iteration limit from the last run's time per cell-iteration (the run record now stores `duration_s` and `cores`; ideal scaling with the cores assumed; "unknown" without a timed run), and the shared layer stack. A level above the 2,000,000 background-cell limit blocks the study with that reason.
  - Run (foam/mesh_study.StudyRun, gui/mesh_study.py, gui/study_control.py): every level is a marker-protected case <project>/case_study/level_<n>/ (meshing dictionaries, the part STLs and seed lines written before the run; case/ is never written); per level MeshRun, then the project's solver files with the study's cores, then SolverRun with the project's stopping criteria; the final value of every quantity is read from the postProcessing files. A level whose mesh is invalid stops the study ("failed"). A level that does not end with "criteria met" is kept and flagged; every GCI row then says "unreliable: <levels> did not meet the stopping criteria". Stop stops the running step cleanly and ends the study ("stopped"). One OpenFOAM job at a time: meshing, Run Solver and the study block each other with a reason.
  - Results: case_study/study.json (settings, per level cells, h, run state, iterations, time and values, status), a non-modal "Mesh study" window: a table per quantity (h and value per level, p, extrapolated value, GCI fine, asymptotic ratio with in / not in range, note), the levels table and a chart of the chosen quantity against h (QPainter; levels that did not meet their criteria hollow in the warning colour), and Export CSV (one row per quantity).
- Alternatives considered: scaling the surface refinement levels instead of the target size (levels are powers of 2: r = sqrt(2) cannot be represented); relative prism layers on each level (y+ changes with the level); the estimated instead of the actual cell counts for h; running the levels inside case/ (would destroy the user's mesh and results).
- Consequences: real study (tests/test_mesh_study.py, marker `long`, 293 s): the coarse sphere (24 x 24 STL, default domain and mesh as the fine level, r = sqrt(2)) at Re 200 (0.003 m/s), alpha 4 deg, 4 cores, Cd and Cl asymptotic (window 50, 1e-4), all levels met the criteria: coarse 36,995 cells, h 0.37400 m, Cd 0.665034, Cl -0.0274491 (337 iterations); medium 82,567 cells, h 0.28619 m, Cd 0.657493, Cl -0.0222605 (429); fine 192,220 cells, h 0.21593 m, Cd 0.643797, Cl -0.0235317 (449). Cd: "divergent" (the change grows with refinement, 0.0075 then 0.0137: these meshes are not in the asymptotic range), Cl: "oscillatory"; no GCI for either. The actual size ratios are 1.307 and 1.325, not 1.414: the background cells scale with r^3 but the surface and layer cells with about r^2 (docs/QUESTIONS.md item 72). Three meshes and runs cost about three times a normal run plus the meshing.

## D-067 Angle-of-attack sweep (polar)
- Status: Partly superseded by D-120 (one mesh per angle, the parts rotated; no initialisation from the previous point; resumable). Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (Task 6.5, [NUMERICS-REVIEW] for the initialisation from the previous point). The far field is still slip (D-022; the freestream change of Part B was not applied, docs/QUESTIONS.md item 71), which channels the flow at high incidence.
- Context: PLAN 6.5: Cl, Cd, Cm and L/D for a list of angles of attack on one mesh, each run judged by its stopping criteria, without touching the user's case.
- Decision:
  - Model (schema v7, `Simulation.studies.polar` = `PolarSweepSettings`, added in the same schema version as D-066 on the unpublished branch): `mode` "range" (`start`, `end`, `step` > 0; start, start + step, ... up to end inclusive within 1e-9, descending when end < start; at most 1000 points) or "list" (`values` in the order given), every angle within -90..90 deg; `cores`; `init_from_previous` (default on).
  - Run (foam/polar_sweep.py, gui/polar_sweep.py, gui/study_control.py): the current mesh of case/ is copied once into <project>/case_sweep/mesh/polyMesh; every point is case_sweep/point_<n>/ with constant/polyMesh a relative symbolic link to it and the solver files of a normal run at Inflow.alpha_deg = alpha (the inlet velocity and the forceCoeffs lift and drag directions follow alpha, D-050); SolverRun with the project's stopping criteria and the sweep's cores. Points run in order; a point that ends at the iteration limit, is stopped or crashes is kept and flagged; Stop stops the running point with the clean stop (state "stopped") and ends the sweep (later points stay unrun). The sweep needs a valid, up-to-date mesh (the same reasons as Run Solver) and blocks meshing, Run Solver and the mesh study while it runs (and is blocked by them).
  - Initialisation: with `init_from_previous`, a point starts from the latest time of the last point that MET its stopping criteria: the internal fields of U, p, k, omega and nut are copied (foamlib) into the new point's 0/ files, which keep the new point's boundary conditions (the new inlet direction); the iterations of the new point start at 1 (its own stopping-criteria windows and postProcessing files). A point after unconverged points starts from the last converged one, and from the uniform free stream when none converged. Each point records where it started ("point_2 at time 194").
  - Record per point (case_sweep/sweep.json): alpha, CL, CD, CM (pitch), L/D, iterations, state and label, time, initialisation source; CL, CD and CM are the last iteration's forceCoeffs values (none for a crashed point).
  - Results: a non-modal "Polar sweep" window with the table (flagged rows in the warning colour) and three QPainter charts, CL against alpha, CL against CD (drag polar) and CM against alpha, with points that did not meet their criteria drawn hollow in the warning colour; Export CSV (alpha_deg, CL, CD, CM, L_D, iterations, status, criteria_met, initialised_from).
- Alternatives considered: one case directory restarted at each angle (the stopping-criteria windows and the postProcessing series would mix the points); mapFields between points (same mesh: copying the internal fields is exact); initialising from the previous point even when it did not converge (an oscillating or diverged state would be carried over); copying the mesh into every point (the symbolic link saves a mesh copy per point; decomposePar and reconstructPar read through it).
- Consequences: real sweep (tests/test_polar_sweep.py, marker `long`, 262 s): flattened ellipsoid (semi-axes 0.5 x 0.5 x 0.15 m, default mesh, 192,458 cells) at 20 m/s, 4 cores, Cd and Cl asymptotic (window 50, 1 %), limit 600: alpha 0: CL -0.0002199, CD 0.027736, CM 3.07e-5, 173 iterations; alpha 5: CL 0.023284, CD 0.030199, CM 0.0055368, L/D 0.771, 194 iterations (from point 1 at 173); alpha 10: CL 0.040787, CD 0.035768, CM 0.012481, L/D 1.14, 179 iterations (from point 2 at 194); all criteria met, CL increasing with alpha; case/ unchanged. The 10 deg value agrees with the separate slip run of Part B (0.0411). Each point keeps its time directories (about the size of the fields per written time).

## D-068 Display guard and targeted testing for agents
- Status: Accepted (2026-10-21, review follow-up R-39: an owner decision, as this line recorded, "owner decision 2026-10-18, recorded in Task 3 Part 0"; its rules are in CLAUDE.md). Was Proposed.
- Context: agent test runs opened windows on the owner's WSLg desktop, and running the full suite before every commit cost several minutes per commit.
- Decision:
  - `vortexcfd.core.display_guard.apply_display_guard()` (no Qt import) sets `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen` and `PYVISTA_OFF_SCREEN=true` when `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` is set, or `DISPLAY` is set on WSL (the /mnt/wslg mount or `WSL_DISTRO_NAME`), unless `VORTEXCFD_ALLOW_REAL_DISPLAY=1`. tests/conftest.py calls it before importing Qt, and so does every script that opens windows (scripts/screenshots.py). The application itself is not guarded.
  - Testing rule (CLAUDE.md): agents run only the test files of the modules they changed while working, and once at the end of a task `pytest -q -m "not slow and not validation and not long"` offscreen with `nice -n 15`; the full suite, slow, long and validation tests run in scripts/ship.sh when the owner publishes. Agents never use the WSLg display.
- Alternatives considered: setting `QT_QPA_PLATFORM` in pytest.ini (does not cover scripts, and the owner could not opt in to the real display); keeping the full suite before each commit (too slow per commit).
- Consequences: tests that need a real OpenGL viewer render offscreen unless the owner sets `VORTEXCFD_ALLOW_REAL_DISPLAY=1`; regressions in slow tests surface only when ship.sh runs.

## D-069 Multi-solid STL import and self-intersection check
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (Task 3.1 missing items).
- Context: PLAN 3.1 lists multi-solid STL splitting and self-intersection detection; both were missing (D-056 era checks covered edges, duplicates, orientation only).
- Decision:
  - Loader: an STL is read with pv.read; when it is ASCII with more than one `solid` block (geometry/loader.stl_solid_names: names in file order, "solid_<n>" for an unnamed block, repeated names suffixed _2, ...), it is read again with vtkSTLReader's scalar tags and every triangle keeps its block index in the cell array `solid` (0 for binary and single-solid files).
  - Import: a file with several solids asks (Dialogs.ask_solids_mode) for one Part with a region per solid (the default; region names = solid names, region ids written to geometry/<part>_regions_<hash>.npy, D-071) or one Part per solid (each solid written to geometry/<file>_<solid>.stl in the file's units, the combined copy deleted, all parts added as one undo step); Cancel imports nothing.
  - Self-intersections (geometry/intersections.py, numpy only): two triangles sharing no vertex intersect when an edge of one crosses the other (Moller-Trumbore, end points excluded); candidates come from a uniform grid of cell size = the mean triangle box diagonal, each pair kept once (in the cell of the lower corner of the two boxes' overlap). Coplanar overlaps are not found (duplicate faces are reported separately). SurfaceReport gains `self_intersections` (None = not checked) and `intersecting` (triangle indices); `snappy_ready` = watertight, 0 self-intersections, 0 duplicate faces. The Checks group shows a "Self-intersections" row and the viewer draws the selected part's intersecting triangles in the error colour.
  - Measured: 1,005,360-triangle sphere in 7.3-7.9 s (two runs; 178,800 triangles: 1.1 s). The check is skipped above 2,000,000 triangles (`intersections.MAX_TRIANGLES`), with the note "not checked: more than 2,000,000 triangles" in the row and the summary.
- Alternatives considered: pymeshfix's PyTMesh.select_intersecting_triangles (the count matches, 172 on the two-sphere test, but in pymeshfix 0.18.1 the returned triangles hold uninitialised indices, e.g. 35702382 on a 724-point mesh, different on every run, so they cannot be highlighted); deduplicating candidate pairs with np.unique (26 s for 1M triangles); vtkOBBTree line intersections per edge (a Python loop over 1.5M edges).
- Consequences: importing or first selecting a 1M-triangle part takes about 8 s more (the report is cached); surfaces above 2M triangles are not checked for self-intersections.

## D-071 Surface regions and patch assignment (schema v8)
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (Task 3.1 introduced the model; Task 3.5 completed it). [NUMERICS-REVIEW] for the wall BC on every patch and the snappyHexMesh region entries.
- Context: PLAN 3.5: group triangles into named patches from CAD faces, STL solids or an angle-based split; the assignment must persist and reach the meshing dictionary.
- Decision:
  - Model (schema v8): `Part.regions` (list of `SurfaceRegion`: `name`, unique in the part; `members`, base ids; `patch`, a name matching `[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,63}` or None; `color` "#rrggbb" or None), `Part.region_ids` (a .npy file of one int per triangle of the loaded surface, relative to the project folder; None = every triangle has base id 0) and `Part.cad` (D-070). A base id belongs to at most one region; triangles outside every region, and regions with `patch` None, are on the part's own patch, named after the part. A part without regions is one region: v7 projects migrate with nothing to fill and mesh exactly as before.
  - Region id files are named <part>_regions_<12 hex of the content's SHA-256>.npy under <project>/geometry/; an edit writes a new file and changes `region_ids` in the same undoable step, so undo points the part back to the previous file (old files are not deleted). A file whose length differs from the surface's triangle count is an error ("the surface file changed").
  - Regions come from STL solids (D-069), CAD face names (D-070) or the angle split: triangles sharing a manifold edge stay together when their normals differ by less than the feature angle (default: the Mesh feature angle, 30 deg); a CAD part's faces are never split (the split regroups whole faces); an STL part gets a new region id file in which the split region's triangles take new ids; the pieces are named <region>_1, <region>_2, ... and keep the region's patch and colour; more than 500 pieces is refused. Rename, merge (into the first selected region), split, assign a patch (empty = the part's own patch) and "Patch per region" (every region on a patch named after it) are each one undoable `update_part`.
  - GUI: the Part's properties have a Regions group (list "region -> patch", multi-select; Region name, Patch, Feature angle; Split, Merge, Patch per region). The selected regions are drawn in the accent colour; clicking a triangle of the selected part in the viewer selects its region. The Fluid Domain node lists one "<patch> (wall)" per patch (a part without regions shows its name, as before) and the Patch name row lists the part's patches.
  - Mesh case: patch names are made safe and unique across all parts together (`mesh_case.part_patch_names`; a name used by two parts becomes name and name_1). A part whose only patch is its own name is written exactly as before (one STL, no `regions` entries; the inputs hash of a project without regions is unchanged, so existing meshes stay up to date). Any other part's STL in constant/triSurface has one ASCII `solid <patch>` per patch; its geometry entry gets `regions { <patch> { name <patch>; } }`, its refinementSurfaces entry `regions { <patch> { level (min max); patchInfo { type wall; inGroups (<part>Group); } } }` and every patch its own nSurfaceLayers entry. The lost-part guard checks every patch (MeshRun, the mesh study); the solver case gives every patch the wall BCs (U noSlip, p zeroGradient, k kqRWallFunction, omega omegaWallFunction, nut nutUSpaldingWallFunction), and the per-wall force objects, y+ and report lists are per patch. The regions and the region id file content are meshing inputs.
  - Real mesh (tests/test_patches.py, marker slow, 11 s): a 1 m cube split by angle, its -x side on patch "front" and the other five on "body", background target 20,000, surface size 0.05 L, no layers: 46,112 cells, Mesh OK, constant/polyMesh/boundary front 724 faces and body 3,888 faces, both `type wall; inGroups 2(CubeGroup wall)`.
- Alternatives considered: triangle lists in the project JSON (a 1M-triangle part would make the project file huge); region membership recomputed from the source (an edit such as a split or a pick would not be representable); one STL per patch (one triSurfaceMesh per patch: refinement levels and seeding are per part today); rejecting a patch name used by two parts (renamed with a suffix instead).
- Consequences: a project folder accumulates small region id files (4 bytes per triangle) as regions are edited. Per-wall reports (forces, y+) follow the patches, so splitting a part into patches changes its per-part report ids from the part name to the patch names. Triangles outside every region (not produced by any edit) fall on the part's first patch.

## D-070 CAD import (STEP, IGES, BREP) through OCP
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (Task 3.2).
- Context: PLAN 3.2: STEP/IGES/BREP with part and face names and colours, tessellation with controllable deflection. The survey (docs/reference/oss_survey.md) chose OpenCASCADE through OCP with a spike for VTK conflicts first.
- Decision:
  - Dependency: `cadquery-ocp-novtk` 8.0.1.1.0 (OCCT 8 bindings without a bundled VTK; Apache-2.0 bindings, OCCT LGPL-2.1 with exception). Spike: OCP, PyVista 0.49.1 / VTK 9.7.1 and PySide6 6.12 import in one process in either order; a box tessellated by BRepMesh rendered off-screen in a PyVista plotter (17,440 non-background pixels of 200 x 200) and was added to a QtInteractor; no library conflict (`pip check`: no broken requirements).
  - geometry/cad.py (no Qt): `read_cad` reads STEP and IGES through XCAF (STEPCAFControl / IGESCAFControl readers with names and colours; STEP sub-shape names on: `read.stepcaf.subshapes.name` = 1 after `STEPCAFControl_Controller.Init_s()`, which OCCT 8 needs before the parameters exist) and BREP through BRepTools. Assemblies are walked with their component locations; a body is a solid (or a shell, or loose faces) named after its product (XCAF's generated "=>[0:1:1:2]" names are ignored, repeated names suffixed); faces are numbered globally in TopExp order with their name and surface colour. STEP and IGES geometry is in millimetres (OpenCASCADE's conversion), BREP has no units (asked at import).
  - `tessellate`: BRepMesh_IncrementalMesh per body (the old triangulation is cleaned first: BRepMesh otherwise keeps a finer one), reversed faces flipped, points merged at float32 precision (binary STL) and collapsed triangles dropped, the CAD face of every triangle kept.
  - Import (File > Import Geometry, which replaces Import Surface and accepts *.step *.stp *.iges *.igs *.brep *.brp): the file is copied into <project>/geometry/; a file with several bodies asks the multi-solid question (D-069): one Part with every body, or one Part per body (`CadSource.bodies`). Regions: one per face name (faces sharing a name are one region), unnamed faces take their body's name, the region colour is the group's first face colour; a part with a single group has no explicit regions.
  - Part (schema v8): `cad` = `CadSource(file, linear_deflection` in metres, default 1/1000 of the bounding-box diagonal rounded to 3 significant digits, `angular_deflection` in degrees, default 20, `bodies)`; `source_file` is the tessellation <part>_tess_<key>.stl in model units and `region_ids` the per-triangle CAD face ids <part>_tess_<key>_faces.npy, key = hash of (CAD file content, deflections, bodies). The files are the cache: the same deflections reuse them. Editing a deflection in the Part's properties (CAD file, Linear deflection (m), Angular deflection rows) re-tessellates and updates `cad`, `source_file` and `region_ids` as one undoable step; regions keep their CAD faces.
- Alternatives considered: the OCP build with VTK (pins its own VTK next to PyVista's); gmsh's OCC import (no face names through its Python API without extra work, not an approved dependency); storing the tessellation inside the project file (large).
- Consequences: STEP/IGES parts are always in millimetres; a repaired CAD part (D-072) loses the repair when its deflection is changed (the tessellation comes from the CAD again). OCCT writes its transfer messages to stdout. The Python loop over the triangulation nodes takes a few seconds per million triangles.

## D-072 Geometry repair: pymeshfix with a fidelity check, fTetWild envelope fallback
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). The fTetWild envelope fallback and its parameters: accepted as implemented (owner, QUESTIONS 163). Was Proposed (Task 3.3).
- Context: PLAN 3.3: an automated repair keeping the original, with a before / after report; the survey chose pymeshfix first and pytetwild's envelope as the fallback.
- Decision:
  - Dependencies: `pymeshfix` 0.18.1 (wheel metadata AGPL-3; MeshFix GPL-3; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 74) and `pytetwild` 0.4.2 (MPL-2.0).
  - geometry/repair.py (no Qt): `repair_surface` returns the input unchanged ("none") when it is already snappy-ready (watertight, consistently oriented, no self-intersections, no duplicate faces; `SurfaceReport.snappy_ready` now also requires consistent orientation); otherwise MeshFix's default repair (fill holes, remove degenerate and self-intersecting triangles) keeping every component. Its result is accepted when it is snappy-ready AND faithful: bounding box within 2 % of the original diagonal and at least 90 % of its points within 1 % of the diagonal from the original surface. Otherwise the fallback is the outer boundary of fTetWild's tetrahedralisation (envelope epsilon 1e-3 of the diagonal, edge length factor 0.05), oriented outward; the report then lists any defect that remains and the notes say why pymeshfix was not used.
  - Edit > Repair part: the repaired surface is written to a NEW file <geometry>/<file stem>_repaired.stl (then _repaired_2, ...) in the part's units, the original stays; region ids are carried over to the new triangles (nearest old triangle centre) in a new region id file; the part points to the new files as one undoable step. A report shows before / after: watertight, open edges, non-manifold edges, self-intersections, duplicate faces, flipped normals, triangles; the console gets the summaries and the notes.
  - Corpus results (tests/test_repair.py): holes (11 open edges, 1049 -> 1056 triangles), flipped patch (10 triangles), duplicated faces (5, 11 non-manifold edges; 1061 -> 1042) repaired by pymeshfix; two overlapping spheres (172 self-intersecting triangles) rejected for pymeshfix (bounding box -33.8 %) and wrapped by the envelope (1440 -> 2160 triangles, volume 0.736 m3 for a 0.752 m3 union). Every result is snappy-ready.
- Alternatives considered: trusting pymeshfix's result when the checks pass (it dropped most of a sphere); the envelope always (changes the triangulation of surfaces with a single hole); repairing in place (the original must stay).
- Consequences: repair runs on the GUI thread (item 77); changing a CAD part's deflection re-tessellates from the CAD and drops the repair; the fidelity thresholds are a heuristic (item 76).

## D-073 Geometry work on background threads; per-part forces over all patches
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). The per-part force objects ([NUMERICS-REVIEW]): accepted as implemented (owner, QUESTIONS 164). Was Proposed ([NUMERICS-REVIEW] for the per-part force objects).
- Context: docs/QUESTIONS.md item 77: CAD tessellation and repair froze the window (the fTetWild envelope took 6.5 s on 1,440 triangles; the self-intersection check about 8 s per million triangles). Review of D-071: a part split into several patches had per-patch force objects but none for the part.
- Decision:
  - gui/geometry_worker.py: `GeometryWorker` runs one job at a time on a Python thread and reports only through signals (job id, result or exception); slots run in the GUI thread. Jobs receive plain data or `snapshot(project)` (a Project over a deep copy of the state, no history) and never touch the live Project. Jobs: reading a CAD file and tessellating it at import (two jobs, the parts-or-regions question in between), re-tessellation after a deflection edit (the properties panel emits `retessellate_requested`), Edit > Repair part. While a job runs, Import Geometry, Repair part, Generate Mesh, the CAD deflection rows, the units and the region edits are disabled, and the status bar and the console show "<label>...". The result is applied with one `update_part` (one undoable step) only if the part still equals the snapshot's part; otherwise it is discarded with a warning ("changed during the repair: the result was discarded").
  - `IntersectionChecker` computes full surface reports (with self-intersections) one after another on its own thread; the GUI shows the report without the self-intersection pass meanwhile (`checks.basic_report`; the Checks row says "checking..."), and on completion refreshes the Checks rows and the viewer highlight. A part imported in the session gets the console warning when its check finds intersections. The check is pending until the GUI thread has handled its result.
  - Per-part forces: `mesh_case.part_force_groups` gives every part with several patches its key (the part's safe name; skipped when that name is also a patch name) and patches; the solver case writes `forces_<part>` / `forceCoeffs_<part>` over ALL of the part's patches next to the per-patch objects (`forces_<patch>`, written as before when the mesh has more than one wall patch), and the report lists offer both (`force_report_keys`). Before this change a two-patch part had only per-patch objects.
  - Checked on a real run (tests/test_patches.py, marker slow): the cube (patches front and body) and a sphere Ball, 30,527 cells, 5 simpleFoam iterations: total force (N, x y z) Cube 284.947, -0.0200936, -0.131601 = front 192.1817, -9.19e-6, -6.80e-6 + body 92.76536, -0.0200844, -0.131594; all walls 312.1847, -0.535030, 0.135279 = Cube + Ball 312.18462, -0.5350302, 0.1352788 (agreement within the 7 significant digits of force.dat).
- Alternatives considered: QThread subclasses (the existing workers use Python threads with signals); running the checks inside the import job (the part would appear only after the slow check); a modal progress dialog (blocks the window).
- Consequences: GUI tests wait for the background work (tests/test_import.wait_geometry). The surface caches are plain dicts shared by the GUI and worker threads (single-key reads and writes).

## D-074 Keep pymeshfix (AGPL-3), third-party licence list, fidelity metrics in the repair report
- Status: Accepted (owner decision, 2026-10-18; docs/QUESTIONS.md items 74 and 76).
- Context: pymeshfix 0.18.1 declares AGPL-3; the repair's fidelity check (D-072) is a heuristic.
- Decision: pymeshfix stays: AGPL-3 is combinable with GPL-3 and its network clause does not apply to a desktop application. THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md lists every runtime dependency (direct and transitive) with its version and licence from the installed metadata, plus the external programs (OpenFOAM, ParaView). The fidelity heuristic is accepted as documented in D-072; the repair report adds two rows for the accepted surface: "Bounding box change" (percent of the diagonal, limit 2 %) and "Points on the original surface" (percent within 1 % of the diagonal, limit 90 %), and the console logs them.
- Alternatives considered: replacing pymeshfix before a release (no equivalent hole filler among the approved dependencies).
- Consequences: THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md must be updated whenever a dependency is added or upgraded.

## D-075 No third-party product names in the repository; design mockup kept local
- Status: Accepted (owner decision, 2026-10-18).
- Context: nothing related to the commercial product the workflow was first compared with may be published.
- Decision: the tracked files use neutral wording ("integrated CFD workflow", "commercial reference workflow", "a range-based asymptotic criterion"); the branding rule is "never use third-party product names or logos in the app" (D-040 reworded). The design mockup is no longer tracked (`git rm --cached`; the file stays in the owner's working copy and is listed in .git/info/exclude); the docs refer to it as "the owner's local design mockup". The feature inventory (Task 0.1) stays local only, also excluded.
- Alternatives considered: keeping the names in the internal planning docs only (D-040 before this change).
- Consequences: the earlier commits still hold the old wording and the mockup in the history; this change covers the current tree only.

## D-076 Import without saving first; .vcfd project files; save targets never overwrite non-project files
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (bug fix reported by the owner, 2026-10-18). The temporary workspace of untitled projects is superseded by D-077; the .vcfd files and the save target rules stay.
- Context: on an unsaved project, Import Geometry opened "Save project as" first without saying so; the owner took it for the import picker, picked a STEP file, and the dialog offered to replace the STEP file with the project file.
- Decision:
  - Import never asks to save. An untitled project gets a temporary workspace, <XDG cache>/vortexcfd/workspaces/untitled-<12 hex>/ (~/.cache by default), created on the first import (`Project.ensure_folder`); imported files go to its geometry/. Part paths stay relative ("geometry/..."); `Project.folder` is the project file's folder once saved, the workspace before. The first save moves every file of the workspace's geometry/ to <project folder>/geometry/ (a name already taken there gets _2, _3, ...; the parts' source, region-id and CAD paths are renamed in the current state and the whole undo / redo history), then deletes the workspace; the project file is written before anything moves. Closing, New or Open after the discard confirmation deletes the workspace. Meshing still needs a saved project (the case lives next to the project file).
  - Project files are "<name>.vcfd" (JSON as before). The save dialog filters "VortexCFD project (*.vcfd)" and does not confirm overwrites itself; the chosen name gets .vcfd when it lacks it (a legacy .json suffix is replaced, any other suffix kept and .vcfd appended). Open accepts *.vcfd and *.json; Save on a project opened from a .json file writes <name>.vcfd next to it (the .json is left untouched) and the project continues from the .vcfd.
  - Save target rules (core/workspace.check_save_target, enforced in `Project.save` itself, so no caller can bypass them): never a file with a geometry extension (.stl .obj .ply .step .stp .iges .igs .brep .brp, any case), never one of the project's geometry sources (surface, region ids, CAD file), never a folder, never an existing file that is not a VortexCFD project (a JSON object with schema_version). A refused target shows an error (status bar and a message box) and the dialog's result is not used; a geometry name is refused before any extension is appended. Overwriting an existing VortexCFD project other than the current file asks for confirmation.
- Alternatives considered: telling the user before the save dialog (still a save-first flow); the dialog's own overwrite confirmation (it accepted the STEP file); storing untitled imports in /tmp (may be cleared while the app runs).
- Consequences: an untitled project with imports leaves its workspace behind only if the app is killed (the cache folder can be cleared at any time). Tests point XDG_CACHE_HOME at a temporary folder (tests/conftest.py).

## D-077 Start screen, New simulation dialog, presets, project folders; no untitled projects (schema v9)
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). The New simulation dialog's default core count: accepted as implemented (owner, QUESTIONS 165). Was Proposed (task qol-start-domain, 2026-10-18). Supersedes the temporary workspace of D-076.
- Context: the app opened an untitled project; imports went to a hidden workspace in the cache moved on the first save (D-076). The owner asked for a start screen, a New simulation dialog with analysis presets, and a project folder from the start.
- Decision:
  - Start state: `vortexcfd` without a path shows the main window with no project (no tree content, the muted viewer message "No project open", only the File and Help menus active; Import, Save, Save As and Save as Preset disabled) and opens the modal Start dialog: "New simulation", "Open project..." and the recent projects (last 10, QSettings key recent/projects, most recent first; a missing file is greyed with "(not found)", cannot be opened and can be removed from the list). Closing the dialog leaves the empty window; File > New Simulation, Open, Open Recent (with "Start screen...") reopen the flows. `vortexcfd <path>` opens that project without the dialog. Every project created or opened goes to the front of the recent list. Help > About.
  - New simulation dialog: project name (also the folder and file name: letters, digits, spaces, "_", "-", "."; starts with a letter or digit, no trailing dot, at most 64 characters), parent folder (browse; the last one used is remembered), analysis preset (built-in "External flow", "Empty", then the user presets), "Run in parallel" with the number of cores (default: `recommended_cores` of D-064 applied to the preset's background cell target, the only cell count known before a mesh; checked when that is above 1; maximum the physical cores). Create makes <parent>/<name>/ with <name>.vcfd, geometry/ and case/ and saves at once (`Project.create`); an existing non-empty folder (or a file of that name) is refused with a message, an existing empty folder is used. Solvers.cores is set from the dialog (1 when not parallel).
  - No untitled projects: the app never creates a project without a file. The temporary workspace (`Project.ensure_folder`, `discard_workspace`, workspace moves on the first save) is removed; core/workspace.py is now core/save_rules.py (the extension and save target rules of D-076, unchanged). `Project.new` stays for scripts and tests (an in-memory project); importing into one is refused ("Create or open a project first").
  - Schema v9: `Simulation.analysis_type` ("external" or "empty"; "internal" reserved for internal flow, not selectable) and `Simulation.preset_name` (provenance, informative). Projects before v9 open as "external" (owner decision). v9 also adds `Domain.shape` ("box" by default), `Domain.radius` and `Domain.parts` (the parts the domain is sized around, None = all; renamed and removed parts are followed), so presets can carry the shape; the rounded shapes are D-078.
  - Presets (core/presets.py, Qt-free): a preset holds ONLY the analysis type, the domain shape, sizes and symmetry, the default boundary type per boundary role and the mesh settings; never physics, turbulence, inflow or solver settings (owner decision; unknown fields are refused). The boundary type of each role is today's fixed one (inlet velocity_inlet, outlet pressure_outlet, farfield / sides / top / bottom slip, symmetry symmetry); the model has no per-role choice yet, so the value is validated and kept but changes nothing (docs/QUESTIONS.md). Applying a preset sets the analysis type, preset_name, the domain fields (enabled only for "external"; the parts the domain is sized around are reset to all) and the mesh settings; physics, inflow, solvers and reports keep their defaults. Built-in presets are defined in code and read-only (their names are reserved, any case). User presets: File > Save as Preset... (name; replacing an existing one asks first) writes a JSON file (format_version 1) into <config>/VortexCFD/presets (QStandardPaths GenericConfigLocation: XDG_CONFIG_HOME or ~/.config); files are validated on load, an invalid one is listed as broken and never raises. File > Manage Presets... renames and deletes user presets (and deletes broken files).
- Alternatives considered: keeping the untitled workspace next to the new flow (two ways to start a project); presets carrying physics (refused by the owner); storing presets in QSettings (not shareable as files); AppConfigLocation (depends on the application name being set before the first call).
- Consequences: tests point XDG_CONFIG_HOME at a temporary folder (tests/conftest.py, which no longer redirects XDG_CACHE_HOME). Save As into another folder wrote only the project file and left the geometry behind (docs/QUESTIONS.md item 79): superseded by D-079.

## D-078 The domain as an operation; bullet and C-type domain shapes
- Status: Accepted (owner review, 2026-10-18: farfield slip as for the box sides, the rounded surface resolved at the base size, the background box margin; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 80).
- Context: the far-field domain was always a box generated with the parts (D-019). The owner asked for rounded domains and for the domain to be an explicit operation.
- Decision:
  - Shapes (`Domain.shape`, schema v9 of D-077), in multiples of L (the largest extent of the union of the boxes of the parts the domain is sized around, `Domain.parts`, None = all; c = its centre): "box" as before; "bullet": a hemisphere of radius R = `radius` L centred at c and a cylinder of the same radius behind it, axis along +x through c, the outlet `downstream` L after the parts' rear (defaults 5 and 10); "c_type": in the x-z plane a half-disc of radius R centred at c in front, flat top and bottom at c_z +- R, a flat rear `downstream` L after the parts, flat spanwise sides at c_y +- `lateral` L (defaults 5, 10, 5). With symmetry "y_min" the bullet's axis lies in the plane y = the parts' y_min and only y >= that plane is kept; the C-type spans [y_min, y_min + `lateral` L] with the symmetry plane at y_min. `downstream` is measured from the parts' rear for every shape. A part outside the domain gets a warning.
  - Boundary roles: box as before; bullet: front hemisphere inlet, rear disc outlet, cylinder farfield; C-type: front inlet, rear outlet, top and bottom farfield, spanwise sides sides; symmetry when enabled. farfield gets the conditions of sides / top / bottom (slip, nut calculated; `physics_case.FAR_FIELD_BY_SHAPE`); no boundary condition changed.
  - geometry/domain_surface.py: the closed triangulated domain surface with a cell array `role` per triangle (every shape, the box as six faces), seams shared so it is watertight; 64 segments around the bullet, 16 per quarter circle, 8 on the rear radius (enclosed volume within 0.22 % of the analytic one).
  - Meshing a rounded shape: blockMesh makes a background box = the domain's bounds grown by `BACKGROUND_MARGIN_CELLS` (2) base cells, all faces in the patch "background"; with symmetry the box's y_min face lies exactly on the plane and is the symmetryPlane patch "symmetry" (exactly planar), and the domain surface written for snappyHexMesh (constant/triSurface/domain.stl, one solid per role) leaves the symmetry region out and continues its open edge 2 base cells through the plane. domain.stl is a refinement surface at level (0 0), its regions named after the roles become the patches (type patch), no layers, its feature edges at level 0; locationInMesh is halfway between the inlet on the axis and the parts' front, at the parts' y-z centre, in the background grid. After snappyHexMesh, `createPatch -overwrite` with no new patches removes the patches left without faces (the background patch). The guard (case/vortexcfd_domain.json: the expected domain patches and the background patches, written for every shape; `mesh_run.domain_problems`, `MeshReport.domain_errors`) marks the mesh invalid when a background patch still has faces or an expected domain patch has none; Run Solver is then disabled. The base size uses the actual domain volume: base = (V / background cell target)^(1/3); the cell counts and the 2,000,000 limit are those of the background box.
  - Operations: in the "Empty" analysis nothing is created automatically: the Fluid Domain node reads "Fluid Domain (not generated)" and holds "Generate domain..." (also Edit > Generate Domain... and a button in the node's properties): a dialog with the parts to size it around (the selected part, else all), the shape, its sizes and the symmetry, applied as one undoable step (enabled, shape, sizes, symmetry, parts). Meshing says "Generate the domain first" until then. In the "External flow" analysis the domain is generated on the first import with the preset's shape, as before. The Regions folder gains "Fluid Region (domain minus parts)": the domain, the subtracted parts and the locationInMesh that selects the kept side. Shape and every size are editable in the Fluid Domain properties (only the fields of the shape are shown; "Lateral" reads "Half-width" for the C-type), each edit one undoable step; the mesh inputs hash includes the domain, so any change marks the mesh out of date. The properties show the bounding box, the volume and the parts the domain is sized around.
  - Viewer: the domain surface as a translucent wireframe for every shape; selecting a boundary role highlights its faces.
- Alternatives considered: blockMesh with curved edges or an O-grid for the bullet (hard to keep general with symmetry and parts of any size); snapping the symmetry plane to the domain surface (a snapped symmetryPlane is not exactly planar); keeping the empty background patch with a boundary entry in the 0/ fields (the solver files would need an extra patch); a domain sized around the bounding sphere of the parts (changes L).
- Real coarse meshes (sphere of radius 0.5 m, 24 x 24, base size 0.5 m, surface levels 1-2, no layers, defaults otherwise; tests/test_domain_shapes.py, marker slow): box 1935.308 m3, 17,798 cells; bullet 1086.376 m3, 11,058 cells; C-type 1442.583 m3, 13,870 cells; checkMesh OK, every expected patch with faces and no background patch left in all three; 3.7 to 4.6 s each. With y_min symmetry: bullet 6,229 and C-type 7,635 cells, also OK. simpleFoam ran 3 iterations on the bullet and on the symmetric C-type mesh (exit code 0).
- Consequences: the reserved patch names gain farfield, background and domain (a part with such a name gets a suffix). Older cases without vortexcfd_domain.json pass the guard unchanged.

## D-079 Save As duplicates the project folder with its geometry
- Status: Accepted (owner decision, 2026-10-18; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 79).
- Context: part paths are relative to the project folder; Save As to another folder wrote only the .vcfd file, so the parts of the new file pointed to a geometry/ folder that did not exist (D-077).
- Decision: File > Save As (Duplicate Project) asks for a new project name and parent folder (the New simulation rules: valid name, an existing non-empty folder is refused, an existing empty one is used; the duplicate cannot lie inside the project's own folder) and `Project.duplicate` creates <parent>/<name>/ with <name>.vcfd (the current state, unsaved edits included, renamed <name>), geometry/ and an empty case/, then copies every file the parts reference (surface or CAD tessellation, region ids, CAD source) to the same relative path. Files the history references but the current state does not are not copied; a referenced file that is missing is reported in the console and skipped; a referenced path outside the project folder refuses the duplicate; a failed copy removes what was created. case/ is not copied, so the duplicate opens without mesh and results. The window continues with the duplicate (the recent list gets it); the original project file and folder are not touched. The save target rules of D-076 still apply (`Project.save` checks the new file). An in-memory project without a folder (scripts, tests) keeps the save-file dialog flow.
- Alternatives considered: copying case/ too (meshes and results can be large and are rebuilt from the inputs); copying the whole geometry/ folder (it may hold earlier file versions nothing references).
- Consequences: the undo history does not follow into the duplicate (it starts clean).

## D-080 Save As warns in a dialog about missing geometry files
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (task 5.0, Part A; requested in the review of D-079).
- Context: D-079 reports a referenced geometry file that is missing only in the console and the status bar; the duplicate is created without it, so a part of the new project has no surface and the user may not notice.
- Decision: after a duplicate that skipped missing files, a warning dialog (`Dialogs.show_warning`) titled "Save As" lists every missing file (path relative to the project folder) and says that the parts using them have no surface in the duplicate. The console message per file stays. The duplicate itself is unchanged.
- Alternatives considered: refusing the duplicate when a file is missing (the user may want to duplicate exactly to repair the project); a status bar message only (the D-079 behaviour, easy to miss).
- Consequences: one more modal dialog, only in that case; the tests use the fake dialog's `warnings` list.

## D-081 Meshing benchmark: generated geometries and case definitions
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (task 5.0, Part B).
- Context: Phase 5.0 (docs/PLAN.md) needs a reproducible benchmark that measures the current mesher as the baseline against which surface preparation, an alternative volume mesher and prism-layer work (5.2-5.4) are judged. It must measure what a user gets, so it runs the app's own defaults.
- Decision:
  - Geometries are generated by code (src/vortexcfd/bench/geometries.py; `benchmarks/geometries/generate.py` or `python -m vortexcfd.bench --generate`), written to benchmarks/geometries/ and git-ignored; a missing file is generated when a case needs it. The files are byte-identical from run to run: the STEP header's time stamp is fixed and its product is named after the file. Axes: x along the flow from the nose or leading edge at x = 0, y spanwise, z up.
  - sphere: diameter 1 m centred at the origin, binary STL in metres, 180 x 90 facets (chord error 76 um); the control case.
  - wing: rectangular wing, NACA 0012, chord 1 m along x, span 3 m along y from y = 0, flat closed tips, at zero incidence. The section is the 4-digit thickness formula with its open trailing edge (coefficient -0.1015, 0.252 % of the chord) corrected linearly in x to exactly 0.25 % of the chord, closed by a flat trailing-edge face; one interpolated B-spline through 401 cosine-spaced points, extruded along y. STEP in millimetres.
  - ahmed: Ahmed-type body without stilts: length 1.044 m, width 0.389 m, height 0.288 m, bottom at z = 0, centred on y = 0; the four edges of the front face rounded with radius 0.1 m; a rear slant of 25 degrees and 0.222 m along the slant (it starts at x = 0.8428 m and meets the rear face at z = 0.1942 m); every other edge sharp. In the free stream (no ground: the app has no ground plane). STEP in millimetres.
  - Case definitions (src/vortexcfd/bench/cases.py, a dataclass per case): every case starts from a new Simulation with the built-in "External flow" preset, as the New simulation dialog creates it, and lists only what it changes. No case changes a meshing, domain or flow default: box domain (5 L upstream, 10 L downstream, 5 L lateral and vertical), 100,000 background cells, target surface size 0.01 L, relative layer sizing with 3 layers (expansion 1.2, final layer 0.5), feature angle 30 degrees, 20 m/s, k-omega SST, target y+ 1 (not used by the relative layer sizing). The reference length that normalises the surface fidelity is the sphere's diameter, the wing's chord and the body's length; the app's own L (the largest extent; 3 m for the wing) is reported next to it. The settings of the indicative y+ run (D-082) are listed as deviations: a fixed iteration count, no stopping criteria, the recommended cores.
  - Zones (coverage reported apart): wing "trailing_edge" (x/c >= 0.95, whole span) and "tips" (within 5 % of the chord of either tip plane); ahmed "slant_edges" and "rear_edges" (face centres within 0.02 m of the four edges of the slant and of the rear face).
  - Measured mesh-only on the development PC with the defaults: sphere 193,151 cells, wing 199,947, Ahmed 188,140, each meshed in 26 to 38 s, all under the 500,000-cell limit without a budget change.
- Alternatives considered: committed STL/STEP files (binaries in the repository, and nothing shows how they were made); a half wing on a symmetry plane (the full span keeps two free tips, the zone of interest); the Ahmed body with stilts and a ground (the app has no ground plane or moving wall); a reduced cell budget (not needed: every mesh is under 500,000 cells).
- Consequences: the benchmark measures exactly the New simulation defaults; a change to a default changes the benchmark, which is then visible in a comparison. A STEP written by a newer OCP may differ in bytes; the geometry does not.

## D-082 Meshing benchmark: runner, metrics, result files and regression rules
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (task 5.0, Part C).
- Context: the benchmark of D-081 needs one command that runs the cases through the app's own pipeline, measures the mesh and writes comparable result files, and a comparison that flags regressions. The meshing orchestration the app uses (`prepare_job`, `prepare_solver_case`, the CAD import) lives in the Qt-free modules gui/meshing.py, gui/solving.py and gui/part_ops.py, but importing any gui module imported Qt through gui/__init__.py.
- Decision:
  - Command: `.venv/bin/python -m vortexcfd.bench [--cases ...] [--compare <file>] [--no-solve] [--result <file>] [--out <folder>] [--generate]`. Each case runs in its own Python process (so the peak memory belongs to that case); a crash is recorded as a failed case. Project folders go to run/bench/<case>/ (an earlier one is removed only when it holds <case>.vcfd).
  - Pipeline: Project.create with the "External flow" preset, the geometry copied into <project>/geometry and added as File > Import does (`part_ops.cad_parts` for STEP), `meshing.readiness` / `prepare_job`, `MeshRun` (with the lost-part and domain guards) and its `MeshReport`. The only change to the generated case is output: `writeFlags ( layerFields );` is appended to system/snappyHexMeshDict so snappyHexMesh writes nSurfaceLayers, thickness and thicknessFraction on the wall patches. A second `checkMesh -writeAllSurfaceFields -writeFields (nonOrthoAngle) -constant -noZero` writes the face non-orthogonality field (the mesh is not changed; its time is not counted).
  - Quality, from the app's checkMesh log: max and average non-orthogonality, faces above 70 degrees (checkMesh's 70-90 degree "severely non-orthogonal" count plus its above-90 "errors"), max skewness and checkMesh's "highly skew" faces (above 4), max aspect ratio, failed checks and every "***" line by type. Faces above 65 and above 70 degrees are also counted from the face field (internal faces only).
  - Layers, per wall patch, for all walls and per zone, area-weighted from the layer fields: coverage = share of the area whose faces have the full target layer count; extruded = share with at least one layer; mean layers; mean achieved thickness (m) and its ratio to the wanted thickness (mean thicknessFraction). Patches and zones with coverage below 80 % are listed.
  - Cost: cells; snappyHexMesh's own stage times (castellation "Mesh refined in", snap, layers, whole run), the wall time of every meshing step, of the meshing run and of the case; peak memory = getrusage(RUSAGE_CHILDREN).ru_maxrss of the case's process after meshing and after the solver run, i.e. the largest single child process (snappyHexMesh or one solver rank), not the sum over MPI ranks.
  - Robustness: MeshReport.valid, the lost parts and the domain guard errors; surface fidelity = the distances from the unique points of the wall patches to the original surface (analytic for the sphere; a tessellation of the STEP at 1e-5 of its diagonal and 2 degrees otherwise), max and RMS divided by the case's reference length.
  - y+, labelled "indicative, not converged": `prepare_solver_case` with the app's default physics, 400 iterations, the stopping criteria removed, write interval 400 and the recommended cores (D-064); `SolverRun`; the yPlus field at the last time on the walls: min, area-weighted mean, max and the share of the wall area with y+ in [0.5, 2] times the target y+. 400 iterations keep the full benchmark near 10 minutes on the development PC.
  - Result files: benchmarks/results/<UTC date and time, YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ>_<short git sha, "-dirty" with uncommitted tracked changes>.json and a .md with the summary table and per-case details; git-ignored. The committed baseline is benchmarks/baseline.json (plus BASELINE.md).
  - `--compare <file>` prints per-case deltas and flags as regressions: wall or zone coverage down more than 2 points; max non-orthogonality up more than 2 degrees; more faces above 70 degrees; cells up more than 10 %; surface fidelity RMS worse by more than 10 %; a failed mesh, a lost part or a case missing from the new result. A total time up more than 25 % is only flagged. The exit code is 1 when there is a regression.
  - Tests: fast tests of the metric code on small fixtures (a captured failing checkMesh log, the existing sphere logs, a one-cell case with layer fields, distances to a sphere, the comparison rules); a real run carries the new marker `bench`, which is skipped unless selected (`pytest -m bench`) and excluded from scripts/ship.sh.
  - Layers (superseded in this point by D-083: the modules moved to workflow/, bench/ imports nothing from gui/ and gui/__init__.py imports MainWindow eagerly again): gui/__init__.py imports MainWindow and main on first access, so `import vortexcfd.gui.meshing` loads no Qt. The new package bench/ may import core, foam, geometry and, from gui/, only meshing, solving and part_ops; tests/test_layers.py enforces it, and a test checks that importing the benchmark loads neither PySide6 nor pyvistaqt.
- Alternatives considered: hand-written dictionaries (would not measure what the user gets); moving the Qt-free orchestration modules out of gui/ into a layer of their own (cleaner, but a larger move; docs/QUESTIONS.md); computing face non-orthogonality in Python from the polyMesh files (OpenFOAM's own cell centres are needed to match checkMesh); coverage as the share with any layer (reported as "extruded": it hides partly collapsed stacks, e.g. 99.5 % extruded but 1.5 of 3 layers at the wing's trailing edge in a development run); snappyHexMesh's final layer table only (one row per patch, no zones).
- Consequences: the numbers describe the meshes the app writes with its defaults. Time and memory depend on the machine and on its load; time is only flagged. A result made with a dirty tree says so in its name.

## D-083 Qt-free orchestration in the workflow package
- Status: Accepted (owner decision, task 5.1, docs/QUESTIONS.md item 83).
- Context: the Qt-free orchestration joining core, geometry and foam lived in gui/ (meshing, solving, part_ops, mesh_study, polar_sweep), so the benchmark had to import gui/ modules and gui/__init__.py imported MainWindow lazily (D-082).
- Decision: the five modules move unchanged to the new package src/vortexcfd/workflow/. workflow/ may import core, foam and geometry (so numpy and pyvista), never PySide6, pyvistaqt, vortexcfd.gui or vortexcfd.bench; gui/ and bench/ import workflow/; bench/ imports nothing from gui/. tests/test_layers.py enforces it; the test that importing the benchmark loads neither PySide6 nor pyvistaqt stays. gui/__init__.py imports MainWindow and main eagerly again (the lazy import is no longer needed). gui/sections.py (Qt-free, pyvista cuts for the Sections window) is post-processing, not orchestration, and stays in gui/.
- Alternatives considered: keeping the modules in gui/ with an allow-list (the D-082 state).
- Consequences: pure move; no behaviour change.

## D-084 Seed lines refine in a castellation-only run, never as snap features
- Status: Accepted (owner review of task 5.1, 2026-10-19: the two-pass seeded run is accepted; [NUMERICS-REVIEW] reviewed).
- Context: docs/QUESTIONS.md item 86. The automatic seed lines (D-052) were listed in snappyHexMesh's `features` with `explicitFeatureSnap true`, so snapping could pull wall points onto them. Confirmed on the benchmark wing (default settings, mesh only): with the seed lines the largest surface deviation is 2.664 mm at (0.990, 1.5, 0) m and the next 2.582 mm at (-0.003, 1.5, 0) m, both on the seed line through the wing's centre; with seeding disabled (a benchmark-only override, not a default) the largest is 1.849 mm at the leading-edge tip corner (-0.002, 3.0, -0.001) m, RMS 0.133 -> 0.127 mm, and the wing is kept (200,856 cells). Checked in the v2606 sources: a feature entry has no option to take part in refinement but not in snapping (refinementFeatures.C reads only `file`, `level` / `levels`; snappySnapDriverFeature.C snaps to every entry); `searchableExtrudedCircle` "can not be used with snappyHexMesh since only implements nearest searching" (its header); and a refinementRegion (shell) is refined only after surface refinement (snappyRefineDriver::doRefine: featureEdgeRefine, surfaceOnlyRefine, ..., shellRefine, then baffleAndSplitMesh with no further surface refinement), so it cannot do the seed lines' job of refining before the surface is searched (D-052 measured 40 faces with a distance region).
- Decision: when at least one part is seeded, the meshing case gets system/snappyHexMeshDict.castellate (the same settings, `snap false; addLayers false;`, the seed lines in `features` at their parts' maximum levels) and system/snappyHexMeshDict (the same settings, `castellatedMesh false;`, the seed lines left out of `features`). MeshRun runs `snappyHexMesh -overwrite -dict system/snappyHexMeshDict.castellate` (step and log "snappyHexMesh.castellate") before the usual `snappyHexMesh -overwrite`; the step is skipped when its dictionary is absent, and a regenerated case without seeded parts removes the file. snappyHexMesh recomputes the surface intersections of the castellated mesh before snapping (snappyHexMesh.C). Without seeded parts nothing changes: one run, the same dictionary as before. The benchmark reads the castellation time from the first log and adds both runs' totals.
- Measured (development runs, default settings): benchmark wing 200,117 cells, the same 10,998 wall points as the single seeded run, max deviation 1.849 mm (the leading-edge tip corner), RMS 0.127 mm, checkMesh OK, both snappyHexMesh runs 44 s together; the D-052 reproduction (ball and pod) 135,265 cells, Ball 1,952 and Pod 1,304 faces, Mesh OK: identical to the D-052 record, the pod is kept.
- Alternatives considered: a searchableExtrudedCircle distance refinementRegion around the seed lines (not usable in snappyHexMesh, and shells refine too late); feature `levels` distance refinement (it is still a snap feature); shorter seed lines that stay inside the part (a heuristic: wall points near the lines' ends would still be attracted on concave parts); removing the seed lines altogether (D-052's lost parts return).
- Consequences: seeded cases pay a second snappyHexMesh start-up (reading the surfaces and recomputing intersections, a few seconds on the benchmark wing); the layer table still comes from log.snappyHexMesh.

## D-085 Surface cell size from l_ref; curvature refinement (schema v10)
- Status: Accepted (owner review of task 5.1, 2026-10-19: the surface size from l_ref and the curvature refinement are accepted; [NUMERICS-REVIEW] reviewed).
- Context: docs/QUESTIONS.md item 85. The target surface size was 0.01 L with L the parts' largest extent, so the 3 m benchmark wing got 24.1 mm surface cells (2.4 % of its 1 m chord). Owner decisions: the surface cell size is derived from the reference length l_ref (Reference Values, D-047), not from the largest dimension; add curvature-based surface refinement, conservative (at most 2 levels above a part's surface level).
- Decision:
  - `Mesh.surface_size_reference` ("l_ref", the default, or "largest_extent"; the value is short so the Mesh properties combo shows it whole): the target surface size (still 0.01, the same factor) multiplies l_ref, or L as before. l_ref is the Reference Values one: the user's value, or its automatic value (the x extent of the union of the parts' boxes, as the force coefficients use) when it was never set. The domain stays sized from L. The Mesh properties' Estimate shows "Surface size from" with the length and its value ("l_ref = 1 m", "l_ref = 1 m (auto: bounding-box length, x extent)", "L = 3 m (largest extent of the parts)") and, when l_ref is automatic, the warning "l_ref is not set: the surface cell size uses its automatic value (auto: bounding-box length, x extent). Set the reference length under Reports & Monitors > Reference Values." The target surface size field's suffix is "x length"; "Surface size length" picks the basis. When l_ref sizes the surface, its override is a meshing input (mesh hash).
  - `Mesh.curvature_levels` (0 to 2, default 2; 0 = off): each part's refinementSurfaces entry gets `curvatureLevel (4 0 <part max level + curvature_levels> -1)`, the v2606 syntax (refinementSurfaces.C, setCurvatureMinLevelFields; tutorial mesh/snappyHexMesh/block_with_curvature): 4 cells per radius of curvature (about 14 degrees of arc per cell), start level unused by v2606, the maximum level, no ignore level. snappyHexMesh gives a surface triangle level round(log2(4 base / radius)) when that is above its surface level and at most the maximum (a radius that would need more gets none); points on edges sharper than resolveFeatureAngle are skipped. Part regions inherit the surface's entry.
  - The estimate (foam/mesh_estimate.py) reports the length used, the finest curvature level (level_max + curvature_levels) and its cell size; the Mesh properties show "Curvature up to: level n (size)". The total cell count is still not estimated (only background cells, D-042), so the 2,000,000 background-cell block and snappyHexMesh's maxGlobalCells apply unchanged.
  - Schema v10: a v9 (or older) file keeps the behaviour it had: the migration sets `surface_size_reference "largest_extent"` and `curvature_levels 0` where the file does not set them (V9_MESH_BEHAVIOUR, hand-written fixture tests/data/project_v9.json). The inputs hash leaves these fields out while they hold the v9 values, so the meshes of existing projects stay up to date. Presets carry Mesh: the built-in ones take the new defaults; a user preset file without the new fields gets the new defaults too (a preset starts a new simulation). (Superseded for user presets by D-090: a preset written before schema v10 keeps the v9 mesh behaviour.)
  - Benchmark (D-081): each case sets l_ref explicitly in its case definition (`reports`): the sphere's diameter 1 m, the wing's chord 1 m, the Ahmed body's length 1.044 m (listed as deviations).
- Alternatives considered: keeping L and lowering the factor (one setting cannot fit both bluff and slender bodies); a curvature level relative to the background level (the conservative cap relative to each part's own level is what the owner asked); more cells per radius (8: about 7 degrees per cell; more cells on every rounded leading edge).
- Consequences: slender parts (wings, blades) get much finer surfaces by default and more cells: the benchmark wing goes from 199,947 to 673,628 cells (mesh only, development run). Mesh study levels keep curvature refinement relative to each level's surface level while its cells per radius stay fixed, so the levels are not exactly geometrically similar where curvature refinement acts (docs/QUESTIONS.md).

## D-086 Absolute layer sizing from a default target y+ of 50
- Status: Accepted (owner review of task 5.1, 2026-10-19: accepted pending the fidelity comparison check of D-091, which found the 5.1 comparison like-for-like; [NUMERICS-REVIEW] reviewed).
- Context: docs/QUESTIONS.md item 88. New simulations used relative layer sizing, so the default target y+ of 1 sized nothing: after the benchmark's 400 iterations the area-weighted mean y+ was 169 (sphere), 231 (wing) and 108 (Ahmed body), with 0.0 % of the wall within [0.5, 2] times the target. Owner decision: absolute layer sizing by default from a target y+ of 50 (wall-function range); relative sizing stays selectable, and in relative mode the target y+ field is disabled with a tooltip saying that the layer thickness then follows the surface cell size and y+ is not targeted.
- Decision: `Mesh.layer_sizing` defaults to "absolute_yplus" and `Mesh.target_yplus` to 50. The first-layer thickness and the layer count come from the existing estimator (D-024, D-026: flat-plate Cf with Re on L, count clamped to [1, 30] with the existing warning when 30 layers do not reach half the surface cell); the relaxed quality controls of absolute sizing apply (D-044, D-045). With relative sizing the Mesh properties disable the Target y+ field, with the tooltip "Relative layer sizing: the layer thickness follows the surface cell size, so y+ is not targeted. Choose absolute_yplus to size the layers for this y+." Existing projects keep their stored layer settings: the app always saved them, and the v9 -> v10 migration (D-085) also fills `layer_sizing "relative"` and `target_yplus 1` where an older file does not set them; tests/data/project_v9.json is the fixture. The built-in presets take the new default.
- Alternatives considered: keeping relative sizing and greying out the target y+ only (the other option in item 88); y+ 30 (the lower edge of the log layer: more layers on coarse surfaces; 50 is the owner's value).
- Consequences: new simulations get a y+-sized stack (on the default 1 m sphere at 20 m/s: first layer 0.90 mm, 10 layers); results depend on the flow speed and viscosity through the layer sizes (they are mesh inputs, D-046). The Layers count field is not used with absolute sizing (unchanged behaviour). The tests that build meshing dictionaries directly, without the y+ estimate, use relative sizing; the real-run fixtures whose recorded data describe the v9 defaults (the shared sphere mesh, the projection run, the speedup study) are pinned to the v9 mesh (V9_MESH_BEHAVIOUR).

## D-087 Benchmark headline layer metric: strict coverage
- Status: Accepted (owner decision, task 5.1, docs/QUESTIONS.md item 84).
- Context: D-082 reported coverage (the wall area with the full target layer count) and extruded (the area with at least one layer); item 84 asked which is the headline for 5.2 to 5.4.
- Decision: the headline is the strict coverage. The summary table labels it "Strict coverage %" and shows the target layer count next to it; "Extruded %" stays a separate column; the regression rules use the strict coverage (wall and zones, as in D-082). `--compare` also flags a changed target layer count (strict coverage of a 10-layer stack is not the same measure as of a 3-layer one); the coverage drop is still reported as a regression.
- Alternatives considered: extruded as the headline (hides partly collapsed stacks).
- Consequences: comparisons across a change of the layer count (D-086: absolute y+ sizing) carry a flag.

## D-088 y+ estimator: Reynolds number on l_ref
- Status: Accepted (2026-10-21, review follow-up R-39: docs/QUESTIONS.md item 95 records the owner's decision "Re on l_ref, the surface size length (D-088)" and item 103 the owner's acceptance of the reading for older projects, both 2026-10-19). Was Proposed (owner request in the review of task 5.1; [NUMERICS-REVIEW]).
- Context: the first-layer thickness for the target y+ (D-024) took its Reynolds number from L, the parts' largest extent: the benchmark's 3 m span wing was sized for Re on 3 m while its surface cells follow its 1 m chord (D-085), and its mean y+ came out at 33.9 for a target of 50.
- Decision: the Reynolds length of the y+ estimator is the surface size length of D-085 (`workflow/meshing.surface_length`): l_ref (the user's value, or its automatic value with the same Mesh-properties warning, which now names the y+ first layer too), or L for a project made before schema v10 (`surface_size_reference "largest_extent"`), so an existing project's layers do not change. Used by the Mesh properties, Generate Mesh and the mesh study's shared layer stack. The estimator itself (Cf = 0.026 / Re^(1/7)) is unchanged.
- Alternatives considered: always l_ref (would change the layers of existing absolute-sizing projects behind an up-to-date mesh hash).
- Consequences: for a slender part sized on its chord, Re is lower, Cf and u* higher, so the first layer is thinner: at 20 m/s the y+ 50 first layer on a 1 m l_ref is 7.5 % thinner than on 3 m. The l_ref override is already a meshing input when it sizes the surface (D-085), so the hash covers it.

## D-089 Layers field disabled with absolute sizing
- Status: Accepted (owner request in the review of task 5.1, docs/QUESTIONS.md item 100).
- Context: with absolute (y+) layer sizing the layer count comes from the estimator (D-026) and Mesh.layers is ignored, but the Layers field stayed editable.
- Decision: with absolute sizing the Mesh properties' Layers field is disabled, shows the computed count (the Estimate's "Layers (computed)") and has the tooltip "Absolute (y+) layer sizing: the layer count follows from the target y+ and the surface cell size; it is shown here and cannot be set. Choose relative sizing to set it." The stored Mesh.layers is not changed (it is the count again when relative sizing is chosen). The field's range is 0 to 30 (the estimator's maximum), so a computed count is never clipped.
- Alternatives considered: hiding the field (the computed count would only be in the Estimate rows).
- Consequences: mirrors the disabled Target y+ field of relative sizing (D-086).

## D-090 Preset files versioned with the project schema
- Status: Accepted (owner request in the review of task 5.1, docs/QUESTIONS.md item 101).
- Context: D-085 and D-086 changed the Mesh defaults; a user preset saved before them has no surface size length or curvature field (and a hand-written one may lack the layer settings), so it silently took the new defaults while old projects kept their behaviour.
- Decision: a preset carries `schema_version`, the project schema its settings follow (preset file format 2; format 1 files have none and were all written before schema v10, so they read as v9). `core/presets.migrate_preset` runs before validation, like the project migration: below v10 the mesh gets the v9 behaviour where the file does not set the keys (V9_MESH_BEHAVIOUR: relative layers with target y+ 1, surface size from the largest extent, no curvature refinement). A preset with a schema newer than the app is listed as broken. A migrated preset is written in the current format when it is next saved or renamed. Hand-written fixture tests/data/preset_v1.json.
- Alternatives considered: migrating presets to the new defaults (the D-085 reading this replaces).
- Consequences: an old preset starts new simulations exactly as it did before; an older app lists a format 2 preset as broken ("format version 2 is newer than this app").

## D-091 Benchmark: fidelity in metres, layer diagnostics
- Status: Accepted (owner requests in the review of task 5.1, docs/QUESTIONS.md item 99).
- Context: the owner asked whether the 5.1 fidelity deltas mixed normalisations (L in the baseline, l_ref in 5.1), and for layer diagnostics that tell a near-miss stack from a collapse and show whether the quality checks remove layers. Checked: both results divide by the case's own reference length (sphere 1 m, wing 1 m, Ahmed 1.044 m; `BenchCase.reference_length`, D-081), never by the app's L, so the 5.1 deltas were like-for-like.
- Decision:
  - Fidelity is stored in metres too (`max_m`, `rms_m`) with the reference used (`reference_m`); the table shows mm, the relative values and the reference; `--compare` prints both, the relative ones with their reference, and applies the RMS rule (+10 %) to the metres (an older result's metres are its relative value times its reference length).
  - Layers, per wall, patch and zone: mean layers as a percentage of the target, the share of the area with at least 80 % of the target count, the share with no layer, the area per layer count.
  - From log.snappyHexMesh (the snap and layer run): faces whose extrusion the layer-mesh check removed ("Removed extrusion at N faces", summed over the iterations) and the checks that failed there; the checks that failed while the mesh was moved for the layers, where snappyHexMesh scales the displacement down locally (to zero at worst) instead of removing faces, and the number of scaling steps; feature-edge points given no layer (layer featureAngle); isolated points stopped; medial-axis thickness reductions.
- Consequences: diagnostics only; no meshing change.

## D-092 Benchmark: half wing on a symmetry plane; y+ run cores
- Status: Accepted (owner decisions in the review of task 5.1).
- Context: the full benchmark after 5.1 took 1,477 s, 813 s of it the 965k-cell full-span wing's y+ run on 4 cores; the y+ runs used the app's recommended core count, capped at 4 by D-064's efficiency limit.
- Decision:
  - The wing case is a half wing: geometry `half_wing` (benchmarks/geometries/half_wing.step, the same NACA 0012 section, chord 1 m, span 1.5 m from the root at y = 0 to the tip at y = 1.5 m), with `domain.symmetry "y_min"` (a deviation from the defaults): the symmetry plane is the parts' y_min, so the root face lies on it, as a user's half model does. Zones: the trailing-edge band (x/c >= 0.95, whole span), the tip (within 5 % of the chord of y = 1.5 m) and the root (within 5 % of the chord of the symmetry plane). The full-span `wing` geometry stays available.
  - The y+ runs use cells / 50,000 ranks (the app's CELLS_PER_CORE) with at most 10 and never more than the physical cores minus 2 (`bench/runner.yplus_cores`), not D-064's cap of 4; the cores are recorded per case (`yplus.cores`, the "y+ cores" column, the deviations).
- Alternatives considered: fewer y+ iterations (the owner chose the geometry and the cores).
- Consequences: the wing is no longer comparable with benchmarks/baseline.json (accepted by the owner); benchmarks/baseline_candidate.json is the new reference candidate. The y+ run times are not comparable either (more ranks, beyond the efficient range measured in D-064).

## D-094 Benchmark: optional local cases, fidelity against a reference surface
- Status: Accepted (owner review, 2026-10-19: the reference reading, the surface-size deviation, the local work folder, the mean chord and the tessellation note are accepted; docs/QUESTIONS.md items 108, 112, 113, 115, 116).
- Context: Phase 5.2 (surface preparation) is measured on the owner's multi-component aircraft (two exports of the same model from a conceptual design tool: separate untrimmed surfaces, and a trimmed export as the reference). That geometry must never enter the repository, and the committed baseline must stay the three public cases.
