Checked while setting the cases up: the trimmed export is not one outer skin but four closed solids (fuselage 8 faces, wing 8, horizontal tail 8, vertical tail 6) that still overlap (fuselage and wing share 0.257 m3, fuselage and horizontal tail 0.020 m3, fuselage and vertical tail 0.009 m3, the two tails 0.003 m3); docs/QUESTIONS.md.
- Decision:
  - Local cases are read from benchmarks/local_cases.json (git-ignored); benchmarks/local_cases.example.json (committed) documents the format: a `reference` block (the reference CAD file, its components by body index, l_ref, the zones), `defaults` (import mode, inflow, mesh, domain; merged key by key into every case) and `cases` (name, geometry file, description, `solve`, `surface_prep` null / "intersection_features" / "cad_union"). `python -m vortexcfd.bench --local [--cases ...]` runs them; a missing config or geometry file skips the cases with a message (`bench/local.load_local_cases`); a case name may not reuse a public one. Their results go to benchmarks/results/local/ (git-ignored, numbers only), never into baseline.json; their projects and the reference cache go to <XDG cache>/vortexcfd/bench_local/ (~/.cache by default), outside the repository, because they hold copies of the owner's geometry and its tessellations.
  - Import mode: the local cases answer the import's multi-body question with "one Part per body" (`import_mode "parts"`), so every component is a Part with its own patch, lost-part guard and per-part forces; the public cases keep "regions".
  - Reference analysis (`bench/reference.py`, cached by file content and settings): a fine tessellation per body (1e-5 of the diagonal, 2 degrees, as the public cases' CAD reference); the outer skin is the part of each body outside every other body, by VTK's enclosed-points test on the other bodies' closed tessellations, after splitting (edge midpoints) every triangle near an intersection curve until its edges are below 2e-4 of the diagonal (the export's tessellation has slivers up to 5 m long that cross the junctions); the intersection curves of every pair of bodies are OpenCASCADE sections, discretised at 1e-5 of the diagonal, without the segments inside a third body. Not a boolean union, so it shares no code with the CAD union strategy it measures.
  - l_ref (set as the project's reference length) is the geometric mean chord of the component named in `l_ref.mean_chord`: the mean of the x extents of 101 plane sections y = const of its fine tessellation, evenly spaced over its span (planform area / span). Aircraft: 1.500 m (wing span 10 m).
  - Zones (half-width `band` x l_ref, 0.05 x 1.5 = 0.075 m for the aircraft): `junctions` (per zone, the wing or a tail: wall points or faces within the band of that component's intersection curves with the others, outer-skin parts only); `trailing_edge` (within the band of the line through the most downstream point of 102 sections y = const of the wing, where that point is outside the other components); `tips` (within the band of the wing's span ends). The tails' mutual curves lie inside the fuselage, so the tail zones are their junctions with the fuselage.
  - Metrics (`runner.reference_measurements`, `metrics.reference_fidelity`, `metrics.force_partition`), overall and per zone: mesh -> reference (distance from every mesh wall point to the outer skin) and reference -> mesh (from every skin point to the triangulated mesh walls, which catches missing geometry), each as max and RMS in mm and the share of points further than 0.5 x the local surface cell size (at a wall point: the mean sqrt(face area) of its wall faces; at a skin point: that of its nearest wall face); the wall surface left inside a component (wall points, and wall faces by their centre, further than 0.5 x the local size from the skin and inside a reference body: count and area); the faces of every component patch; and the per-part force check without a solve: the app's own force function objects (`physics_case.control_dict`) per part must partition the all-walls object's patches, every patch with faces, so the per-part forces sum to the total.
  - Aircraft cases (deviations from the New simulation defaults): one Part per body, inflow 30 m/s, l_ref = the mean chord, and `mesh.target_surface_size 0.1` (x l_ref = 0.15 m, surface level 5 = 84 mm cells), the finest setting under the 500,000-cell limit: 0.05 (levels 5-6) had 413,976 cells after castellation alone, before layers; 0.1 gave 257,752 cells on the trimmed export (mesh only, 146 s). The trimmed export also gets the indicative y+ run.
- Alternatives considered: committing the local results (the task forbids it: numbers only, in a git-ignored folder); the outer skin from a boolean fuse of the reference solids (the strategy under test does exactly that); the reference skin from triangle centres alone (wrong where long slivers cross a junction: 2.8 m2 too much on the synthetic two-box test); projects in run/bench/ (git-ignored but inside the repository folder); the mean aerodynamic chord instead of the geometric mean chord (1.556 m for this taper; the task asked for the mean chord).
- Consequences: a local run analyses the reference once (about 160 s for the aircraft, then cached). Local numbers depend on the owner's files, which only the owner has.

## D-093 Surface preparation: diagnostics, intersection features, CAD union (schema v11)
- Status: Accepted (owner review, 2026-10-19: CAD union is the default, docs/QUESTIONS.md item 109; the parameters of item 110 are accepted; the fallback to intersection features was confirmed after its junction level was corrected, D-097). [NUMERICS-REVIEW] reviewed.
- Context: PLAN 5.2. A multi-component model whose components interpenetrate and are not sewn (the owner's untrimmed aircraft export: six compounds of loose faces, every overlapping pair crossing; benchmarks/RESULT_5.2_before.md) reached snappyHexMesh as separate surfaces with nothing at the junctions: no feature line where a wing enters the fuselage, and nothing in the app looked across parts (the self-intersection check is per part, the repair is per part and lost the wings on a one-part import).
- Decision:
  - Model (schema v11): `Simulation.surface_prep` (None = not run: the parts mesh as imported, as before) holds the operation's result: the strategy, the hash of the parts' geometry when it ran (`inputs_hash`), per Part a `PreparedPart` (the strategy applied to it, its prepared surface and region ids or None, a note when it fell back), the intersection-curve file (`features_file`), the report rows and the run time. v10 files migrate with nothing to fill (fixture tests/data/project_v10.json); presets hold no geometry, so a v10 preset reads as v11 unchanged.
  - Operation: Edit > Prepare Surfaces... (also the "Surface Preparation" node between the geometry and the continua, with "Prepare surfaces..." and "Remove" in its properties) asks the strategy and runs `workflow/surface_prep.prepare` on the geometry worker (D-073) over a snapshot; the result is applied with `Project.set_surface_prep` as one undoable step if the parts did not change meanwhile. Files are written under <project>/geometry/ with content-hashed names, so undo only points back; Save As copies them. The node shows the strategy, the state (up to date, or out of date), the per-part result and the report.
  - Meshing uses the result only while the parts' geometry still hashes to `inputs_hash` (`surface_prep.state`); an out-of-date result blocks meshing with "The surface preparation is out of date: the parts changed after it ran. Run Prepare Surfaces again, or remove it." It is a meshing input (the mesh hash includes the strategy and the files); without a preparation the hash is unchanged.
  - Diagnostics (geometry/surface_prep.py, on the parts' tessellations in metres, against the surface cell size of the mesh settings): per part whether it is closed (open and non-manifold edges) and, for CAD parts, whether its faces sew into a closed solid; the pairs of parts that cross each other with their intersection-curve length (and how much of it is on the outside of the other parts); gaps smaller than a cell (open boundary loops: the median distance from one loop to the nearest open edge of another, or a single loop's smaller in-plane extent; and pairs of parts closer than a cell without crossing, "touching" at 0); the share of a part's area thinner than a cell (rays along each sampled triangle's normal in both directions, VTK cell locator: thickness, or facing surfaces closer than a cell).
  - Intersection curves: computed on the tessellations snappyHexMesh sees, as the segments where triangles of different parts cross (geometry/intersections.intersection_segments: the self-intersection check's grid, then the overlap of each pair's plane cuts), instead of OpenFOAM's surfaceBooleanFeatures: no extra utility run per pair, the pairs come out for the diagnostics too, and it is tested without OpenFOAM. Segments whose middle lies inside a third part (generalised winding number above 0.5, robust to the small holes of these tessellations) are left out: they are not on the outer skin and, as snap features, would pull that part's surface inwards; pairs whose curves are shorter than 1e-6 of the diagonal only touch.
  - Strategy "intersection_features": the parts stay as they are; the curves are written as a featureEdgeMesh and added to snappyHexMesh's `features` (castellation and snapping, with `explicitFeatureSnap` as for every feature) at the junction level: the highest maximum surface level of the parts (their overrides, else the mesh's), so a junction is resolved like the finer of the two surfaces [NUMERICS-REVIEW]. The parts' insides are discarded by snappyHexMesh as before (locationInMesh is outside all parts).
  - Strategy "cad_union" (geometry/cad_union.py): each CAD part's faces are sewn (BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing) with a tolerance of 1e-5 of its model's bounding-box diagonal (0.145 mm on the 14.5 m aircraft; OpenCASCADE's own default is a fixed 1e-6 model units) [NUMERICS-REVIEW], every closed shell is made a solid and oriented (ShapeFix_Solid), all solids are fused in one BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse (one part at a time when that fails, so the failing part is named), and the union is tessellated with the parts' smallest deflections. Every face of the union keeps its source part and source CAD face through the sewing and fuse history (Modified / IsDeleted), so each part gets its share of the outer skin as its prepared surface (metres) with the source CAD face ids as region ids: patches, regions and per-part forces stay one per part; together the shares are closed (they share their boundary nodes). The shares' open boundaries are the junctions, which surfaceFeatureExtract already extracts as features (openEdges). A part that is not CAD, cannot be sewn closed (reason with the free-edge count), cannot be fused, or keeps no outside surface falls back to intersection features: its own surface, with the curves between it and every other surface (the union's shares included) as features.
  - Only the parts that cross another part take part in the union; a part that crosses nothing (every part of a one-part project) keeps its own surface, so the operation never changes single-component geometry (the meshing case of a prepared one-part project is byte-identical to the unprepared one, tests/test_surface_prep.py).
  - Default strategy (the one the dialog proposes): **CAD union**, proposed from the 5.2 comparison on the owner's aircraft (benchmarks/RESULT_5.2.md) [NUMERICS-REVIEW]: against the trimmed reference it is the only strategy that moves the junctions closer (reference -> mesh RMS at the wing, horizontal and vertical tail junctions 1.33 / 2.03 / 1.68 mm against 4.87 / 5.10 / 2.60 mm as imported and 5.17 / 3.06 / 2.19 mm with intersection features), it leaves 0.030 m2 of wall inside a component against 0.079-0.080 m2, and its largest mesh -> reference deviation is 18.4 mm against 28.7 and 40.5 mm. Intersection features measured within noise of no preparation. Its mesh (like the trimmed reference's) fails checkMesh at the benchmark's 24-layer stack with negative-volume cells on the wing surface away from the junctions; without the layer phase it fails only the skewness check the as-imported mesh also fails (a 5.4 matter). The owner accepts or changes it.
- Alternatives considered: OpenFOAM's surfaceBooleanFeatures per pair (a utility run per pair at mesh time; the same curves); replacing the Parts' files with the prepared ones (like Repair part: a deflection edit would silently drop the union, and the operation could not be removed); a shell wrapper (implicit-surface or envelope wrapping: not built; Part D says whether it is needed); one combined STL for the union (the per-part patches and forces would need regions).
- Consequences: the junctions get feature lines; a preparation must be re-run after any change to the parts' geometry (meshing says so). The report rows are plain text stored in the project.

## D-095 The case/ and geometry/ folders found in the home folder
- Status: Accepted (owner review, 2026-10-19).
- Context: the owner found ~/case and ~/geometry dated 2026-10-18 20:53-20:54 and asked whether the app made them.
- Finding (read-only; nothing moved or deleted): the app made them. ~/case carries the app's marker file .vortexcfd-case, its dictionaries, logs and vortexcfd_mesh.json (138,801 cells, Mesh OK, patch "pues_veamos"; blockMesh log "Case : /home/vic/case", 20:54:23); ~/geometry holds the imported STEP copy and its tessellation in the app's naming (pues_veamos_tess_<key>.stl and _faces.npy, 20:53:47). The source STEP, ~/pues veamos.STEP, is the owner's own export (written 20:51:27, unchanged). No project file referencing them exists any more under the home folder. The app at that time (commit 76b560a and earlier) asked "Save project as" before an import into an unsaved project and wrote geometry/ and case/ next to the chosen project file: that is the incident D-076 records; the project file was saved in the home folder (the STEP's folder), so both folders landed there. The paths were never built from the working directory. The flow was replaced the same evening by D-076 and then D-077 (every project is created as <parent>/<name>/ with its own geometry/ and case/; an in-memory project cannot import). In the current code the only paths built from the working directory are fTetWild's own debug file during the repair envelope (deleted afterwards, D-072) and the benchmark's repository-root fallback outside git.
- Decision: no code change. A regression test (tests/test_save_safety.py::test_a_project_writes_only_inside_its_own_folder) imports a CAD file into a new project, runs the surface preparation and writes the meshing case with the working directory set elsewhere, and checks that nothing appears outside <parent>/<name>/.
- Consequences: the two folders in the home folder are leftovers of that session; the owner may delete them.

## D-096 A mesh that fails checkMesh blocks the solver
- Status: Accepted with a change (owner review, 2026-10-19): only hard checkMesh failures block, D-100. [NUMERICS-REVIEW] reviewed.
- Context: the 5.2 aircraft meshes failed checkMesh with negative-volume cells (benchmarks/RESULT_5.2.md). Checked: Run Solver was NOT blocked by failed checkMesh checks; only lost parts (D-052) and the domain guard (D-078) blocked it, the mesh badge said "Mesh invalid" and the Mesh report showed the count. The benchmark already solved only valid meshes and the mesh study already required a valid mesh per level; the polar sweep did not.
- Decision: `MeshReport` keeps checkMesh's "***" lines (`failed_check_lines`, also in case/vortexcfd_mesh.json; older records have none) and `failed_checks_message()`. Run Solver (`workflow/solving.readiness`) and the polar sweep (`workflow/polar_sweep.sweep_readiness`) are disabled when the current mesh failed checkMesh, after the lost-part and out-of-date reasons, with the reason "The mesh failed checkMesh (checkMesh failed N checks: <the *** lines>): the solver must not run on invalid cells: change the mesh settings (refinement, layers) and generate the mesh again." as the button's tooltip; the Mesh report's "Failed checks" row lists the lines and says that Run Solver is blocked. There is no override (none existed). Every checkMesh failure blocks, not only negative volumes: checkMesh's failed checks are its own error thresholds (non-orthogonality above 90 degrees, skewness above 4, ...).
- Alternatives considered: blocking only negative volumes and face-pyramid errors (a skewness failure, as the 5.2 as-imported mesh shows, is milder; the owner may relax this); an explicit "run anyway" action (not added: the task allows only an existing override).
- Consequences: a project whose mesh fails only the skewness check (4.23 on the as-imported aircraft) can no longer be solved until the mesh passes; tests/test_solver_gui.py::test_a_mesh_that_failed_checkmesh_blocks_run_solver.

## D-098 Component names from the STEP entities
- Status: Accepted (owner review, 2026-10-19; the file-name fallback and the identifier rule, docs/QUESTIONS.md item 119).
- Context: the untrimmed aircraft export names its components only on its GEOMETRIC_SET entities ("<id>, Fuselage, 0", "<id>, Ala, 0", ...); it has one product. OpenCASCADE's XDE document gives every component the placeholder name "COMPOUND" (the shape type), so the parts were COMPOUND, COMPOUND_2, ...
- Decision: `geometry/cad.read_cad` names a body after, in order: its XDE label or product name, the name of the STEP entity it was transferred from (`XSControl_TransferReader.EntityFromShapeResult`, mode 1), the inherited assembly name, the file name. A name is cleaned (`cad.clean_name`): whitespace collapsed; empty names, XDE's generated "=>[0:1:1:2]" and OpenCASCADE's type names (COMPOUND, SOLID, SHELL, FACE, ...) count as no name; a comma-separated name drops a leading identifier token (8 or more upper-case letters and digits) and joins the rest with "_" ("<id>, Fuselage, 0" -> "Fuselage_0"); characters other than letters, digits, space, "_", "." and "-" become "_". Repeated names get _2, _3 (as before); files use `part_ops.file_stem`. Face names go through the same cleaning. A file without names falls back to the file name instead of "COMPOUND".
- Measured: the untrimmed export reads as Fuselage_0, Ala_0, Ala_1, HTail_0, HTail_1, VTail_0; the trimmed export has no component names (empty solid names, one product) and keeps Trimmed_Plane_1 to _4. Tests: a hand-written synthetic STEP surface model with named GEOMETRIC_SETs (tests/cad_corpus.surface_model_step).
- Alternatives considered: parsing the STEP text for GEOMETRIC_SET names (bypasses OpenCASCADE's mapping from entity to shape); keeping the identifier token (names like "QWERTYUIOP_Fuselage_0").
- Consequences: re-importing such a file gives new part names (existing projects keep theirs); the identifier rule is a heuristic (docs/QUESTIONS.md).

## D-097 Junction curves refine to the finest surface level
- Status: Accepted (owner review, 2026-10-19; [NUMERICS-REVIEW] reviewed). Corrects the junction level of D-093; extended to the CAD union by D-101.
- Context: intersection features measured within noise of no preparation on the aircraft, with a worse largest deviation (40.5 against 28.7 mm). Investigated on the meshing case (benchmarks/RESULT_5.2.md, "Intersection features, investigated"): snappyHexMesh reads the junction file (log "Read edgeMesh surface_prep_junctions.eMesh, points 262, edges 262") and snaps to it (1,534 boundary points attracted to feature edges against 1,131 without it); the curves match the reference's intersection curves (7.71 / 3.69 / 1.92 m for the wing, horizontal and vertical tail junctions), so the rule that drops segments inside a third part removes only the two tails' mutual curves, which lie entirely inside the fuselage; but the junction level was the parts' maximum surface level, 5, while curvature refinement (D-085) already puts the cells along the junctions at levels 6-7 (within 0.05 m of the curves: 6 cells at level 5, 124 at 6, 1,200 at 7), so the feature entry refined nothing. A plain .eMesh reaches snappyHexMesh as "external" edges without surface normals (refinementFeatures.C; the same holds for the parts' own surfaceFeatureExtract features, listed as <part>.eMesh); replacing the curves with OpenFOAM's surfaceBooleanFeatures output (concave "internal" edges with normals, trimmed by the other parts) changed no junction number by more than 0.02 mm, so the normals are not the cause.
- Decision: the junction level is the finest level the parts' surfaces can reach: their highest maximum surface level plus `Mesh.curvature_levels` (5 + 2 = 7 on the aircraft; `workflow/meshing.junction_level`). The curves stay a plain featureEdgeMesh.
- Measured (aircraft_untrimmed_intersection_features re-run, mesh only): 281,235 cells; reference -> mesh RMS at the wing / horizontal / vertical tail junction 2.13 / 2.07 / 2.32 mm (as imported 4.87 / 5.10 / 2.60, CAD union 1.33 / 2.03 / 1.68), max 11.2 / 7.7 / 8.5 mm (as imported 27.7 / 22.0 / 10.7); mesh -> reference max 21.0 mm overall (28.7 as imported, 40.5 before the fix). Trade-off: at the wing junction almost no layer is extruded (0.4 % of the area against 59.4 % as imported) and 52.4 % overall (58.4 %).
- Fallback: intersection features now improve every junction against no preparation, so it stays the per-part fallback of the CAD union (the "as is with a warning" alternative of the review is not needed).
- Alternatives considered: surfaceBooleanFeatures per pair (same numbers, an OpenFOAM run per pair); keeping level 5 and adding a distance refinement around the curves (the cells are already at level 7 near most of the curve).
- Consequences: about 11,000 more cells on the aircraft; junction layers collapse more (input for 5.4). The CAD union's junctions are its parts' open edges, still refined at the parts' surface level (5): not changed here (docs/QUESTIONS.md).

## D-099 The import checks report parts that cross each other
- Status: Accepted (owner review, 2026-10-19).
- Context: the per-part self-intersection check reported 0 on every component of the aircraft while every overlapping pair crossed (benchmarks/RESULT_5.2_before.md).
- Decision: `workflow/surface_prep.crossing_parts` finds, for every part, the other parts its surface crosses (the surface-preparation intersection curves, the parts inside a third part included), cached by the parts' geometry hash and skipped above 2,000,000 triangles in total (as the self-intersection check). The GUI runs it on a background thread (`gui/geometry_worker.OverlapChecker`). A part's Checks group gains the row "Other parts crossed": "checking...", "none", "<parts>: the parts overlap; snappyHexMesh needs their junctions prepared. Run Surface Preparation (Edit > Prepare Surfaces)." (a warning), or "<parts> (resolved by the surface preparation, <strategy>)" while a preparation is current; the "Self-intersections" row of such a part reads "0 within the part; it crosses other parts (below)", as a warning. After an import into a project with several parts the console warns "Parts cross each other: A / B; ...: run Surface Preparation (Edit > Prepare Surfaces)." unless a current preparation covers them.
- Alternatives considered: folding the crossings into the self-intersection count (it would mix a defect of a part with a property of the assembly).
- Consequences: one more background check after imports and part edits (0.1 s on the aircraft); tests/test_surface_prep.py.

## D-100 checkMesh failures: hard ones block the solver, soft ones warn
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). The classification of each checkMesh line as hard or soft ([NUMERICS-REVIEW]): accepted as implemented (owner, QUESTIONS 166; the policy, item 120). Was Proposed (owner review of 5.2, docs/QUESTIONS.md item 120). [NUMERICS-REVIEW] Changes D-096: only hard failures block.
- Context: D-096 blocked Run Solver and the polar sweep for every failed checkMesh check, so a mesh failing only the skewness check (4.23 on the as-imported aircraft) could not be solved. The owner asked for two classes.
- Decision: `foam/mesh_report.py` classifies every line checkMesh prints with "***" (a failed check) by the start of its text (`HARD_CHECKS`, `SOFT_CHECKS`, `check_class`); the mapping comes from the v2606 sources (src/OpenFOAM/meshes/primitiveMesh/primitiveMeshCheck/primitiveMeshCheck.C, src/OpenFOAM/meshes/polyMesh/polyMeshCheck/polyMeshCheck.C, applications/utilities/mesh/manipulation/checkMesh/checkTopology.C and checkGeometry.C):
  - HARD (block Run Solver and the polar sweep; the badge reads "Mesh invalid: checkMesh failed"):
    - "Zero or negative cell volume detected" (checkCellVolumes);
    - "Zero or negative face area detected" (checkFaceAreas);
    - "Error in face pyramids: N faces are incorrectly oriented" (checkFacePyramids);
    - "Number of non-orthogonality errors" (checkFaceOrthogonality: faces above 90 degrees);
    - "Open cells found, max cell openness ..." (checkClosedCells) and "Open cells found, number of cells" (checkCellsZipUp);
    - "Cells with invalid face labels found" (checkClosedCells) and "Illegal cells (less than 4 faces or out of range faces)" (checkTopology, printed without "***");
    - "Faces not in upper triangular order" (checkUpperTriangular);
    - "Boundary openness ... possible hole in boundary description" (checkClosedBoundary);
    - "Total number of faces on empty patches ..." (checkTopology, 2D set-up);
    - "Cannot find opposite wedge", "Wedges do not have the same axis", "Wedge patch ... not planar" (checkGeometry, axisymmetric set-up);
    - any other "***" line, and any failure checkMesh counts ("Failed N mesh checks") beyond its "***" lines (checks that report without one, such as illegal cells, invalid face vertices, face-face connectivity, zones out of sync between processors), shown as "N failed checks without a description"; a mesh record written before D-096 keeps no lines, so its failures count as hard.
    - Lost parts and the domain guard keep blocking (D-052, D-078).
  - SOFT (the solver can run; the badge reads "Mesh: warnings", the Run Solver tooltip adds "The mesh has warnings (...); the solver can run.", the Mesh report lists each line as "warning:" and says "Warnings only: the solver can run"):
    - "Max skewness = ..., N highly skew faces detected" (checkFaceSkewness, above 4);
    - "High aspect ratio cells found" (checkClosedCells, above 1000);
    - "Concave cells (using face planes) found" (checkConcaveCells);
    - "Faces with small interpolation weight" (checkFaceWeight);
    - "Faces with small volume ratio" (checkVolRatio);
    - "Cells with small determinant" (checkCellDeterminant);
    - "Number of edges not aligned with or perpendicular to non-empty directions" (checkEdgeAlignment, 2D meshes);
    - "Unused points found in the mesh" (checkPoints);
    - and the single-"*" lines checkMesh prints without failing the mesh: "*Number of severely non-orthogonal faces" (70 to 90 degrees), "*There are N faces with concave angles", "*There are N warped faces", "*Number of regions". They are kept in `MeshReport.warning_lines` (also in the mesh record).
  - `MeshReport.valid` is now: no hard failure, no lost part, no domain error (soft warnings allowed). The mesh study and the benchmark use it, so a level or case with soft warnings only counts as valid (the benchmark then runs its y+ run).
- Measured on the 5.2 aircraft logs: as imported (skewness 4.23 only): valid, not blocked; trimmed export and CAD union: hard (negative volumes, non-orthogonality errors, face pyramids) plus soft (aspect ratio, skewness, severe non-orthogonality).
- Tests: tests/test_solver_gui.py (the captured hard log tests/data/bench/checkMesh_failed.log, a captured soft-only log tests/data/bench/checkMesh_soft.log, the as-imported aircraft's lines, every class mapping, a failure without a "***" line).
- Alternatives considered: blocking on skewness above a higher limit (a number the source does not use); treating "*" lines as nothing (severe non-orthogonality would go unseen when checkMesh says "Mesh OK").
- Consequences: a mesh with only quality warnings can be solved, with the warning visible in three places; a status message after meshing is an error for hard failures and a warning for soft ones.

## D-101 The CAD union's junction edges refine at the junction level
- Status: Rejected (owner review, 2026-10-19): the small junction gain (wing junction reference -> mesh RMS 1.33 -> 1.29 mm) does not justify losing the junction prism layers (wing junction extruded area 57.3 -> 3.5 %). The code was reverted (commit "Geometry: revert the CAD union junction level (D-101 rejected)"): the CAD union's junctions are again only its parts' open edges in their own feature files, at the parts' surface level, and no junction-level feature file is written for it (tests/test_surface_prep.py). The intersection-features junction level (D-097) stays. The measurements below remain as the evidence (benchmarks/RESULT_5.2.md). [NUMERICS-REVIEW] reviewed.
- Context: D-097 refines the intersection curves to the junction level (the maximum surface level plus the curvature levels), but the CAD union's junctions were only its parts' open edges inside the parts' own feature files, refined at the parts' surface level (5 on the aircraft).
- Decision: the CAD union strategy also writes its junctions to the preparation's feature file (`geometry/surface_prep.open_edge_segments`: the open edges of every part's share of the union, merged where two shares meet; plus the fallback parts' intersection curves, as before), which meshing adds at the junction level (D-097). The report gains "Union junction edges" (length, edge count).
- Measured (aircraft_untrimmed_cad_union re-run, mesh only, git 1b96112; benchmarks/RESULT_5.2.md): junction edges 13.32 m in 130 edges at level 7; reference -> mesh RMS at the wing / horizontal / vertical tail junction 1.29 / 1.69 / 1.41 mm (before 1.33 / 2.03 / 1.68), max 12.2 / 7.4 / 6.4 mm (12.4 / 11.1 / 8.5); checkMesh now fails only the skewness check (4.22, soft) where it failed five checks before with negative volumes on the wing surface; but the wall left inside a component rises from 0.030 to 0.064 m2, the extruded area falls from 50.8 to 44.6 % (wing junction 57.3 -> 3.5 %), and the cells rise from 281,113 to 290,885.
- Consequences: the CAD union and its fallback refine all junctions the same way; the layer collapse at refined junctions is a 5.4 input (docs/QUESTIONS.md item 122).

## D-102 cfMesh evaluation case and the benchmark's mesher switch
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (task 5.3, Part B; evaluation only: the app keeps snappyHexMesh, no default changes). [NUMERICS-REVIEW].
- Context: PLAN 5.3 compares cfMesh with the snappyHexMesh pipeline on the benchmark, prism layers first. cfMesh is in the v2606 installation (benchmarks/RESULT_5.3.md, Part A); its sources were read from the community integration repository (cfMesh v1.1).
- Decision:
  - `foam/cfmesh_case.py` (no Qt, no pyvista) writes system/meshDict and the steps from the same `MeshCaseInput` snappyHexMesh gets; `workflow/meshing.mesh_inputs` (split out of `prepare_job`, unchanged behaviour) builds it. `workflow/cfmesh.prepare_cfmesh_job` writes the case into <project>/case: the parts' STLs (surfaceFeatureExtract's input, as for snappyHexMesh), the combined surface constant/triSurface/vortexcfd_cfmesh.stl and the domain guard (expected domain patches, no background box).
  - Combined surface: the domain surface (geometry/domain_surface: box, bullet, C-type) plus every part as meshing uses it (the surface preparation's shares when one is current, so the CAD union output), one STL solid per patch named as the snappyHexMesh patches (part regions included). With a symmetry plane, the parts' triangles on the plane are dropped and the plane is re-triangulated (VTK constrained Delaunay in x-z, interior points every background cell) with holes along the parts' open edges on it, so the surface stays closed (checked by an area test). Points shared with the domain and the parts are bit-identical copies, merged exactly.
  - Steps: surfaceFeatureExtract (the parts' <part>.eMesh), `surfaceFeatureEdges -angle <feature angle>` (combined .stl -> .fms with feature edges), cartesianMesh, improveSymmetryPlanes (with symmetry), checkMesh; MeshRun's lost-part and domain guards apply unchanged. cartesianMesh is used (the octree cartesian mesher closest to snappyHexMesh's castellated hex mesh; tetMesh and pMesh give another cell type and nothing in Part A argued for them).
  - Settings mapping:

    | snappyHexMesh (app default) | cfMesh | Note |
    |---|---|---|
    | blockMesh background cells (base size, D-042) | `maxCellSize` = base size | cfMesh's root cube is maxCellSize x 2^k, so its levels are snappyHexMesh's levels (checked in the logs) |
    | refinementSurfaces `level (min max)` per part | `localRefinement` per part patch, `cellSize` = size of the part's min level | snappyHexMesh gives the min level to every cell the surface cuts and the max level only where the surface turns more than resolveFeatureAngle within a cell |
    | `features` <part>.eMesh at the part's max level (+ explicitFeatureSnap) | `edgeMeshRefinement` with the same <part>.eMesh, `cellSize` = size of the part's max level; cfMesh captures the feature edges of the .fms (surfaceFeatureEdges at the same feature angle) | |
    | intersection-features junction curves at the junction level (D-097) | `edgeMeshRefinement` entry with the junction file at its level | |
    | `curvatureLevel` (4 cells per radius, up to max + 2) | none [NUMERICS-REVIEW] | `minCellSize` (cfMesh's automatic curvature and proximity refinement down to max + 2) was tried on the Ahmed body: its curvature criterion marked about 100,000 boxes, 5,862,900 cells against snappyHexMesh's 225,842, 146 s and 4.6 GB; left out |
    | domain boundary at level 0 | no `boundaryCellSize` | boundaryCellSize refines every boundary, the domain's too; the parts get theirs per patch |
    | seed lines (D-052, D-084) | none | the octree is refined from the surface triangles themselves; no part can fall between lattice lines |
    | `locationInMesh` | none | cfMesh keeps the region the closed combined surface encloses |
    | layers: `nSurfaceLayers` = y+ count, `firstLayerThickness` = y+ first layer, `expansionRatio` (D-086, D-088) | `patchBoundaryLayers` per part patch: `nLayers` = the same count, `thicknessRatio` = the same ratio, `maxFirstLayerThickness` = the y+ first layer; relative sizing: no cap; fewer than 2 layers: no boundaryLayers | [NUMERICS-REVIEW] cfMesh splits its one boundary cell into the layers (refineBoundaryLayers.C): the stack is about one boundary cell thick, the first layer is min(cap, geometric split of that cell), so it is thinner than the y+ estimate unless the boundary cell is large |
    | layer quality controls, featureAngle 60, relaxed tet quality (D-044, D-045) | `optimiseLayer 1` with cfMesh's default `optimisationParameters` (nSmoothNormals 3, maxNumIterations 5, featureSizeFactor 0.4, reCalculateNormals 1, relThicknessTol 0.1), `allowDiscontinuity 0` | cfMesh has no layer-removal quality check: it extrudes everywhere and untangles afterwards |
    | meshQualityControls (meshQualityDict values) | cfMesh's built-in optimiser defaults (no meshQualitySettings) | |
    | patch types wall / patch / symmetryPlane | `renameBoundary newPatchNames` with the same types | |
    | snappyHexMesh runs on one core | cartesianMesh pinned with `taskset` to one CPU of each of 10 physical cores (`bench/runner.CFMESH_CORES`) | its OpenMP threads follow the affinity, not OMP_NUM_THREADS |

  - Symmetry plane: OpenFOAM's symmetryPlane needs an exactly planar patch; cartesianMesh's is not (half wing: one point 3.5 mm off the plane at the trailing edge's root in a development run, 3.0 mm in the comparison run, the rest within 0.07 mm) and improveSymmetryPlanes skips it ("large deviation"), so checkMesh cannot even read the mesh. The benchmark therefore stops before checkMesh, moves the symmetry patch points onto the plane (`workflow/cfmesh.flatten_symmetry`, displacement recorded as `symmetry_flattened`) and lets checkMesh judge the cells. [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
  - Benchmark switch: `python -m vortexcfd.bench --mesher cfmesh` (default snappy; local cases too); the project folder and the result's case name get the suffix `_cfmesh`; the result records `mesher` and `cfmesh_cores`. Layers are also counted from the mesh for both meshers (`bench/layer_walk`, result key `layers_walk`): from every wall face the column of cells is walked while each cell is a prism on the current face (a top face with no shared point, at least as many points, normal within about 45 degrees of the wall's, the most parallel one when a split side face also qualifies) with height at most 0.6 x sqrt(face area) and at most 3 x the layer below. Thickness percentages there are of the planned y+ stack (first x (r^n - 1) / (r - 1)). Checked on the snappyHexMesh Ahmed mesh against its layer fields: strict coverage 31.0 % (fields) vs 31.1 % (walk); mean layers 6.86 vs 6.40; zero-layer area 0.08 vs 9.3 %: snappyHexMesh counts collapsed stacks whose last cells are as thick as core cells (aspect about 1.1) as layers, the walk does not. [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Alternatives considered: boundaryCellSize from the parts' level (refines the domain boundary; the reading taken is localRefinement per part patch, docs/QUESTIONS.md); the layer count re-solved so cfMesh's geometric split gives the y+ first layer (the task asked for the same count and ratio); the `symmetry` patch type instead of flattening (the solver case writes symmetryPlane); tetMesh or pMesh.
- Consequences: no app behaviour changes; `meshing.prepare_job` and `write_part_surfaces` are refactored without a change in output (`part_patch_labels`, `mesh_inputs`).

## D-103 User refinement volumes and the wake helper (schema v12)
- Status: Partly superseded by D-120 (the wake helper's box is no longer pitched: the free stream is always +x; volumes get a body or wind frame). Accepted with changes (owner review, 2026-10-20; [NUMERICS-REVIEW] reviewed): the box takes pitch and yaw instead of a z-only rotation; the wake default is a free-stream aligned box; a volume outside the domain is ignored with a warning; the estimate includes the level buffer; only enabled volumes enter the mesh hash (QUESTIONS 132-136 closed). The entry below describes the changed behaviour. [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the mesh could only be refined at the parts' surfaces (levels, curvature, features); wakes and other flow regions away from the walls kept the background cells.
- Decision:
  - Model (schema v12): `Simulation.refinement_volumes`, an ordered list of `RefinementVolume` (id, name, shape, `target_size` in metres, `enabled`). Shapes (discriminated by `kind`): box (`centre`, `size`, `pitch_deg` about y, nose-up positive like the angle of attack, -90 to 90, and `yaw_deg` about z, both default 0; owner review: replaces the z-only rotation, changed in place because v12 is unpublished), cylinder (`point1`, `point2`, `radius`), sphere (`centre`, `radius`), cone (`point1`, `point2`, `radius1`, `radius2`; one may be 0). Names: a letter then letters, digits and "_" (at most 64), unique ignoring case; ids ("v1", "v2", ...) are stable across renames. v11 files migrate with no volumes (fixture tests/data/project_v11.json). Volumes are geometry-specific: presets hold only the mesh and domain settings, so they never carry volumes; applying a preset keeps the project's volumes.
  - Edits (`Project.add_refinement_volume`, `duplicate_`, `remove_`, `update_refinement_volume`; a shape edit is merged into the current shape) are one undo step each. The mesh inputs hash includes only the enabled volumes (owner review, QUESTIONS 136): editing a disabled volume leaves the mesh current; enabling or disabling one, or editing an enabled one, puts it out of date; a project without enabled volumes keeps the hash it had without volumes.
  - Sizing: the level is the coarsest whose cell size (base size / 2^level) is at or below the target, i.e. ceil(log2(base / target)), 0 for a target not finer than the background cell (`foam/mesh_estimate.volume_level`); the achieved size and the level are shown next to the requested size. A level above the finest part surface level (the parts' highest maximum surface level, overrides included) is allowed with the warning "The target is finer than the finest part surface level (level n, size m): this volume may dominate the cell count."
  - snappyHexMeshDict [NUMERICS-REVIEW]: per enabled volume that is not wholly outside the domain, a geometry entry `volume_<name>` (the prefix keeps it apart from the part and domain surfaces) and a `refinementRegions` entry `{ mode inside; levels ((1e15 <level>)); }`. Shapes, confirmed in the v2606 sources (src/meshTools/searchableSurfaces/<type>/<type>.H and .C) and the motorBike tutorial: `searchableBox` (min, max); `searchableRotatedBox` for a pitched or yawed box (span; origin = the corner at the local (0 0 0), because the local box spans 0..span, searchableRotatedBox.C; e1 = (cos p cos y, cos p sin y, sin p), e3 = (-sin p cos y, -sin p sin y, cos p), so e2 = e3 x e1 = (-sin y, cos y, 0); `BoxShape.axes`); plain `searchableBox` when both angles are 0; `searchableCylinder` (point1, point2, radius); `searchableSphere` (centre, radius); `searchableCone` (point1, radius1, innerRadius1 0, point2, radius2, innerRadius2 0). The inside mode and the `levels` syntax: src/mesh/snappyHexMesh/shellSurfaces/shellSurfaces.C (mode names, `levels` read at line 661), tutorial incompressible/simpleFoam/motorBike. Shells refine after the surface refinement (D-084), which is what a volume needs. The castellation-only dictionary of seeded parts (D-084) gets the same entries.
  - Estimate [NUMERICS-REVIEW] (owner review: a staircase of levels, QUESTIONS 135): for a volume at level n, the level-n region is the volume grown by nCellsBetweenLevels x h_n (h_k = base / 2^k; nCellsBetweenLevels = `CELLS_BETWEEN_LEVELS` = 3, the value the dictionary writes); each coarser level k from n - 1 down to 1 adds a shell, the previous region grown by nCellsBetweenLevels x h_k (`foam/mesh_estimate.volume_staircase`, `geometry/refinement_volumes.grow`: a box's edges + 2 x the distance, radii + it, cylinder and cone ends moved out by it). Extra cells (`workflow/refinement.staircase_cells`) = the sum over the level-n region and the shells of V (8^k - 1) / base^3 (the cells at level k minus the background cells in the same volume), each region clipped to the domain (its exact volume times its share inside: 0 when its bounding box misses the domain's, 1 when it lies in a box domain's bounds, otherwise sampled on 25 x 25 x 25 points over its bounding box) and a shell's volume = the clipped region minus the clipped region inside it. Hand check (tests/test_refinement_volumes.py): a 1 m box at level 2 on a 1 m base: a 2.5 m level-2 region (15.625 x 63 = 984.375) and a 5.5 m level-1 region (shell 150.75 m3 x 7 = 1,055.25): 2,040 cells. Overlapping volumes are counted twice (the tooltip says so). The cells count against the existing limit (`MeshEstimate.budget_cells` = background + volumes; 2,000,000), which blocks Generate Mesh with a message naming the volumes.
  - Domain check (owner review, QUESTIONS 134): a volume wholly outside the domain is labelled "Ignored: outside the domain" in the warning style (never an error; also the tree item's tooltip and a warning-coloured icon), left out of the dictionary, and does not block meshing. A volume partly outside gets a warning with the share outside; only the part inside refines.
  - Wake helper [NUMERICS-REVIEW] (owner review: a box, no longer a cone): Edit > Add Wake Volume (also the tree context menu and the Wake button of the Refinement volumes node) adds a box behind the selected part, or all parts when no part is selected, aligned with the free stream: pitch = the angle of attack (its e1 is (cos a, 0, sin a)), yaw 0; its upstream face is centred where the line through the centre of the parts' box along the flow leaves the box; length 2 x L (L: the box's largest extent), width (y) 1.1 x the parts' y extent, height (z) 2 x the parts' z extent; target size 2 x the part surface cell size (the finest maximum surface level of those parts). Example: a 10 x 10 x 2.4 m part from x = 0 at 0 degrees gets a 20 x 11 x 4.8 m box centred at x = 20 m (searchableBox from (10, -5.5, -2.4) to (30, 5.5, 2.4)). Every value stays editable; the cone remains a shape of its own.
  - GUI: a "Refinement volumes (n)" node under Mesh with one child per volume (disabled ones muted); add by shape, wake, duplicate, delete, enable/disable and rename from the node's buttons, the volume's buttons and the tree's context menu; the properties panel edits every field numerically with units (m, degrees); the achieved size and level, the extra cells and the domain status are read-only rows; the Mesh node's estimate gains "Refinement volumes: +N cells (n volumes; total ...)". The viewer draws every enabled volume as a translucent surface with its edges (geometry scene), the selected one stronger, and redraws an edited shape from the uncommitted field values while it is being changed. No drag handles yet (docs/QUESTIONS.md).
- Alternatives considered: refinement by distance from the parts (not a volume); counting overlaps once (needs a union of the shapes); refusing a volume partly outside the domain; blocking meshing on a volume wholly outside, or showing it as an error (the owner chose a warning); hashing disabled volumes too (they cannot change the mesh).
- Consequences: projects with volumes can need far more cells; the estimate shows it before meshing. Schema v12: an older app cannot open a project saved by this one.

## D-104 NVIDIA adapter selected under WSL at start-up
- Status: Accepted (owner, 2026-10-20: confirmed on a real start-up, the console reported the renderer "D3D12 (NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3080 Ti)"; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 137).
- Context: under WSL2 the viewer renders through Mesa's d3d12 driver on a Windows GPU adapter, which may be the integrated GPU instead of the discrete NVIDIA one.
- Decision: `core/gpu_select.select_gpu` sets GALLIUM_DRIVER=d3d12 and MESA_D3D12_DEFAULT_ADAPTER_NAME=NVIDIA, each only when the user has not set it, only under WSL (WSL_DISTRO_NAME or WSL_INTEROP set, or "microsoft" in /proc/version), and not in an offscreen run (QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen: no display, so tests and the display guard are unaffected). It runs at the top of `vortexcfd/gui/__init__.py`, before its first import of a Qt or VTK module: the installed `vortexcfd` console script imports `vortexcfd.gui.main_window`, which runs the package's `__init__` first, so no entry point or reinstall change is needed. Once the viewer exists, the main window logs "OpenGL renderer: <the renderer string of vtkRenderWindow.ReportCapabilities()>" and the variables it set, to the console.
- Alternatives considered: a separate launcher module as the console script (needs the package reinstalled to regenerate the script); setting the variables in `vortexcfd/__init__.py` (every import of the package, the benchmark and the tests included).
- Consequences: on a WSL PC without an NVIDIA adapter Mesa falls back to its default adapter; a user who sets either variable keeps it.

## D-105 Benchmark layer headline counted from the mesh; hard and soft checks per case
- Status: Accepted (owner review of 5.4, 2026-10-20; task 5.4, Part A; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 129).
- Context: since 5.3 every result has both snappyHexMesh's own layer fields and the layers counted from the mesh (bench/layer_walk, D-102). The fields count a collapsed stack, squashed to core-cell thickness, as layers; the walk does not (Ahmed zero-layer area 0.1 % by the fields, 9.3 % by the walk). Task 5.4 asks for the mesh-based count as the headline.
- Decision: the summary tables, the worst zone and `--compare` (bench/report.py) take the layer columns from `layers_walk` when the result has it, with a "Basis" column ("mesh" or "fields") and snappyHexMesh's own strict coverage as a separate column; a comparison uses the walk only when both results have it (like for like; older results such as baseline.json have no walk and are compared on the fields). Each case also records its checkMesh failures by the D-100 classes (`checks`: hard and soft counts and lines), shown as the "Hard" and "Soft" columns. The 5.4 starting point is benchmarks/baseline_5.4_start.json (public cases); the local aircraft's result stays under the git-ignored benchmarks/results/5.4/ (D-094: local results are never part of a committed result file) and its numbers are in benchmarks/RESULT_5.4.md.
- Alternatives considered: keeping the fields as the headline (hides collapsed stacks); committing the aircraft result in the same JSON (it holds names and figures derived from the owner's geometry).
- Consequences: the headline coverage drops slightly where stacks are squashed (half wing 78.4 -> 76.8 %, Ahmed 31.0 -> 31.1 %, sphere 100.0 -> 99.5 %); thickness percentages are of the planned y+ stack (bench/runner.planned_stack) instead of snappyHexMesh's own target.

## D-106 Robust prism-layer defaults: layer cap 12, expansion up to 1.3, featureAngle 130, nOuterIter 2, tet check on (schema v13)
- Status: Accepted (owner review of 5.4, 2026-10-20); updated in task 5.4b, Part B (nOuterIter 3 -> 2, the tet check on during layering; docs/QUESTIONS.md items 140, 143), the update accepted in the owner review of 5.4b. [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: benchmarks/RESULT_5.4.md. With the 5.1 layer controls the Ahmed body had 31 % strict coverage (rear and slant edges 0 / 15 %), the half wing 77 % (tip and trailing edge 0 %), and the aircraft 0 % with hard checkMesh failures from a 24-layer, 251 mm stack. The diagnosis found feature-angle termination (layer featureAngle 60 unextrudes every edge sharper than 60 degrees, and the thickness smoothing tapers the stacks around it) as the dominant mechanism on the public cases, and the stack depth plus a failed shrink as the aircraft's.
- Decision: a new Mesh field `layer_controls` ("robust", the default; "legacy") and, with absolute (y+) layer sizing and "robust":
  - Layer cap (D2): `foam/yplus.plan_layers(..., max_layers, max_expansion)`: the count rule (D-026) gives the planned count and the planned total at the user's expansion ratio; above LAYER_CAP = 12 layers the stack keeps the y+ first layer, gets 12 layers and the expansion ratio is raised (bisection) until it reaches the planned total, at most MAX_EXPANSION_RATIO = 1.3; if 1.3 cannot reach it the stack is thinner (`LayerPlan.thickness_warning`). The ratio written (`MeshCaseInput.layer_expansion`) is the plan's; the mesh study's shared stack uses the same plan. The y+ first layer is never changed. Cap 12 from the study: the aircraft is valid at 12 layers with the plain cap and with both combined candidates, invalid at 15 with both.
  - addLayersControls (`mesh_case.ROBUST_LAYER_CONTROLS`): featureAngle 130 (was 60) and nOuterIter 3 (v2606 default 1). Everything else is unchanged, including the `relaxed` set (minTetQuality -1e30 from iteration 0, D-045) and the main meshQualityControls (the install's meshQualityDict).
  - Relative sizing and "legacy" projects write the 5.1 controls and no cap.
  - Mesh properties: "Layers (computed)" shows "12 (cap; planned N)"; a new row "Planned / achievable" shows both stack thicknesses, the share reached and the ratio written ("ratio 1.3 (set 1.2)"); when the stack is thinner than planned the row is in the warning colour and a warning line says so; the same text joins the Generate Mesh tooltip warning (`workflow/meshing.layer_warning`).
  - Schema v13: the v12 -> v13 migration sets `layer_controls "legacy"` (V13_MESH_FIELDS), so projects and presets saved before keep their layers; the legacy value is left out of the mesh hash, so their meshes stay up to date. Fixture tests/data/project_v12.json. The slow tests written for the 5.1 controls are pinned to "legacy" (V9_MESH_BEHAVIOUR includes it).
- Not adopted (measured): a layer-only second snappyHexMesh pass (identical mesh), the displacementMotionSolver shrinker (did not run: see RESULT_5.4.md), featureAngle 180 (equal on the Ahmed, better tip, worse trailing edge, no termination left for cusps), the tet check on during layering (valid aircraft at 24 layers, but Ahmed 31 -> 23.5 %), relaxed maxNonOrtho 70 / 75, medial-axis, smoothing, nLayerIter, nGrow, nBufferCellsNoExtrude and nRelaxIter changes (no gain or worse), nOuterIter 5 (aircraft too slow).
- Alternatives considered: the D2 cap at 15 (aircraft hard failures with the combined candidates); changing the dictionaries for every project (would change the meshes of saved projects behind an up-to-date hash).
- Update (task 5.4b, Part B; benchmarks/RESULT_5.4b.md): with the first layer at the cell centre (D-107) the 5.4 robust controls leave the aircraft with hard checkMesh failures, as do the tet check alone and nOuterIter 2 alone. Measured with the four combinations, only nOuterIter 2 with the tet check on is valid on every case (half wing 94.4 %, Ahmed body 96.5 %, sphere 99.6 % strict coverage). The robust controls now write `nOuterIter 2` and `meshQualityControls/relaxed { minTetQuality 1e-15; }` (`mesh_case.ROBUST_RELAXED_QUALITY`: the tet check on in the layer phase, where D-045 disables it for the legacy controls). The mesh hash of a robust project carries `ROBUST_CONTROLS_REVISION` ("5.4b"), so a mesh made with the 5.4 controls is out of date; legacy projects keep their hash and dictionaries. Cost: the wing trailing edge 68 -> 38 %, wing tip 91 -> 50 %, Ahmed slant edges 100 -> 56 % against the 5.4 controls with the new first layer.
- Consequences: Part E of RESULT_5.4.md. Meshing takes longer on large stacks (three layer passes): within the noise on the public cases in Part E (about +30 % on the half wing under the study's load), 168 -> 793 s on the aircraft. The cell count grows only by prism cells (the core mesh is identical): +8 % half wing, +24 % Ahmed, +58 % aircraft (docs/QUESTIONS.md: the 20 % limit read as excluding the planned stack's own cells).

## D-107 Target y+ at the first cell centre (schema v14)
- Status: Accepted (owner review of 5.4b; task 5.4b, Part A; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 142). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: with full stacks the public cases reported a mean y+ of 25.9-27.1 for a target of 50 (benchmarks/RESULT_5.4.md, Part E). The estimator used the target wall distance y = y+ nu / u* as the first-layer height. In OpenFOAM v2606 y+ is evaluated at the first cell centre: the yPlus function object (src/functionObjects/field/yPlus/yPlus.C) takes `nutWallFunctionFvPatchScalarField::yPlus()` on wall-function patches and `nearWallDist::y()` otherwise; nutkWallFunction's yPlus() and its nut use `turbModel.y()`, which is a `nearWallDist`; and `nearWallDist::calculate()` (src/finiteVolume/fvMesh/wallDist/nearWallDist/nearWallDist.C) sets it to the distance from `mesh.C()` of the wall-adjacent cell, its centre, to the nearest wall face. For a prism layer of height h that is h / 2.
- Decision: a new Mesh field `yplus_at` ("cell_centre", the default; "cell_height"). With "cell_centre" the first layer is 2 y, so the target falls at the first cell centre; `foam/yplus.first_layer_thickness(..., yplus_at)` (its own default stays "cell_height" for direct callers such as the flat-plate validation case, which is unchanged); used by `workflow/meshing.plan_layers_for` (Mesh properties, Generate Mesh, the benchmark) and the mesh study's shared stack. The Target y+ field's tooltip says where the target is placed ("The target y+ is placed at the first cell centre, where OpenFOAM evaluates y+: the first layer is twice the target wall distance."; a "cell_height" project gets a tooltip saying OpenFOAM reports about half the target). Schema v14: the v13 -> v14 migration sets `yplus_at "cell_height"` where the file does not set it (V14_MESH_FIELDS; presets too), so saved projects keep their layers; the legacy value is left out of the mesh hash, so their meshes stay up to date. Fixture tests/data/project_v13.json; the estimator is tested against a hand calculation (V 20 m/s, nu 1.5e-5, L 1 m, y+ 50: 0.90065 mm target distance, 1.8013 mm first layer).
- Alternatives considered: keeping the convention and halving the benchmark's band (the y+ the user asks for would still not be the y+ the solver reports); doubling only in the benchmark.
- Tooltip guidance (owner review of 5.4b): with absolute sizing the Target y+ tooltip ends with "Wall-resolved (no wall functions): about 1 at the first cell centre. Wall functions: 30-300; the default is 50." (`properties_panel.YPLUS_GUIDANCE`).
- Validation (owner review of 5.4b, docs/QUESTIONS.md item 150): the flat-plate case (tests/validation/flat_plate_case.py) uses the cell-centre convention with target y+ 1, the equivalent of the recorded study's target 2 under the cell-height convention (y+ about 1 at the first cell centre, the wall-resolved requirement). Its first-cell heights equal the recorded coarse, medium and fine grids' (tests/test_flat_plate_grids.py, 1e-9 relative), so the recorded TMR verification (PASS WITH NOTE) applies by equivalence; no rerun. The y+ 2 run at the cell centre (outside the band) is kept as evidence in the git-ignored benchmarks/results/5.4b/flat_plate_cell_centre/.
- Consequences: new projects get a first layer twice as thick and about log(2) / log(r) fewer layers (3.8 at r 1.2) for the same surface cells; the stack total is unchanged where the layer count is not capped. The benchmark's y+ band [0.5, 2] x target is now centred on the expected value.

## D-108 Placeholder fvSchemes written as token streams; regular-expression keywords quoted
- Status: Accepted (owner review of 5.4b; task 5.4b, Part D; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 145). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the meshing case's placeholder system/fvSchemes was written with list values (`default (Gauss linear corrected);`, also `gradSchemes`), which snappyHexMesh and checkMesh never read but a displacement motion solver cannot parse (the 5.4 motionSolver variant failed to start on it). The unquoted `cellDisplacement.*` fvSolution key was in the 5.4 study's session driver only (the app's meshing fvSolution is `solvers {}`): unquoted, OpenFOAM reads it as a plain word, not a pattern.
- Decision: `foam/mesh_case.fv_schemes` writes the scheme entries as tuples (`default Gauss linear corrected;`). tests/test_foam_dict_syntax.py writes the meshing case (y+ and relative sizing) and the solver case and checks OpenFOAM's rules on every dictionary: no scheme entry is a list, and every keyword written as a regular expression (`.*`, `.+`, `|`, a leading `^`, a bracket class) is quoted; with OpenFOAM installed, foamDictionary reads `laplacianSchemes/default` as "Gauss linear corrected".
- Alternatives considered: none (a defect).
- Consequences: no change to any mesh (snappyHexMesh does not read the schemes) or to the mesh hash.

## D-109 The achieved first cell reported after meshing; y+ drift warning
- Status: Accepted (owner review of 5.4b; task 5.4b, Part C; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 141). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the aircraft's planned first layer was kept, but where its stacks collapse the wall cells are core cells several times thicker (RESULT_5.4: mean first cell 3.7 mm against 0.64 mm, indicative y+ 618.6 for a target of 50); nothing in the app said so.
- Decision: the layer walk moves from bench/ to `foam/layer_walk.py` (bench/layer_walk re-exports it) and records for every wall face the wall-adjacent cell's height (`first_cell`: the prism's height when the face carries a prism, layer or not; otherwise twice its centre's distance from the wall face). With absolute (y+) layers the GUI passes the planned first layer and layer count to `MeshRun` (`first_layer`); after checkMesh `foam/mesh_run.measure_first_cell` walks the part patches of the final mesh and stores `MeshReport.first_cell` (`FirstCell`: area-weighted mean, area-weighted 95th percentile, the planned first layer; or `error` when the walk fails: a measurement never fails the mesh step). It is kept in the mesh record (older records: none). When the mean is above `FIRST_CELL_DRIFT` = 1.5 x the planned first layer, the Mesh report's new "First cell at the wall" row (in the warning colour) and the Run Solver tooltip ("Warning: ...") say "Layers collapsed near the wall: first cell X mm vs Y mm planned; y+ will be about Z times the target.", Z = mean / planned (y+ grows about linearly with the wall distance at a given u*). A warning, never a block. Relative sizing is not measured.
- Alternatives considered: snappyHexMesh's own thickness fields (they count collapsed stacks as layers, D-105); a block (the owner asked for a warning).
- Consequences: a few seconds after checkMesh on the public cases (the walk took 4.4 s on the Ahmed mesh); the benchmark keeps its own walk measurements (its MeshRun gets no first layer, so the walk is not run twice).

## D-110 Benchmark baseline after task 5.4b
- Status: Accepted (owner decision in the review of task 5.4b; the entry accepted in the following review).
- Decision: the 5.4b public run (git `37b1281`, RESULT_5.4b.md Part F) is the committed baseline, benchmarks/baseline.json; the 5.1b baseline is kept as benchmarks/baseline_1.json (baseline_0.json stays the pre-5.1 one); the superseded 5.4 candidate (baseline_candidate.json) is removed. BASELINE.md describes the new baseline. Checked with `--result benchmarks/baseline.json --compare` against the run's own result file: no deltas.
- Consequences: `--compare benchmarks/baseline.json` now measures against the D-106 / D-107 meshes (6-7 layers of a 1.80 mm first layer); comparisons with older runs flag the changed target layer count (D-087).

## D-111 Parallel snappyHexMesh with reconstruction to a serial mesh (schema v15)
- Status: Accepted (owner review of task 5.x-parallel-mesh, with two changes: the automatic meshing cores no longer depend on the Solvers cores, QUESTIONS 155; the mesh study meshes its levels in parallel, QUESTIONS 156). Was Proposed (owner decisions P1-P3 of the task). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: meshing ran on one core while the solver already ran on several (D-063); the aircraft takes 13 min to mesh (D-106). The owner decided: the mesh is always reconstructed into one serial mesh after a parallel run, so the viewer, the lost-part guard, the mesh report, checkMesh and the first-cell walk are unchanged, and the solver keeps decomposing as today (P1); meshing cores = the project's cores capped by a recommendation from the estimated cells, never above the physical cores, editable in the Mesh properties, 1 = the serial path unchanged (P2); the benchmark stays serial by default (P3).
- Decision:
  - Pipeline (`foam/mesh_case.mesh_commands(cores)`; `MESH_COMMANDS` unchanged for 1 core): blockMesh and surfaceFeatureExtract serial; `decomposePar -force`; every snappyHexMesh pass (the castellation-only pass of D-084 and the main one) as `mpirun -np N snappyHexMesh -overwrite [-dict ...] -parallel` on that one decomposition; `reconstructParMesh -constant` once after the last pass; createPatch (rounded domains) and checkMesh on the serial mesh. `-constant`: with `-overwrite` the passes write processor*/constant/polyMesh, and reconstructParMesh (timeSelector with constant enabled) refuses a time without polyMesh/points; it is what the v2606 motorBike, turbineSiting and createZeroDirectory/motorBike tutorials run after `runParallel snappyHexMesh -overwrite`.
  - Decomposition: system/decomposeParDict with `numberOfSubdomains N; method scotch;` (foam/parallel.decompose_par_dict, the solver's, D-063), written by MeshRun and removed with processor*/. snappyHexMesh in parallel needs a parallel-aware method (snappyHexMesh.C: FatalError otherwise, "hierarchical, ptscotch"); scotchDecomp is parallel aware (scotchDecomp.H, it gathers the graph on the master). Several v2606 snappyHexMesh tutorials use scotch (snappyMultiRegionHeater, rotated_block, lumpedPointMotion building / bridge, the 3DBox reEval cases); the motorBike family uses hierarchical, which needs subdomain counts per direction chosen for the domain shape. snappyHexMesh's own balancing (maxLoadUnbalance 0.10, maxLocalCells, the layer pre-balance) re-decomposes with the same method and is kept unchanged; no dictionary of the meshing case changes.
  - Logs: Info is master-only, so log.snappyHexMesh has the serial format plus the parallel header; the layer table, the stage times and the layer diagnostics parse unchanged (tests/data/snappyHexMesh_sphere_parallel.log). Steps and logs are named as before plus log.decomposePar and log.reconstructParMesh.
  - Stop: the running step's FoamRun.request_stop (no clean stop: SIGTERM to the process group and every process of the run's session, then SIGKILL, D-063) ends mpirun and the ranks. processor*/ and the decomposeParDict are removed after the reconstruction, before checkMesh, and after a stopped or failed run (and stale ones before decomposePar). `prepare_case_dir` now also deletes case/vortexcfd_mesh.json together with constant/polyMesh (serial runs too), so a stopped or failed run leaves the mesh not generated even after reopening the project.
  - Errors: a failed step sets `MeshRunResult.error` ("decomposePar failed (exit code 1): see <log>"), logged after "Meshing failed in <step>"; the mesh is not generated.
  - Model (schema v15): `Mesh.cores: int | None` (None = automatic). `workflow/meshing.mesh_cores`: automatic = `foam/parallel.recommended_mesh_cores(estimated background cells incl. refinement volumes, physical)` = min(physical cores, cells / 12,500, 8), at least 1; the Solvers cores do not enter (owner review: the solver's cap of 4, D-064, measured the solver's efficiency, not meshing's; the solver recommendation is unchanged); a set number is used as it is, at most the physical cores. The recommendation is `recommended_cores` with the meshing constants MESH_CELLS_PER_CORE = 12,500 background cells per rank and MESH_EFFICIENT_CORES = 8 (from the measurement, below). Left out of the mesh hash (a parallel run meshes the same inputs) and out of presets (machine setting, like the solver cores); the v14 -> v15 migration fills nothing. The Mesh properties get a "Parallel" section: "Mesh cores" (spin box, 0 = "auto", up to the physical cores) and "Cores used" ("8 (automatic; recommended 8 for 100,000 background cells, 12,500 per core)"); the field's tooltip says "Meshing scales better than the solver; it can use more cores". The mesh record stores the cores used (`MeshRecord.cores`, older records 1).
  - Benchmark: `--mesh-cores N` (default 1, P3), `mesh_cores` and `mesh_peak_total_mb` (resident memory of all the run's processes together, sampled every 0.5 s from /proc; getrusage only gives the largest single process) in the result.
  - Mesh study (owner review, QUESTIONS 156): `workflow/mesh_study.prepare_study` stores the project's meshing cores (`mesh_cores(project, box).used`: Mesh.cores, else automatic) in `StudyRecord.mesh_cores` (study.json; older records 1), and StudyRun meshes every level on them, one level at a time; each level's MeshRun decomposes, reconstructs and removes its own processor*/ (also after a stop or failure), and a stop ends the running level's ranks and the study. The polar sweep reuses one mesh. Checked on a real 2-level sphere study on 4 cores (34 s, tests/test_mesh_study.py, slow).
- Alternatives considered: keeping the decomposed mesh for the solver (P1: always reconstruct); hierarchical decomposition (needs per-direction counts); ptscotch (not used by most snappyHexMesh tutorials, same balancing role); a schema-free optional field (an older app would reject the file with an unknown-field error instead of the version message).
- Measured (benchmarks/RESULT_parallel_mesh.md; mesh only, nice 15, one run per point): mesh wall-clock 1 / 4 / 8 cores: sphere 52 / 33 / 25 s, half wing 63 / 43 / 32 s, Ahmed body 46 / 29 / 25 s, aircraft (CAD union) 497 / 189 / 120 s (speedup 8 cores 2.13 / 1.97 / 1.85 / 4.13). Validity, hard and soft checks, strict coverage overall and per zone, the walked first cell and the fidelity are the same to the noise (cells within 0.1 % on the public cases, -0.9 % on the aircraft; aircraft strict coverage 2.4 / 2.5 / 2.7 %). Every case has about 100,000 background cells, so the background estimate cannot separate the aircraft's speedup from the public cases'; the cap rule takes the larger core count that was still faster everywhere. The summed memory grows with the ranks (sphere 460 -> 1027 MiB at 8).
- Consequences: a parallel mesh is not bit-identical to the serial one (snappyHexMesh's decisions depend on the partition); measured in benchmarks/RESULT_parallel_mesh.md. The benchmark's secondary layer figure from snappyHexMesh's layer fields is not available for parallel runs (the fields stay in processor*/); the headline walk is unaffected (D-105).

## D-112 A mesh or solver run is bound to the case folder it started in
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). The owner also accepted the behaviour after a project switch: the mesh record goes to the run's own case folder and that case's out-of-date results are removed (D-051). Was Proposed (review follow-up, docs/REVIEW.md R-01).
- Context: switching projects only requests a stop of a running mesh or solver; a run that ended normally in the meantime wrote its record into the newly opened project's case/ and, for a mesh, deleted that project's results (D-051).
- Decision: the window remembers the case folder (and, for a mesh, the part patches) of the run it starts. When the run ends and that folder is not the open project's case/, the mesh record (with the removal of the results it makes out of date, D-051) or the run record goes to the run's own case folder, the console says so, and nothing is shown or changed in the open project; a stopped or failed mesh writes nothing. Results read for a case that is no longer open are ignored.
- Consequences: the open project's records and results are never touched by another project's run; a project whose run finished after a switch shows that run when it is reopened.

## D-113 Every background run reports its end, also after an exception
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). Was Proposed (review follow-up, docs/REVIEW.md R-02).
- Context: `MeshRun` ran its steps in try/finally only: an exception (a step that cannot start, a log or the decomposeParDict that cannot be read or written) ended the thread without `on_done`, so the top bar stayed in "Meshing". `SolverRun` caught only OSError, ValueError and RuntimeError, and nothing around the classification after the process; the mesh study and the polar sweep wrote their first and last record outside their exception handler.
- Decision: `MeshRun` turns any exception into a failed `MeshRunResult` (`failed_step` = the step that was running, `error` = "<step> failed: <type>: <message>") after removing processor*/ as before; `SolverRun` reports any exception as a crashed run whose `crash` text is the error; the study and the sweep save their first record inside the handler and report a last record that cannot be written in the console. In each case `on_done` is called, so the state badge returns and the error appears in the console. The geometry worker, the overlap and intersection checks and the results loader already caught every exception.
- Consequences: no change for runs without an exception.

## D-114 Project files, presets and records written atomically with the umask's permissions
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). The owner also accepted that a file saved again gets the umask's mode (a mode set by hand is not kept). Was Proposed (review follow-up, docs/REVIEW.md R-04, R-05).
- Context: the project file and the presets were written through a temporary file created with mode 0600, so every .vcfd and preset file was readable by its owner only, unlike every other file the app writes (0644 under the usual umask 022). The mesh record, the run record, study.json and sweep.json were written in place (truncate, then write): a crash mid-write left half a file, which reads as no record.
- Decision: one writer, `core/atomic_io.write_text_atomic`: a temporary file in the target's folder, chmod to 0666 minus the process umask (read from /proc/self/status, so the umask is never changed), then `os.replace`; on any failure the temporary file is removed and the old file is left whole. The project file, the presets and the four records use it; their names and content are unchanged.
- Consequences: a file saved again gets the umask's mode (an older 0600 .vcfd becomes 0644 under umask 022 at its next save; a mode set by hand on one of these files is not kept).

## D-115 Edit > Add part (placeholder) disabled
- Status: Accepted (owner, as implemented, QUESTIONS 162). The owner also accepted that the Edit menu shows its actions' tooltips (Add Wake Volume's too). Was Proposed (review follow-up, docs/REVIEW.md R-18).
- Context: the Edit menu item "Add part (placeholder)" (from Phase 2, before geometry import existed) added a part with no surface file; File > Import Geometry is the way to add parts since Phase 3.
- Decision: the item stays in the Edit menu, disabled, with the tooltip "Not built yet: an empty part without a surface. Add parts with File > Import Geometry." (D-039: features not built are omitted or disabled with a tooltip); the Edit menu shows its actions' tooltips (so Add Wake Volume's tooltip is shown there too). `MainWindow.add_placeholder_part` stays for the tests, which call it directly.
- Consequences: the user can no longer add an empty part from the menu.

## D-116 Ground plane boundary of the box domain (schema v16)
- Status: Accepted (owner review of validation-ahmed round 1, 2026-10-21, with the slip strip of D-118; QUESTIONS 168 b). Open: 168 a and c (see D-118 for the ground layers in the Ahmed round 2). Was Proposed (task validation-ahmed, Part A, owner decision V1). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the Ahmed body validation needs the ground of the experiment; the box domain's bottom face was always the slip far field "bottom" (D-022), so a body in ground proximity could not be modelled.
- Decision:
  - Model (schema v16): `Domain.ground` = `Ground`: `enabled` (default False), `motion` "stationary" (default) or "moving", `position` "parts_min" (default: the lowest z of the parts the domain is sized around) or "value" (`z`, metres), `layers` (default False). Only the box shape takes it (validator: "the ground plane needs the box domain shape"). The v15 -> v16 migration fills nothing: older projects keep the slip bottom. A domain setting, so presets store it (`PresetDomain.ground`; preset files without it read it off).
  - Geometry (`geometry/domain.compute_domain`): with the ground the box's z_min face is at the ground plane (the top keeps `vertical` x L above the parts) and its role is "ground", replacing "bottom" (`shape_roles(shape, symmetric, ground)`); a part wholly below the plane gets a warning; meshing is blocked when the plane is above every part. Parts that cross the plane (supports) are cut by the domain: the blockMesh face is the plane and snappyHexMesh removes the cells inside the parts, as the v2606 motorBike tutorial does with its wheels on the lowerWall.
  - Mesh: the blockMesh z_min face is the patch `ground` of type wall (`mesh_case.GROUND_PATCH`; "ground" is now a reserved patch name: a part named "ground" gets the patch "ground_1"); the domain guard expects it instead of "bottom". Prism layers on the ground only with `layers` (the parts' stack, `layers.ground`). Off by default: the floor of an external-aerodynamics box is the largest wall of the domain (for the Ahmed case 13 m x 5.4 m against the body's 0.9 m2 half surface), so a stack on it multiplies the near-wall cells for a boundary layer whose effect on the body's forces is the gap flow under the body, which the wall functions on the castellated ground cells already carry; the user can turn them on where the ground boundary layer itself matters.
  - Solver (`physics_case.initial_fields`): the ground gets the part walls' conditions (p zeroGradient, k kqRWallFunction, omega omegaWallFunction, nut nutUSpaldingWallFunction); U noSlip when stationary; when moving U fixedValue (U_inf, 0, 0): the free-stream speed along the flow direction projected on the ground, which is +x at every angle of attack since the app turns the free stream about y (the v2606 motorBike tutorial's lowerWall uses fixedValue at the free-stream velocity). The far-field slip patches lose "bottom" (`far_field_patches`).
  - Forces and y+: the force, forceCoeffs and wallShearStress objects list the part patches only, so the ground is never in a part's or in all walls' forces; the yPlus function object writes every wall patch (v2606 yPlus.C has no patch selection), and the reports' y+ (`YPlusTail.series`) leave the ground out.
  - Hashes: the mesh hash carries the ground only when it is on and without its motion (a solver input: the results hash carries it), so projects without the ground keep their mesh and results hashes.
  - GUI: the tree lists "ground" among the domain boundaries; the Fluid Domain node and the ground node show a "Ground plane" section (enabled, motion, position, z when "value", prism layers, and the boundary conditions written); each edit is one undo step.
- Alternatives considered: a separate slip section upstream of the ground (the ERCOFTAC 9.4 workshop practice, matching a measured boundary layer thickness; needs a split bottom face, left for later); a moving ground along the free stream at incidence (would push flow through the ground plane); layers on the ground by default.
- Consequences: a validation case in ground proximity can be built through the app (docs/validation/ahmed/). Schema v16: an older app cannot open a project saved by this one.

## D-117 Mesh study levels keep the refinement volumes and junction curves
- Status: Accepted (owner review of validation-ahmed round 1, 2026-10-21; QUESTIONS 169 closed). Was Proposed (task validation-ahmed, Part A). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the mesh study (D-066) was written before the refinement volumes (D-103) and the junction curves (D-093, D-097): `workflow/mesh_study.prepare_study` built each level's MeshCaseInput without them, so every level of a project with a wake volume was meshed without the wake refinement, unlike Generate Mesh.
- Decision: every level gets the project's enabled refinement volumes and junction curves at the refinement levels of the current settings (`_volume_entries`, `_junction_features` of the project). A level scales the base size and every target size by the same factor (D-066), so the level numbers are unchanged and the cells inside a volume scale like the rest; the junction curve file is written into each level case. The ground plane (D-116) is passed too.
- Alternatives considered: recomputing the volume levels per level from the scaled target size (gives the same level numbers by construction, with rounding risk at a power of two).
- Consequences: a study of a project with volumes meshes what Generate Mesh meshes at every level. The study's background-cell limit check still counts background cells only (as before).
