## D-118 Ground slip strip and the mean-stable stopping criterion (schema v17)
- Status: Accepted (owner review of validation-ahmed round 2, 2026-10-21; QUESTIONS 176 accepted, 175 open). Was Proposed (task validation-ahmed round 2, Part A; owner review of round 1, QUESTIONS 168 b and 170). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the owner read the round 1 FAIL of the Ahmed validation as inconclusive. Two of the reasons need the app: (b) the inlet 1.35 L ahead of the nose was where Ahmed's ground board started, not where the free stream started, and the ground had no slip section ahead of the board (D-116 left it for later); (QUESTIONS 170) the steady run oscillates about a stationary mean, so neither the residual nor the range-based asymptotic criterion can stop it and no averaged value was shown.
- Decision:
  - Ground slip strip. `Ground.wall_start_x: float | None` (metres; None = the wall starts at the inlet, as before). When it lies strictly inside the box's x range (`geometry/domain.ground_wall_start`), the bottom face is two patches: `ground_slip` (type patch) upstream, `ground` (wall) downstream. The blockMesh background is written as two blocks split at the background cell face nearest to the value (`mesh_case.ground_split`, kept between 1 and nx - 1 cells), so the cells, the refinement levels and locationInMesh are those of the single block; the split is at most half a background cell from the asked x (`ground_split_x` gives the actual one; the mesh study's levels split at their own nearest face). Prism layers (`Ground.layers`) go on `ground` only. The domain guard expects both patches. Solver: `ground_slip` is one of the far-field patches (`far_field_patches(shape, ground, slip_strip)`), so it gets the far field's conditions (U slip; p, k, omega zeroGradient; nut calculated); the forces, wall shear stress and part y+ never list it (a patch, not a wall). A wall start at or behind the outlet blocks meshing with a message. "ground_slip" is a reserved patch name. GUI: "Wall starts at x" on the ground plane rows (the minimum shows "inlet" = unset), and the boundary-condition text names the strip. Mesh hash: `wall_start_x` enters only when set (projects without a strip keep their hash).
  - Mean-stable criterion. `MeanStableCriterion` (kind "mean_stable"; quantity and floor as the asymptotic criterion, of which it is a subclass): met when d = |mean(last W) - mean(previous W)| / max(|mean(last W)|, floor) <= tolerance, evaluated from 2 W values and from `min_iterations` on (`convergence.mean_change`, `criterion_value`, `first_met_iteration`). App-side like the asymptotic criterion: SolverRun evaluates it after every iteration and stops the solver with the D-058 clean stop. Defaults W = 500, tolerance 0.005 (0.5 %).
  - Defaults from round 1's recorded body Cd histories (tests/data/ahmed_round1_cd_body.csv, 1500 iterations per level, oscillation period about 167 iterations): with W = 500 (three periods) the criterion is met at iterations 1002 (coarse), 1003 (medium) and 1036 (fine) with means 0.3416, 0.3464 and 0.3250, within 0.21 % of the means over iterations 501-1500 judged in RESULT.md (0.3409, 0.3468, 0.3250). The 500-iteration rolling mean swings by 0.60 / 0.46 / 0.02 % over the stationary part (iterations 300-1500) against 2.63 / 1.36 / 0.14 % for W = 100 and 1.40 / 0.74 / 0.04 % for W = 250: a shorter window's mean depends on the phase of the oscillation and its stop on a chance coincidence of two phases (W = 100 stops at 206-260 on every level for any tolerance from 0.1 % to 1 %). The tolerance hardly matters on these histories (0.1 % to 1 %: the same stop within 30 iterations); 0.5 % is the 500-window mean's own swing on the coarse level and a third of the smallest level-to-level change of round 1 (1.7 %), so the stopping error stays below the grid differences to be resolved.
  - Monitors and results: each report slot shows, under the latest value, "mean m ± a% (n it.)" over the window of a mean-stable criterion watching that report, else the oscillation window (a = half of (max - min) as a share of max(|mean|, floor), the D-059 scale; `convergence.window_stats`). The Forces table gets the rows "Cd mean" and "Cl mean" (same windows) under the latest values. The D-059 warning of a quantity without a mean-stable criterion adds: "If its mean is steady, stop on it with a "mean stable" criterion on <quantity>".
  - Schema v17 (the new field and criterion kind); v16 -> v17 fills nothing.
- Alternatives considered: a slip strip from a topoSet / createPatch split after meshing (layers would be grown on the strip, the patch would not exist for snappyHexMesh); an exact split position with two blocks of different cell sizes (locationInMesh and the estimate assume one uniform background spacing); a drift test on the windowed mean (slope of a running mean; needs a second window anyway and a slope tolerance in coefficient units per iteration); under-relaxation changes for oscillating cases (a numerics change outside this task).
- Consequences: the Ahmed round 2 starts the ground wall 1.35 L ahead of the nose with a slip strip to the inlet 5 L ahead, and stops on the mean-stable criterion on the body Cd (docs/validation/ahmed/SETUP.md, "Round 2"). The mesh study still records the last iteration's values; the windowed mean is reported by the validation driver (open point in QUESTIONS; decided by D-119). `workflow/mesh_study.prepare_study(project, only=...)` prepares and records only the named levels and leaves the other level directories untouched, so a study can be run one level per command (the round 2 driver); the Mesh Study node still prepares every level.

## D-119 The mesh study records the windowed mean of a mean-stable or oscillating quantity
- Status: Accepted with one change (owner review, 2026-10-21: the oscillation case's mean window is the D-118 window, below). Was Proposed (task validation-ahmed, owner review of round 2, QUESTIONS 175). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: with the mean-stable criterion (D-118) a level stops on its windowed mean, but `foam/mesh_study` recorded and compared the last iteration's value of every quantity; on an oscillating run that value depends on the phase at the stop (Ahmed round 1: last values 0.3441 / 0.3435 / 0.3227 against means 0.3416 / 0.3464 / 0.3250). The validation driver had to compute the means and their GCI beside the study.
- Decision: at the end of each level, the value recorded for a quantity, and used by the GCI rows and the CSV, is (`mesh_study.recorded_value`):
  - the mean over the last W iterations when a mean-stable criterion of the study's stopping criteria watches the quantity and at least W values exist (W its window), met or not;
  - else, when the D-059 oscillation warning applies to the final series (same window, threshold, floor and drift test as the live warning), the mean over the D-118 window (the mean-stable criterion's default W, 500 iterations), or over the whole history if it is shorter; never over the warning's own window (`oscillation_window`, 100 by default): on round 1's Ahmed Cd (period about 167 iterations) a 100-iteration mean swings by 2.6 % with the phase (owner review);
  - else the last iteration's value (as before).
  `LevelResult` keeps `values` (the recorded value) and adds `last_values`, `value_basis` ("mean stable", "oscillation", "last"), `windows` (iterations in the mean) and `amplitudes` ((max - min) / max(|mean|, floor) over the window, the D-059 scale) in study.json; records written before have none of them and read as last values. A GCI row on windowed means says so in its note ("windowed means: coarse, medium, fine"), shown in the results window and the CSV's note column.
- Alternatives considered: the mean over the oscillation warning's window (the first proposal; rejected by the owner: shorter than one period of round 1's oscillation); the mean only when the mean-stable criterion was met (a level stopped at the iteration limit would fall back to a phase-dependent last value); a mean over a fixed window for every quantity (a converged run's last value is its best value); taking the oscillation amplitude into the GCI as an uncertainty (no recognised procedure; reported beside the value instead).
- Consequences: a study of an oscillating steady case compares windowed means without the validation driver; the GCI of such a study is still oscillatory or not applicable when the levels differ by less than the oscillation, which the amplitudes make visible. Tests: tests/test_mesh_study.py on round 1's recorded body Cd histories (tests/data/ahmed_round1_cd_body.csv).

## D-120 Angle of attack by geometry rotation; wind-axis forces; a polar sweep meshes each angle (schema v18)
- Status: Accepted (owner review of the NACA 0012 round 1, 2026-10-22; QUESTIONS 183-185 accepted as reported). Was Proposed (task W2, Part A; owner decisions A1-A4; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 71). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the angle of attack turned the free stream (inlet velocity (cos a, 0, sin a), forceCoeffs directions turned with it, D-050, D-067) while the far field stayed a slip box aligned with x: at incidence the flow was channelled by the slip walls, and the freestream far field that would fix it changed the flat-plate validation (QUESTIONS 71, open since phase 4). The owner decided (A1-A4): rotate the geometry, keep the flow along +x, report forces in wind axes, give refinement volumes a frame, remove the angled inlet, and mesh each angle of a polar sweep.
- Decision:
  - Rotation (geometry/attitude.py): a positive alpha turns the parts nose up about the y axis through the reference centre of rotation c (the Reference Values' CofR: its override, else the centre of the union of the parts' boxes as imported): p' = c + R (p - c), R = R_y(alpha) = [[cos a, 0, sin a], [0, 1, 0], [-sin a, 0, cos a]] (right-handed about +y: a point ahead of c moves up). A symmetric airfoil with its leading edge at the smaller x then gets a positive lift (+z) at a positive alpha (tests/test_attitude.py, the sign test). The rotation is applied when the meshing case is written (workflow/meshing._loaded_parts): the part surfaces (copies; the cached loads and the project's files never change), the surface preparation's junction curves (featureEdgeMesh points), and the "body"-frame refinement volumes (a box becomes an internal `OrientedBoxShape` with its axes turned, written as searchableRotatedBox; the other shapes have their points turned). Seed lines follow from the rotated parts' boxes. The surface preparation itself works on the parts as imported.
  - Domain and checks: the domain is sized around the rotated parts (their boxes). The reference values (A_ref, l_ref, CofR auto values) and the surface size length keep the parts as imported (`meshing.body_bounds`, `parts_layout`'s bounds). A ground plane at the parts' lowest z stays at the lowest z of the parts as imported; at alpha != 0 a rotated part that crosses the ground plane (any point below it) or leaves the domain (any point outside the domain shape) blocks meshing with a message naming the part and the angle (`meshing.attitude_problem`). At 0 degrees nothing changes (a support may cross the ground, D-116).
  - Refinement volumes (schema v18): `RefinementVolume.frame` "body" (the default: turns with the parts) or "wind" (stays; the wake helper's). The wake helper adds an unpitched box along +x behind the parts as meshed (rotated), frame "wind". The viewer draws every volume where meshing puts it.
  - Solver (foam/physics_case.py): the inlet and the initial velocity are (V, 0, 0) at every angle; forceCoeffs1 and the per-part objects use the wind axes, dragDir (1 0 0), liftDir (0 0 1), pitch axis e3 x e1 = +y (nose up positive), CofR = the rotation centre; a body-axis object `forceCoeffsBody` (all walls) has dragDir = x_b = (cos a, 0, -sin a) and liftDir = z_b = (sin a, 0, cos a), so its Cd and Cl are the axial and normal force coefficients, and its coefficient.dat header gives the body axes to the breakdown. The moving ground stays at (V, 0, 0).
  - Post-processing (foam/forces.py): Cd, Cl and their pressure / viscous parts in the wind axes (project_coefficients loses its angle); new report ids `C_axial` and `C_normal` (body axes, all walls or per part) and Forces table rows C_A, C_N; x_cp along the rotated body x axis: x_cp = x_CofR - M_y / F_N (F_N = F . z_b), in the coordinates of the parts as imported. Cases written before this decision have no body-axis object: the global axes are used, which were their body axes (the body was not rotated).
  - Removal and migration (A3): the angled-inlet path is gone (flow_direction() is +x). Schema v18: v17 projects keep alpha; their refinement volumes get frame "body" (they were placed on the parts as imported, so they keep their place on the parts; an old wake box pitched by alpha ends up along +x); the polar sweep's `init_from_previous` is dropped; a project with alpha != 0 gets a migration note ("The angle of attack (X deg) is now a rotation of the parts ...: the mesh is out of date") logged and shown in the status bar when opened (`load_simulation(path, notes)`, `Project.migration_notes`). Mesh hash: alpha and the CofR override enter only when alpha != 0, and a volume's frame only when alpha != 0, so projects at 0 degrees keep their hash and a rotated project meshed before v18 is out of date. Fixture tests/data/project_v17.json.
  - Polar sweep (A4, foam/polar_sweep.py, workflow/polar_sweep.py): every angle is its own marker-protected case case_sweep/point_<n>/ (its meshing case with the parts at that angle, written on the worker thread just before it is meshed), meshed with MeshRun on the project's meshing cores (parallel, D-111; with the lost-part guard and the first-cell report, D-109), then solved from the uniform free stream on the sweep's cores; one angle at a time. A point whose mesh fails is recorded ("mesh failed") and the sweep goes on. Stop ends the running step (meshing: its processes; solving: the clean stop). Run again resumes: when the angles, the solver cores and the inputs hash (results hash at alpha 0, CofR override, volumes) are unchanged, the finished points (criteria met, iteration limit, crashed, mesh failed) are kept and only the others are meshed and run; otherwise every point runs. The readiness checks every angle (each rotated part in the domain and above the ground). The record gains per point the cells, mesh time and error, and the meshing cores and planned first layer; old records still read. The sweep no longer needs case/ to be meshed. The mesh study keeps working at the project's fixed alpha (its levels are built from the same rotated parts).
  - Viewer: at alpha != 0 the part actors (and the selection overlays) carry the rotation as a VTK user transform about the CofR, and a note in the viewer's top-left corner says so ("Parts shown rotated to the angle of attack ... The project's geometry is unchanged."); the Angle of attack field's tooltip explains the rotation.
- Alternatives considered: the freestream far field with the angled inlet (QUESTIONS 71: changed the flat-plate validation, oscillated at 10 deg); rotating the domain instead of the parts (the box's faces would no longer be the inlet and far field); one shared mesh for the sweep with the inlet turned (the method this replaces); initialising each angle from the previous one through mapFields (the meshes differ; left out for robustness).
- Consequences: each sweep angle costs a mesh (parallel); the cases are independent and can be inspected one by one. Results of rotated projects made before v18 are out of date. Check (tests/test_attitude.py, marker slow/long, run once, 213 s): a sphere (24 x 24 STL, r 0.5 m, 60,000 background cells, Re 200) through the polar sweep at alpha 0 and 20 deg, each angle meshed with the sphere rotated about its centre (4 cores): 127,685 / 127,536 cells, Cd 0.646758 / 0.646118 (-0.10 %), Cl 2.99e-5 / -0.00264, both stopped by the Cd and Cl asymptotic criteria (438 / 892 iterations).

## D-121 2D section mode: airfoil import, a one-cell slab by directional refinement, extrudeMesh (schema v18)
- Status: Accepted with the span of one surface cell (owner review of the NACA 0012 round 1, 2026-10-22; QUESTIONS 186 accepted). Was Proposed (task W2, Part B; owner decision A5). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the NACA 0012 validation and airfoil work need a 2D analysis. The owner's pipeline (A5): the section extruded into a thin slab, meshed by snappyHexMesh with layers, then extrudeMesh to one cell across the span with empty front and back; checkMesh must pass in 2D.
- Decision:
  - Model (schema v18, in place on the unpublished branch): `Simulation.dimension` "3d" (default) or "2d" ("2D section (x-z plane)"); 2D takes the box or C-type domain without symmetry (validator; `Project.set_dimension` turns a bullet into a C-type and the symmetry off, one undo step). `Part.section` = `AirfoilSource` (file copy, format, scale, trailing-edge closure, airfoil name). Presets carry the dimension; built-in preset "2D airfoil": C-type radius 50 L, wake 50 L (100 chords across), 2D, 20,000 background cells, target surface size 0.002 l_ref, no curvature refinement, y+ 1 at the first cell centre with the wall-resolved layer controls (D-122).
  - Airfoil import (geometry/section.py, workflow/part_ops.airfoil_part, File > Import Airfoil...): Selig (TE -> upper -> LE -> lower -> TE) and Lednicer (point counts, then upper and lower from the LE) coordinate files, detected by the counts line; bad files are refused with the line and the reason (non-numbers, too few points, counts that do not match, surfaces that do not share the leading edge, x not increasing). Options: scale (the chord of a file in chord units) and trailing-edge closure: "blunt" closes an open trailing edge with a straight base, "sharp" moves both surfaces by a thickness growing linearly from the leading edge so they meet at the mid point. The section becomes a closed slab STL (span = chord), the part's surface; the file is copied into <project>/geometry/.
  - Any part is meshed in 2D by its section: its cut at mid y (`section.section_loops`, closed curves only) extruded across the slab and one span beyond each side (written into the case), so a thin extruded STL or CAD part works too. A part with several patches, or without a closed section, blocks meshing with a message.
  - Mesh [NUMERICS-REVIEW]: snappyHexMesh splits cells in all three directions, so a surface level n would put 2^n cells across the span (about 1000 for a 100-chord domain refined to millimetre cells). In 2D the background is one cell across y (blockMesh nx x 1 x nz; base size = (domain area / background cell target)^(1/2); span = the surface cell size base / 2^level_max, so the near-wall cells are cubes); the parts are refinement surfaces at level (0 0), and every surface level is a directional shell refinement in x and z only: around each part a staircase of searchableBoxes, the part's box grown by nCellsBetweenLevels cells per level, level k with `levelIncrement (0 0 (k 0 k))` (v2606 shellSurfaces.C reads levelIncrement as (minLevel maxLevel (nx ny nz)) for the cells inside, "ignores levels"; snappyRefineDriver.C runs it after the other refinement; tutorial mesh/snappyHexMesh/aerofoilNACA0012_directionalRefinement); the refinement volumes refine the same way at their level; no curvature refinement, features at level 0, no seed lines. The slab's spanwise faces are the patches slab_front / slab_back. After snappyHexMesh (and reconstructParMesh when parallel) `extrudeMesh` rebuilds the mesh from slab_front as one cell (extrudeMeshDict: constructFrom patch; sourceCase "<case>"; sourcePatches (slab_front); exposedPatchName slab_back; extrudeModel linearDirection, direction (0 1 0), thickness = span; nLayers 1; expansionRatio 1; flipNormals true; mergeFaces false; mergeTol 0; confirmed in applications/utilities/mesh/generation/extrude/extrudeMesh/extrudeMesh.C, src/mesh/extrudeModel/extrudeModel.C and linearDirection.C, and the tutorial compressible/rhoSimpleFoam/aerofoilNACA0012, which runs extrudeMesh on its own case). flipNormals true was found by trial: without it the new cells are inverted (every cell a negative volume); the default mergeTol (1e-4 of the bounding box) is set to 0 so no layer edge can be collapsed. createPatch then merges slab_front and slab_back into the empty patch `frontAndBack` and removes the emptied patches (the rounded domain's background). Steps: blockMesh, surfaceFeatureExtract, [decomposePar,] snappyHexMesh, [reconstructParMesh,] extrudeMesh, createPatch, checkMesh. The domain guard expects frontAndBack and no "sides".
  - Solver: frontAndBack is `empty` in every field; the far field has no "sides" (box: top, bottom; C-type: farfield). Forces are those of the span; the automatic reference area is chord (x extent of the parts as imported) x span, whatever the basis (a custom area is kept), so the coefficients are per unit span with A_ref = c.
  - Reports: the lost-part guard (patch face counts), the layer walk and the first-cell report (D-109) and the y+ function object work unchanged on the one-cell mesh (the wall faces are the section's edges times the span).
  - GUI: the New simulation dialog's "Analysis" (3D / 2D section) follows the preset and can be changed; the Simulation node has the same choice; a 2D part shows its airfoil source.
- Alternatives considered: isotropic refinement of a thin slab (2^n cells across the span); a span of one background cell (the owner's wording: with directional refinement any span keeps one cell across; one background cell is up to 1000 times the near-wall cell and the near-wall cells would be needles, failing the cell determinant); extrusion along the outward normal (the mesh would move by one span).
- Consequences: measured on the NACA 0012 slab (tests/test_section_2d.py, slow, 30 s): 129,553 cells at 10 deg with the 2D airfoil preset, checkMesh OK with 2 geometric directions, every point at one of two y values, 18.9 of 19 layers, first cell 9.11 um against 8.91 um planned.

## D-122 Wall-resolved layer controls: stack of one surface cell, surface cell at most 220 first layers, relaxed determinant and non-orthogonality in the layer phase
- Status: Accepted (owner review of the NACA 0012 round 1, 2026-10-22; QUESTIONS 188 accepted; 187 decided by D-123). Was Proposed (task W2, Part B2; docs/QUESTIONS.md item 180). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the Ahmed round 4 (QUESTIONS 180) showed that the robust defaults (D-106: cap 12, expansion up to 1.3) cannot build a y+ 1 stack: snappyHexMesh removed it on the cell determinant and face weight checks. The NACA 0012 needs y+ 1 at the first cell centre (D-107). The mechanism, read in the v2606 sources (src/dynamicMesh/motionSmoother/polyMeshGeometry/polyMeshGeometry.C checkCellDeterminant): snappyHexMesh's determinant is det(sum |S_f| n_f n_f / sum |S_f|) / (1/27), 1 for a cube; a layer cell of height t on a face h x s gives about 27 t^2 / (h s), so a y+ 1 first layer (t about 9 um) on 2-4 mm cells has 1e-4 to 6e-4, below the default minDeterminant 0.001 whatever the stack: every such layer is removed, in 3D (the Ahmed) as in 2D.
- Study (the NACA 0012 slab of D-121, hand-written prototype cases with the app's dictionaries: box -10..20 x -10..10 chords, base 1 m, directional levels 8 / 9 / 10 (h = 3.906 / 1.953 / 0.977 mm), first layer t1 = 8.91 um (y+ 1 at the first cell centre, 90 m/s, nu 1.5e-5, chord 1 m), featureAngle 130, nOuterIter 2, ratio 1.2 unless stated; "strict" = wall area with the full layer count, from the layer walk; "first cell" = area-weighted mean first cell / t1; "within 2x" = wall area whose first cell is within a factor 2 of t1; valid = checkMesh without hard failures; scratch cases under run/w2/proto, git-ignored):

  | h / t1 | layers | layer-phase relaxed set (besides minTetQuality 1e-15) | strict | first cell | within 2x | valid |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 439 | 30 | none (minDeterminant 0.001, maxNonOrtho 65) | 0 % | 16.1 | 0 % | yes |
  | 439 | 30 | minDeterminant -1 | 25.8 % | 11.3 | 26.9 % | yes |
  | 439 | 30 | minDeterminant 1e-6 | 25.8 % | 11.3 | 26.9 % | yes |
  | 439 | 30 | minDeterminant -1 (or 1e-5), maxNonOrtho 75 (70: the same mesh) | 94.6 % | 1.31 | 95.6 % | yes |
  | 439 | 38, ratio 1.15 | minDeterminant 1e-5, maxNonOrtho 75 | 92.9 % | 1.44 | 94.6 % | yes |
  | 439 | 20 / 25 / 28 | minDeterminant 1e-5, maxNonOrtho 75 | 0 / 30.4 / 95.0 % | 6.7 / 6.4 / 1.24 | 0 / 31.5 / 96.2 % | no / yes / yes |
  | 219 | 21 | none | 0 % | 6.5 | 0 % | no (open cells) |
  | 219 | 21 | maxNonOrtho 75 only | 0 % | 6.2 | 0 % | yes |
  | 219 | 21 | minDeterminant 1e-5 only | 13.7 % | 5.8 | 14.2 % | yes |
  | 219 | 17 / 21 / 22 | minDeterminant 1e-5, maxNonOrtho 75 | 66.6 / 98.3 / 98.1 % | 2.18 / 1.04 / 1.05 | 67.2 / 98.8 / 98.6 % | yes |
  | 219 | 23 / 26 | minDeterminant 1e-5, maxNonOrtho 75 | 97.8 / 97.7 % | 1.05 / 1.09 | 98.6 / 98.3 % | no (2 inverted pyramids / 4 negative volumes behind the trailing edge) |
  | 219 | 21, 10 and 15 deg | minDeterminant 1e-5, maxNonOrtho 75 | 98.1 / 98.0 % | 1.03 / 1.04 | 98.9 / 98.8 % | yes |
  | 110 | 13 / 17 / 21 | minDeterminant 1e-5, maxNonOrtho 75 | 99.4 / 99.4 / 99.5 % | 1.00 / 1.01 / 1.02 | 100 / 99.6 / 99.4 % | yes / yes / no |
  | 110 | 17, 15 deg | minDeterminant 1e-5, maxNonOrtho 75 | 99.2 % | 1.01 | 99.6 % | yes |

  Through the app with this decision (the "2D airfoil" preset: C-type 100 chords across, levels 8-9, h = 1.31 mm, h / t1 = 147, 19 layers, run/w2/e2e1 and e2e10): 82,283 cells at 0 deg and 129,553 at 10 deg, strict 98.8 % both, first cell 1.02 t1, 99.3 % within 2x, checkMesh OK (max non-orthogonality 62.4). Findings: both relaxations are needed (each alone keeps at most 14 %); maxNonOrtho 70 and 75 give the same mesh; at h / t1 = 439 the result depends erratically on the count (0 % at 20 layers, 95 % at 28); at 219 a stack of one surface cell (21 layers, its last layer 0.17 h) is clean, a thinner one (17, 0.45 h) keeps 67 %, and a thicker one (23-26, 1.5-2.6 h) inverts cells just behind the sharp trailing edge, where the stack ends; at 110 thinner stacks work too.
- Decision: a third value of `Mesh.layer_controls`, "wall_resolved" (schema v18; for targets in the viscous sublayer, y+ below about 5), with absolute (y+) sizing:
  - the stack: ratio WR_EXPANSION = 1.2 (fixed), the smallest count whose stack reaches one surface cell, n = ceil(ln(1 + h (r - 1) / t1) / ln r), at most WR_LAYER_CAP = 40 (`foam/yplus.plan_wall_resolved`); the first layer is the y+ estimate, unchanged; the final-layer to core-cell ratio is then about (r - 1) / r = 0.17;
  - the surface: the surface cell h at most WR_MAX_CELL_TO_FIRST = 220 first layers: meshing raises the maximum surface level (and the minimum to one below) until it is (`workflow/meshing.meshing_settings`, used by the estimate, the plan, the meshing case and the mesh study); the plan warns when h is coarser (a part level override) or the cap is reached;
  - the layer phase: the robust addLayersControls (featureAngle 130, nOuterIter 2) and a `relaxed` set used from the first layer iteration (nRelaxedIter 0), never while snapping: minTetQuality 1e-15 (as robust), minDeterminant 1e-5 (a y+ 1 layer cell is 1e-4 to 6e-4; a degenerate cell still tends to 0), maxNonOrtho 75 (`mesh_case.WALL_RESOLVED_RELAXED_QUALITY`); checkMesh still judges the final mesh with its own limits and a hard failure blocks the solver (D-096, D-100);
  - the mesh study: every level keeps the y+ first layer and the ratio and grows its stack to one of its own surface cells (`workflow/mesh_study.level_layers`; D-066's shared stack would be half a cell on the coarse level, the 67 % row); the first cell is still the same on every level, which is what D-066 protects. The levels record their planned stack and the measured first cell (mean, and the wall share beyond twice the plan; `FirstCell.share_over_2x`); `StudyRun(before_solve=...)` lets a caller leave a level unsolved (the NACA validation's rule A6).
  - The robust and legacy controls are unchanged (proved below); the GUI offers the value in the Mesh node's "Layer controls"; the "2D airfoil" preset uses it.
- Benchmark check (owner requirement: the wall-function defaults D-106 must not change): `python -m vortexcfd.bench --no-solve --compare benchmarks/baseline.json` (mesh only, serial, nice 15, at commit 61600ae plus the mesh study changes of this entry; result run/w2/bench/20261022T063249Z_61600ae-dirty.json, git-ignored): every mesh metric identical to the baseline on the three public cases: sphere 233,534 cells, max non-orthogonality 46.46, strict coverage 99.59 %, 6 target layers, fidelity RMS 0.08881 mm / max 0.154 mm; half wing 300,025, 64.91, 94.37 %, 7, worst zone (trailing edge) 38.36 %, 0.1068 / 0.7768 mm; Ahmed body 231,606, 64.28, 96.55 %, 6, worst zone (slant edges) 56.01 %, 0.06339 / 0.3629 mm; hard failures 0 everywhere; "no regressions" (only the run times differ: the baseline included the y+ runs).
- Alternatives considered: the defaults with a larger cap (25-40) and expansion 1.15-1.2 only (the 0 % row: the determinant removes the layers whatever their number); minDeterminant -1 (the same coverage as 1e-5, no check left against degenerate cells); a thicker stack (inverted cells at the trailing edge); a relaxed set for the robust controls too (the owner's D-106 defaults stay as they are).
- Consequences: wall-resolved meshes need fine surfaces (h <= 220 t1: about 2 mm for a 1 m chord at Re 6e6), so they are mainly for 2D sections and small 3D bodies; the y+ 1 Ahmed of round 4 would need about 2.8 mm surface cells on the whole body (D-106 robust used 5-7 mm).

## D-123 A target y+ below 5 selects the wall-resolved layer controls
- Status: Accepted (owner review of round 2, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w2-naca-r2, Part A1; owner review of the NACA 0012 round 1, QUESTIONS 187). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: D-122 made the wall-resolved controls a choice; with the robust controls (the default) a y+ 1 target gets a stack that the determinant check removes (D-122's 0 % rows). The owner decided (QUESTIONS 187) that a target y+ below 5 switches the wall-resolved mode on automatically, visibly, and that the user cannot lose it silently.
- Decision: `Mesh.wall_resolved_auto` is true when `layer_controls` is "robust", the sizing is absolute (y+) and `target_yplus` < `WALL_RESOLVED_AUTO_YPLUS` = 5.0 (core/model.py); `Mesh.effective_layer_controls` is then "wall_resolved". The stored value is not changed: a target of 5 or more gives the robust controls back. Meshing uses the effective value everywhere: `workflow/meshing.meshing_settings` returns a copy carrying "wall_resolved" (so the estimate, the raised surface level, the meshing case's relaxed quality set and the mesh study levels follow), `plan_layers_for` and `mesh_study.level_layers` read the effective value. "legacy" (projects saved before v13) and relative sizing never switch; an explicit "wall_resolved" stays as chosen. Mesh hash: an automatic switch adds `wall_resolved_auto` (instead of the robust controls' revision), so a robust y+ < 5 mesh made before is out of date; every other project keeps its hash. GUI (Mesh node): a read-only row "Layer mode in use" ("wall-resolved (automatic: target y+ 1 < 5)", "wall-resolved (chosen)", "robust", ...) with a tooltip giving the reason; while the target is below 5 the Layer controls combo shows wall_resolved and is disabled, with the same tooltip ("Set the target y+ to 5 or more to choose the layer controls"); legacy stays editable.
- Alternatives considered: storing "wall_resolved" when the target is lowered (raising the target would leave the wall-resolved mode on unseen, and the old robust choice would be lost); a warning only (QUESTIONS 187's other option; the owner chose the switch).
- Consequences: a new project with a target y+ below 5 meshes as the "2D airfoil" preset does (finer surface, a stack of one surface cell). The public benchmark (y+ 50) is unchanged. Tests: tests/test_wall_resolved.py, tests/test_physics_gui.py (the robust cap now shown at y+ 5).

## D-124 Wall-resolved 2D sections: a staircase buffer of 12 cells per level
- Status: Accepted (owner review of round 2, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w2-naca-r2, Part B; QUESTIONS 190). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the NACA 0012 round 1 failed at 10 deg with CD +24 % (all pressure drag) and CL -3 %, while 0 deg passed (CD -0.1 %) and the three study levels agreed within 1.2 %. The 2D staircase (D-121) grows each level's box by 3 cells of that level (snappyHexMesh's nCellsBetweenLevels), so at a distance d from the part's box the cell is about d / 6, on every level of a study alike (the base size cancels): measured on round 1's three levels, 44-63 mm at 0.3 chord above the airfoil, 177-250 mm one chord ahead, 250 mm two chords down the wake, the same on the coarse and the fine level; 97 % of the cells were in the part's box. The study refined the boundary layer and not the near field, where a lifting section's pressure field (decaying as 1 / r) and its wake live; a symmetric section at 0 deg hardly needs it.
- Study (10 deg, the round 1 medium level, base 1.414 m, surface cell 1.381 mm, every other input as round 1; docs/validation/naca0012/RESULT.md, Round 2): buffer 3 / 12 / 24 cells: 119,064 / 162,854 / 246,330 cells; CL 1.0346 / 1.0360 / 1.0344; CD 0.015592 / 0.012120 / 0.011904 (TMR SST 0.012467); CD pressure 0.010127 / 0.006670 / 0.006461; CD viscous 0.005466 / 0.005451 / 0.005443. 12 cells take 97 % of the change of 24 for 66 % of its cells.
- Decision: in 2D with the wall-resolved controls and absolute (y+) sizing, chosen or automatic (D-123), i.e. the "2D airfoil" preset, every staircase box is grown by `WR_SECTION_BUFFER_CELLS` = 12 cells of its level (`foam/mesh_case.section_buffer_cells`); every other 2D mode keeps `SECTION_BUFFER_CELLS` = 3 and 3D is untouched (no staircase). The mesh hash of a wall-resolved 2D project includes the buffer (its earlier mesh is out of date); other projects keep their hash. snappyHexMesh's own nCellsBetweenLevels stays 3. The cell estimate (background only) is unchanged.
- Alternatives considered: refinement volumes around the airfoil in the validation set-up only (the preset would keep the defect); 24 cells (1.8 % less drag for 51 % more cells); a buffer for the robust 2D modes too (left to the wall-function cases' own validation; the owner asked to limit proposals to the 2D airfoil preset and the wall-resolved mode).
- Consequences: about +35 % cells on the NACA 0012 at 10 deg. A mesh study still does not refine the near field (the cell at a distance d stays about d / 24 on every level): its resolution is set by the buffer, and the 3 / 12 / 24 comparison above is its convergence check (stated in RESULT.md).

## D-125 Wall-resolved 2D sections: the layer phase's medial-axis walk limited to 10 edges
- Status: Accepted (owner review of round 2, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w2-naca-r2, Part C; QUESTIONS 191). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: round 1's fine 0 deg NACA 0012 mesh failed checkMesh with 2 incorrectly oriented face pyramids (max skewness 42). Located (round 2, Part C): a mirror pair of internal faces at x = 1.0018, z = +-0.062 (chord 1), between two core hexahedra, about 60 cells from the wall, on the medial axis of the sharp trailing edge (6.11 cm from the nearest upper-surface face, 6.14 cm from the trailing-edge point); the points are off the lattice by up to 0.36 mm. snappyHexMesh's log shows the cause: the layer phase (displacementMedialAxis) moved them, and its quality check reported 2 faces above the relaxed non-orthogonality limit 75 (D-122) that it could not undo. Not the extrusion (extrudeMesh copies slab_front's faces; the inverted faces are in snappyHexMesh's mesh) and not the staircase box corner (with the D-124 buffer the corner is 11.7 mm away and the same 2 faces appeared: 179,784 cells, max skewness 21.8); the surface level decides only whether the distortion reaches inversion (the medium 0 deg and the fine 10 and 15 deg meshes passed). v2606's medialAxisMeshMover walks the whole mesh by default (nMedialAxisIter = the point count, medialAxisMeshMover.C).
- Decision: in 2D with the wall-resolved controls (chosen or automatic), addLayersControls gets `nMedialAxisIter 10` (`mesh_case.WALL_RESOLVED_2D_LAYER_CONTROLS`; the value of v2606's etc/caseDicts/mesh/generation/snappyHexMeshDict.cfg); the wall-resolved stack is one surface cell thick, so 10 edges reach well beyond it. 3D and the other modes are unchanged. The mesh hash of a wall-resolved 2D project includes it (with D-124's buffer).
- Check: the fine 0 deg level remeshed once (Part C, 48 s, 3 cores): 179,784 cells, checkMesh "Mesh OK", max skewness 1.69, first cell 1.02 times the plan, 0.5 % of the wall above twice the plan; snappyHexMesh "Limited walk to 10 steps".
- Alternatives considered: a stricter relaxed non-orthogonality (D-122's study: 75 is needed for the stack); changing the hard / soft checkMesh classes (excluded by the owner); moving the staircase corner (D-124 alone did not remove the faces).

## D-126 Two-stage turbulence scheme for the wall-resolved mode
- Status: Accepted (owner review of round 3, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w2-naca-r2, round 3, Part A; QUESTIONS 193; owner review of round 2: the round 2 PASS came from the validation driver and W2 closes only when the app reproduces it). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the NACA 0012 round 2 passed only with a driver restart that switched k and omega from first-order upwind (D-022) to a bounded second-order scheme after a converged first-order run (+1.4 to +2.2 % CL at 10 deg; at 15 deg CL steady to 0.8 % instead of 14.5 %). Round 1's second-order run from the free stream diverged (iteration 201).
- Decision: in the wall-resolved mode (chosen or automatic, D-123; `workflow/solving.two_stage_turbulence`) one solver run has two stages:
  - stage 1: the app's fvSchemes (bounded Gauss upwind for k and omega), from the free stream;
  - the switch, app-side: when the run's own stopping criteria are met (the driver's rule: its restart started from the first-order solution converged to the app's criteria, asymptotic Cl and Cd at 0 and 10 deg, mean stable at 15 deg; a converged first-order field is the start that does not diverge), or at half the iteration limit if that comes first (so stage 2 always gets half the budget when stage 1 does not settle; new, not tested by round 2), the solver is stopped with the D-058 clean stop (it writes its latest time);
  - stage 2: `physics_case.TURBULENCE_STAGE2` (div(phi,k) and div(phi,omega) bounded Gauss linearUpwind grad(k) / grad(omega), those gradients cellLimited Gauss linear 1: round 2's scheme) is merged into system/fvSchemes, controlDict starts from the latest time, and the same solver command continues on the same decomposition, appending to log.simpleFoam; the criteria are those of the run, not met before the switch plus what each needs from its first value (`solver_run.stage2_criteria`: the minimum iterations, the window, two windows for mean stable: +500 for the NACA asymptotic set, +1000 for its mean-stable set, as the driver), so no window reaches into stage 1.
  - Case file: the solver writer adds `system/turbulenceStages` (the stage-2 entries; OpenFOAM ignores it), and leaves out runTimeControl (two stages always evaluate the criteria in the app). SolverRun reads the file, so the GUI run, the mesh study, the polar sweep and the benchmark all run two stages for a wall-resolved case and one otherwise.
  - Stops and results: a stop in either stage ends the run with its latest time written (and reconstructed in parallel), as before; a switch that cannot start stage 2 (a crash at the switch) is classified from its exit code. `SolverRunResult.stages` and `RunRecord.stages` / `switch_iteration` keep both stages (scheme, first and last iteration, the switch reason and the iteration its condition was met); a mesh study level keeps `switch_iteration`. Records of one stage are unchanged.
  - Monitors: a line under the header, "Turbulence stage 1 of 2: first-order k, omega ..." then "Turbulence stage 2 of 2: second-order k, omega from iteration N (switch: criteria met)", also when a finished run is reopened; the console prints the switch; the convergence panel judges the criteria as stage 2 does.
  - The postProcessing readers follow a run continued from a later time: `ReportMonitor` starts from the earliest start-time directory and `TailReader` goes on with the next one (`postprocessing.next_start_file`), so the monitors, the criteria and the recorded values see both stages (the app clears postProcessing before every run, so a single-stage case has one directory and reads as before).
  - Results hash: a two-stage project adds `turbulence_stages` (earlier one-stage results of wall-resolved projects are out of date); others keep their hash.
- Check: fake-runner tests (tests/test_two_stage.py: the switch at the criteria and at half the limit, a stop in each stage, the record, the case files, the monitors); one real run (slow, long; run once, 151 s): the NACA 0012 coarse level at 10 deg through the app, switch at iteration 1269 (CL 1.0361, CD 0.012138), criteria met at 1926 (CL 1.0524, CD 0.012294). The wall-function mode is unchanged: the meshing and solver dictionaries of the three public benchmark cases are byte-identical before and after (48 files), and `--compare` (mesh only) reports no regressions.
- Alternatives considered: second order from the free stream (diverged in round 1; not retested); a switch on a residual drop (no threshold validated by round 2; the criteria are what the driver used); a separate log for stage 2 (the residual history and the reopened monitors read one log).

## D-127 Mesh study levels keep the current settings' refinement regions in metres (2D staircases, 3D level-scaled buffers)
- Status: Proposed: the 2D part verified (NACA 0012 round 3); the 3D part, rewritten to level-scaled buffers in task w3-close (owner decision), not passed in its fourth check; QUESTIONS 204 closed by the owner's closure rule with this limitation accepted for v1 (task w2-naca-r2, round 3, Part B; QUESTIONS 195, 204). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: D-066's levels scale the background (base) size and keep every refinement level number, so the wall cells and the background scale, but the regions between them are buffers counted in cells of each level: in 2D the staircase grows each box by n cells of its level (D-121, D-124), in 3D snappyHexMesh's nCellsBetweenLevels (3) does the same around every refinement surface. The cell at a distance d from a part is then about d / (2 n) on every level of a study: the NACA 0012 round 1 study refined the boundary layer and the box around the airfoil but not the field one chord away (measured: the same 44-250 mm cells at 0.3-2 chords on all three levels), and its 1.2 % spread missed a 25 % drag error (D-124). The 3D study has the same flaw in the transition zone around each part (up to about 3 base cells from it); its background and the cells next to the wall scale.
- Decision: a study level other than the current settings' (`MeshCaseInput.buffer_scale` = the current background cell over the level's, `workflow/mesh_study.level_buffer_scale`; exactly 1 for the current level and every single mesh) keeps the current settings' regions in metres:
  - 2D: the staircase buffer is the D-124 count times buffer_scale (a real number of cells), so every level has the same staircase boxes; at a fixed point the level index is the same and the cell is base / 2^k, scaling with the base;
  - 3D (since task w3-close; it replaced the distance shells described in the checks below): no shells; level k's nCellsBetweenLevels is max(1, round(3 r^(k - k_current))) (`mesh_case.study_cells_between_levels`, of the level's factor f = r^(k_current - k), rounded half up so 3 / 2 gives 2 whatever the factor's last bit; `MeshCaseInput.cells_between_levels`, 3 by default), so the buffers keep about the current settings' extent in metres and the current level, with 3, is the user's single mesh (its dictionaries byte-identical to prepare_job's on the sphere, the half wing and the Ahmed body). With r = sqrt(2) and the current settings as the fine level the counts are 2 / 2 / 3; as the medium level 2 / 3 / 4. `MeshCaseInput.study_level`, `study_buffer_shells` and `STUDY_CELLS_BETWEEN_LEVELS` are removed. 2D keeps nCellsBetweenLevels 3 (its transitions are the staircases, which already scale exactly, above).
  The current level's dictionaries, and every single mesh, are unchanged (the public benchmark's meshing and solver dictionaries byte-identical, 48 files).
- Check: tests/test_wall_resolved.py (the planned cell one chord ahead, one chord above and two chords down the wake shrinks by the study ratio, 1.414, from coarse to medium to fine; the staircase boxes identical on the three levels; the 3D shells), tests/test_mesh_study.py (a 3D sphere study: the coarse and medium shells identical); measured on the NACA 0012 study meshes in docs/validation/naca0012/RESULT.md, Round 3.
- Alternatives considered: integer nCellsBetweenLevels per level (3 / 2 / 2 for a "fine" current level: not a constant ratio); scaling the current level's buffer up (the study's current level would no longer be the user's mesh).
- Check of the 3D part (owner review of round 3, item 1; docs/validation/study3d/RESULT.md): real mesh-only studies of the sphere and the half wing: every level valid, coverage, layers and quality within noise of the single meshes at the same settings (the 1-cell transitions do not hurt), but inside the transition zone the cell size at fixed points does not shrink with the level ratio (the wing one length ahead: 318 / 450 / 318 mm; the sphere half a length ahead: 137 / 96 / 135 mm): the shells do not reproduce the current level's own snappyHexMesh transitions (the sphere's non-current levels have twice the single mesh's cells). The 3D part does not pass; the entry stays Proposed; fix proposed in QUESTIONS 204 (not applied).
- Owner decision on QUESTIONS 204 (2026-10-22; task w3-internal, Part 0): ALL levels of a 3D study, the current one included, are meshed with the same mechanism, the distance shells and nCellsBetweenLevels 1 (`MeshCaseInput.study_level`, set by `workflow/mesh_study.prepare_study` on every level; `buffer_scale` still gives the current settings' cell, exactly 1 on the current level). The study's finest (or, with "current as" medium, middle) level is then not identical to the user's single mesh: it has the shells' transition band where the single mesh has snappyHexMesh's 3-cell buffers (measured: 2.27 x the single mesh's cells on the sphere, 1.53 x on the half wing). This is acceptable because the study estimates the discretisation error of a consistent family of meshes, which needs the same transition geometry in metres on every level; a family whose current level transitions differently (round 1: 137 / 96 / 135 mm half a length ahead of the sphere) has no defined refinement ratio in that zone, and the GCI would not describe it. The single mesh stays the user's mesh; the study reports its own levels. Single meshes and the public benchmark are unchanged (`study_level` False).
- Check of the one-mechanism family (round 2, docs/validation/study3d/RESULT.md): every level valid, layers and quality within noise of round 1 (the sphere fine level's walked 92.3 % is the walk's aspect limit: snappyHexMesh and the walk at 0.8 give 100 %), the size of the cell containing each fixed point shrinks by the ratio at all 6 sphere points but at only 2 of 6 wing points: those 4 lie within half a coarse cell outside a shell edge, where snappyHexMesh's test at the cell centres (meshRefinementRefine.C, markInternalRefinement) refines a straddling coarse cell and not the medium one (318 / 450 / 318 mm one length ahead). Not passed: the entry stays Proposed (QUESTIONS 204 open).
- Round 3 (owner review of W3): shell distances rounded up to whole coarse cells and the size measured as a cube mean over 2 coarse cells: 2/6 sphere and 1/6 wing points pass (docs/validation/study3d/RESULT.md, Round 3); the rounding is not kept. Still Proposed.
- Round 4, the level-scaled buffers (task w3-close, owner decisions: the point-wise criterion withdrawn, an octree mesh cannot give the level ratio at every fixed point; the defect is the fixed 3-cell buffers; docs/validation/study3d/RESULT.md, Round 4): the same script and points, the cube mean over 2 coarse cells; pass: every level valid with quality and coverage within noise, the geometric mean of the level-to-level ratios over the six points within 10 % of r, no point off by more than a factor of 2. Every level valid (0 hard, 0 soft), the fine levels the benchmark's single meshes to the cell (233,534 and 300,025); no point off by more than 1.82 x; but the geometric means are 1.00 (coarse / medium) and 1.25 (medium / fine) on the sphere and 0.94 and 1.24 on the wing, against 1.414 +- 10 %: the rounding gives the coarse and the medium level the same count (1.5 and 2.12 both round to 2), so the coarse level's buffers are 1.41 x the medium's in metres and its near field is no coarser (the medium level is coarser than the coarse one at four of six points on each case), and the medium / fine step is 12-13 % short. The sphere's medium level also walks 97.55 % strict coverage and 57.8 degrees against the single mesh's 100 % and 49.8 (two-cell buffers). Not passed; by the owner's closure rule W3 is closed with this limitation: a 3D study's near field (the transition zone, up to about 3 base cells from the parts) is not refined by the ratio between the levels, so its GCI describes the wall cells and the background, not the near field. Kept rather than the shells because it fails the same check by less (the shells' round 2, by the containing cell: the wing's coarse / medium geometric mean 0.90 and four ratios of 0.71, a factor 2.0 off; round 3, by the cube mean with rounded shells: 3 of 12 points within 15 %) while the current level is the user's mesh at its own cost (the shells' was 1.5-2.3 x the cells); QUESTIONS 204.
- Consequences: a 3D study's coarse and medium levels mesh with 2-cell buffers (current as fine; existing study records stay as they were); the current level and every single mesh are unchanged (the public benchmark's dictionaries byte-identical, 51 files). The 2D studies are unchanged.

## D-128 A wall-resolved mesh study places the current settings within the first-layer limit
- Status: Accepted (owner, task w3-internal, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w2-naca-r2, round 3 follow-up, item 2; QUESTIONS 199). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the "2D airfoil" preset's surface cell (1.307 mm on the NACA 0012) is 147 first layers; as a study's fine level (the default "current as fine") the coarse level is 2.61 mm, 293 first layers, beyond D-122's tested limit of 220 (WR_MAX_CELL_TO_FIRST), where the stacks collapse erratically. Round 3 set "current as" medium by hand.
- Decision: with the wall-resolved controls (chosen or automatic, D-123) the study chooses the level the current settings are (`workflow/mesh_study.study_current_as`): the user's choice when its coarsest level's surface cell is within the limit, else the next position that is (fine -> medium -> coarse; the new internal position "coarse", factors 1, 1/r, 1/r^2, is always within it, since meshing keeps the current settings within the limit, D-122). The plan and the record carry the position used and why (`StudyPlan.current_as` / `current_as_note`, `StudyRecord.current_as` / `current_as_note`, e.g. "current settings as the medium level: as the fine level the coarse level's surface cell (2.61 mm) would exceed the wall-resolved limit (1.96 mm, 220 first layers, D-122)"); the Mesh Study node shows "Current settings used as: medium (automatic)" with the reason as its tooltip, and the "Current settings are" field keeps the user's setting. Other modes keep the user's choice; the user's options are still fine and medium.
- Consequences: the preset needs no "current as"; a wall-resolved study's levels never exceed 220 first layers. Old study records read with no note.


## D-129 Internal flow: the inside of the closed geometry, patch roles, mass-flow and total-pressure inlets, flow reports (schema v19)
- Status: Accepted (owner review of W3, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w3-internal, Part A; owner decisions I1-I4). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: v1 includes internal flow (docs/PLAN.md, W3); "internal" was a reserved analysis type (D-077).
- Decision:
  - Model (schema v19; v18 -> v19 fills nothing): `analysis_type` "internal"; `Simulation.boundaries` (PatchBoundary per model patch name, Part.patch_names(): role inlet, outlet or wall, a patch without an entry is a wall; `InletSpec` kind velocity (m/s normal to the patch), mass_flow (kg/s) or total_pressure (Pa gauge), turbulence intensity (default 5 %, as the TJunction tutorial) and length scale (None: 0.07 x the patch's hydraulic diameter, I2); `OutletSpec` static pressure (Pa gauge)); `Reports.planes` (CutPlane: name, point, normal) and `Reports.pressure_drops` (PressureDrop: upstream and downstream, "patch:<mesh patch>" or "plane:<name>"). The settings of an inlet and an outlet are kept when the role changes. `Project.update_boundary` is one undo step per edit.
  - Meshing: no domain. The DomainBox of internal flow (`geometry/domain.internal_box`) is the union of the parts' boxes with the fluid volume inside them (`geometry/internal.enclosed_volume`); the background box is that box grown by BACKGROUND_MARGIN_CELLS base cells (`estimate_mesh(internal=True)`), its faces the patch "background", removed by createPatch and checked empty by the domain guard (which also expects every inlet and outlet to have faces). locationInMesh is the background-grid point (at mesh_case.LOCATION_OFFSET of its cell) inside the closed geometry farthest from its surface (`geometry/internal.inside_point`: inside where the signed distance to the surface, its triangles oriented consistently outwards, is negative; vtkSelectEnclosedPoints' ray casting put a point below the 2D step's upstream wall inside), recomputed for every mesh study level's own grid; the fluid volume is that of the oriented surface (an STL's triangles need not be oriented alike: the step's gave 798 m3 for 1330). The geometry must be closed: the parts' surfaces together, coincident points merged, have no open or non-manifold edge; otherwise readiness names the counts and points to Surface Preparation or Repair. Inlet and outlet patches are snappyHexMesh patches of type patch, the others walls; prism layers on walls only; the first-cell measurement walks the walls only (single meshes and every mesh study level, `StudyRun(walls=...)`). In 2D (D-121) the section loop keeps a patch per segment (the nearest triangle's patch, `geometry/internal.labelled_section`), so a 2D part may have several patches, and a patch the section does not cut (e.g. end caps on a patch of their own) is refused before meshing (it would be a patch without faces); the slab has the same staircase as external 2D, i.e. the whole fluid at the parts' level. The surface size length (D-085) is l_ref when set, else the first inlet's hydraulic diameter; the y+ estimate (D-088), the mesh study's shared stack, the results' U_ref (Cf, Cp, sections) and the probe floors use the first inlet's bulk speed (`workflow/meshing.flow_speed`) (velocity; m / (rho A); for a total-pressure inlet the ideal sqrt(2 (p0 - p_out,min) / rho), an upper bound) with that length. No domain patch name is reserved in internal flow (QUESTIONS 206, owner review: a region named "inlet" stays "inlet"; only the background box's and the 2D slab's own names, `mesh_case.INTERNAL_RESERVED_PATCH_NAMES`); external projects keep the reservation. No angle of attack, ground plane or polar sweep. The mesh hash adds the roles, l_ref and the y+ speed (external projects keep their hash).
  - Boundary conditions (v2606 types confirmed in src/finiteVolume/fields/fvPatchFields/derived/*/ *.H, src/TurbulenceModels/turbulenceModels/RAS/derivedFvPatchFields/turbulentMixingLengthFrequencyInlet/ and the tutorial incompressible/pimpleFoam/RAS/TJunction): velocity inlet U surfaceNormalFixedValue refValue -V, p zeroGradient; mass-flow inlet U flowRateInletVelocity volumetricFlowRate Q = m / rho_ref (uniform, extrapolateProfile no), p zeroGradient; total-pressure inlet p totalPressure p0 / rho_ref (kinematic, p = p0 - 0.5 |U|^2 on inflow), U pressureInletOutletVelocity (TJunction's pair: inflow from the patch-normal component of the internal value, zero gradient on outflow; the inflow is normal to the patch); every inlet k turbulentIntensityKineticEnergyInlet (k = 1.5 (I |U|)^2) and omega turbulentMixingLengthFrequencyInlet (omega = k^0.5 / (Cmu^0.25 L)), both zero gradient on backflow; static outlet p fixedValue, U, k, omega inletOutlet (zero velocity and the inlet turbulence on backflow); walls as external (noSlip, kqRWallFunction, omegaWallFunction, nutUSpaldingWallFunction). Initial field at rest with the reference inlet's k and omega. Schemes, solution settings, the wall-resolved two-stage scheme (D-126) and the stopping criteria are the external ones.
  - Reports (I3): no force objects. The pressure function object makes p_static and p_total in Pa (rhoInf = rho_ref); per inlet and outlet sum(phi) (massFlow_<patch>), areaAverage and the mass-flux average (weightedAreaAverage with the vector weightField U: weight U . Sf, v2606 surfaceFieldValueTemplates.C) of both; per plane (regionType sampledSurface, a pointAndNormal plane, written 1e-6 of the parts' largest extent along its normal from the user's point: a plane on a layer of mesh faces is sampled with the faces of both neighbouring cells, measured in the pipe validation at x = 40 D, 368 faces and 105 % of the area) areaNormalIntegrate(U) and the same averages. Report ids `flow:<mdot|p_area|p_mass|p0_area|p0_mass>:<patch|plane>:<name>` and `dp:<name>:<static|total>` (mass-averaged upstream minus downstream), in the report slots, the asymptotic and mean-stable criteria (no default floor) and the mesh study. The mass imbalance is the net flux of every inlet and outlet over the inflow. `system/internalReports` (rho and the pressure drops; OpenFOAM ignores it) lets the monitors read a case on their own. The Reports & Monitors node shows the Internal flow table (latest values, windowed means of the mass flow and the mass-averaged total pressure, the drops) in place of the Forces table.
  - GUI: the presets "Internal flow" (3D, wall functions at the default y+ 50, surface cell 1/20 of the surface length) and "2D internal flow" (y+ 1, wall-resolved, D-122 to D-126); the New simulation dialog shows the analysis type; the tree's Fluid Region lists every patch with its role (no Fluid Domain, Inflow or Polar Sweep; Generate Domain and the wake helper disabled); a patch node edits the role and the inlet or outlet with units; "Planes & Pressure Drops" under Reports & Monitors; a multi-solid STL imported into an internal project puts each solid on its own patch.
- Check: tests/test_internal_flow.py (dictionaries per kind, the mass-flow conversion, the hydraulic diameter of a disc, a square and a 2D channel, the closed-geometry message, the meshing dictionaries, the 2D section's patches, the reports from a recorded run (tests/data/internal_run), the migration, the GUI rows); one real run (slow, 42 s): a 0.6 m pipe of water with a mass-flow inlet converges to the residual criteria with |imbalance| < 0.1 %. Validation: the turbulent pipe and the TMR backward-facing step (task w3-internal, Parts B and C; docs/validation/pipe, docs/validation/bfs). External flow unchanged: the meshing and solver dictionaries of the three public benchmark cases are byte-identical before and after (51 files, `scripts/validation/public_dicts.py`) and `--compare` (mesh only) reports no regressions.
- Alternatives considered: flowRateInletVelocity with massFlowRate and rhoInlet (equivalent for a constant density; the explicit conversion shows Q in the case); pressureDirectedInletOutletVelocity for the total-pressure inlet (needs an inflow direction the user would have to give); a generated domain around an internal geometry (the owner's decision I1: none).

## D-130 Parallel meshing decomposes the mesh only
- Status: Accepted (owner review of W3, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w3-internal, Part B).
- Context: parallel snappyHexMesh (D-111) runs `decomposePar -force` on the background mesh. Meshing a case again after a solver run (the GUI keeps the case's 0/ until a new mesh succeeds, D-051) made decomposePar decompose those 0/ fields too, whose boundary entries are those of the previous mesh: with a "background" patch in the new background mesh (a rounded domain, D-078, or internal flow, D-129) it failed ("Cannot find patchField entry for background"; found by the pipe validation's second meshing). A box domain only worked because its background patches happen to be in the fields.
- Decision: the meshing decomposition is `decomposePar -force -no-fields` (`mesh_case.MESH_DECOMPOSE_COMMAND`; v2606 option "Suppress conversion of fields"). The solver's decomposition (`foam/parallel.DECOMPOSE_COMMAND`) is unchanged.
- Check: tests/test_parallel_mesh.py (the command); the pipe validation's re-meshing (docs/validation/pipe/RESULT.md).

## D-131 A pressure-driven internal flow solves with SIMPLE and a relaxed pressure
- Status: Accepted (owner review of W3, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w3-internal, Part B; QUESTIONS 208). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the app's fvSolution (D-027) is SIMPLEC (consistent) without pressure relaxation, U 0.9, k and omega 0.7. The pipe validation's total-pressure inlet (p0 1257.8 Pa, the mass-flow run's own inlet total pressure, the same mesh) diverged from rest: the inlet mass flow passed the target (7.85 kg/s) at iteration 28 and grew without bound until the solver crashed at iteration 727.
- Decision: when an internal-flow case has a total-pressure inlet (`physics_case.fv_solution(pressure_driven=True)`), fvSolution is SIMPLE (consistent no) with relaxationFactors fields p 0.3, equations U, k, omega 0.7 (the v2606 tutorial incompressible/simpleFoam/pitzDaily's values). Every other case (velocity or mass-flow inlets, all external flow) keeps SIMPLEC (the public benchmark's dictionaries unchanged).
- Check: by hand on the pipe's case, then through the app (docs/validation/pipe/RESULT.md): converged to the mass-flow run's flow; tests/test_internal_flow.py (the dictionaries).
- Alternatives considered: a velocity initial field (the flow rate is not known); ramping p0 in time (a steady solver; more settings); SIMPLEC with a pressure relaxation (not tried).

## D-132 Results scene objects (clip, isosurface, streamlines) and saved views (schema v20)
- Status: Accepted (owner review of W3, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w3-internal, Part E; owner decision P1).
- Decision: `Simulation.results` (ResultsScene, schema v20; v19 -> v20 fills nothing) holds the scene objects and the saved views. A clip (`ClipObject`: a plane point and normal, the side kept "behind" or "front" of the normal), an isosurface (`IsoObject`: a volume field key of foam/results.FIELD_SPECS and a value) and streamlines (`StreamlinesObject`: seeds on a line, point1 to point2, or on a square plane, centre point1, normal, side size; the number of seeds; the maximum length in metres; the direction both ways, forward or backward; the colour field). Each has an id kept across renames, a name and a visibility. Their geometry comes from the latest results' internal mesh with the cell fields also as point data (`workflow/results_objects.py`, PyVista: clip, contour, streamlines_from_source with Runge-Kutta 4-5, steps of 0.2-0.5 cell lengths, at most 20,000 steps); the Results Scene draws them, clips and isosurfaces coloured by the scene's field on its colour range and scalar bar, streamlines by their own field on their own range. An object that draws nothing (a value outside the field's range, a plane or seeds outside the fluid, a field the case does not have) says why on its node. The tree's "Results" node lists the objects and the saved views; its rows add an object (starting from the results' box: a clip through its centre normal to x, an isosurface of |U| at its median, streamlines from a vertical line near the inflow side) or save the viewer's camera as a view; every edit is one undo step (`Project.add_scene_object`, `update_scene_object`, `remove_scene_object`, `add_view`, `remove_view`).
- Check: tests/test_scene_objects.py (the geometry of each object on tests/data/results_case, persistence, the v19 fixture, undo, the Results node offscreen).
- Fix (task w4-w5, Part C): with the display "Section", adding any scene object left the section actor's mapper input empty (PyVista's pipeline output, valid only until the next render), so the telemetry's field range raised KeyError '_shown' and the UI refresh failed; `ResultsView` keeps the section dataset it drew (`section_data`) and reads the values from it (tests/test_scene_objects.py).

## D-133 Image export: an offscreen render at the requested size
- Status: Accepted (owner review of W3, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w3-internal, Part F; owner decision P2).
- Decision: File > Export Image (and the Results node's "Export image") writes a PNG of the viewer at a chosen size: 1920 x 1080, 2560 x 1440, 3840 x 2160 or custom (1 to 8192 px each way), optionally with a transparent background (RGBA, the background's alpha 0), by default into <project>/images/. The scene is rendered OFFSCREEN in a new render window of that size (`gui/image_export.render_image`), not a screenshot scaled up: every visible 3D actor is copied with its own mapper instance (the same input, colours and range, so the new OpenGL context allocates its own resources), the scalar bars are copied, and the viewer's camera is copied exactly (a different aspect ratio shows more or less of the scene sideways); the viewer's 2D overlays other than the scalar bars (the telemetry card, notes) are left out. `png_bytes` gives the same render to the HTML report.
- Check: tests/test_image_export.py (the PNG's size equals the request, RGB by default; the transparent background with the walls opaque in the middle and the scalar bar's region drawn; the presets and the custom size).
- Fix (task w4-w5, Part C, found while making the manual's images): the offscreen plotter's first render replaced the copied camera with PyVista's default view (its `camera_set` was False), so every export and every report view was drawn from that default view, not the viewer's or the saved camera; `render_image` now sets `camera_set`. The scalar bars' fixed font size (`UnconstrainedFontSize`, not part of `ShallowCopy`) is copied too, so the exported bars look as in the viewer. tests/test_image_export.py checks the camera with an off-centre sphere (it failed before the fix; the earlier assertion compared the viewer's camera with itself).

## D-134 The HTML report
- Status: Accepted (owner review of W3, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w3-internal, Part G; owner decision P3).
- Decision: File > Create Report (and the Results node's "Create report") writes one self-contained HTML file to <project>/reports/<timestamp>.html (`workflow/report.py`, Qt-free; styles inline, images as base64 PNG, no external link). Sections, in order: the project and set-up (analysis, physics, reference values or, for internal flow, the boundaries table, the iteration limit and the stopping criteria); the geometry (parts, files, patches, the surface preparation and refinement volumes); the mesh report from the mesh record (cells, faces, points, checkMesh, non-orthogonality, skewness, the layer coverage table, the first cell against the plan, whether the mesh is up to date) with the walls' y+ from the run's yPlus output; the convergence (the run record's stop reason, iterations, final residuals, time and cores, whether the results are up to date, and a log-scale plot of the initial residuals parsed from log.simpleFoam with matplotlib); the forces (Cd, Cl, CmPitch, the pressure and viscous drag, of all walls and of each part) or the internal-flow reports (every location's flow quantities and the pressure drops) as windowed means: the window of a mean-stable criterion on the quantity, else the oscillation window (D-118, D-119), with the half range; the saved views, rendered offscreen at 1600 x 900 with their cameras in the Results Scene when the case has results (D-133; the viewer's camera and scene are restored). A section without data is left out with one line saying why; nothing is estimated.
- Check: tests/test_report.py (a fixture project with a mesh and results: every section, valid nesting, no external link, the images decode as PNG; a bare project: every empty section's note; an internal-flow report; the window's report with a saved view at 1600 x 900 and the camera restored).

## D-135 Wall-resolved layer phase: minFaceWeight 0.02 in the relaxed set
- Status: Accepted (owner review of w3-close, 2026-10-22). Was Proposed (task w3-close, item 2; QUESTIONS 209). [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Context: the backward-facing step's medium level (0.091 H, the finer family) kept 69 % of the step face's layers in both rounds (36 % of its faces full), the only level with a partial stack and the outlier of the study (5.63-5.64 H against 6.47 and 6.43). Diagnosis on round 2's case (meshed again serially: the same 240,295 cells and 68.8 %): snappyHexMesh's layer phase, outer iteration 0, layer iteration 0, finds 3 faces with "interpolation weights (0..1) < 0.05" (minFaceWeight, the main meshQualityControls value; D-122's relaxed set does not touch it) and removes the extrusion at 2 wall faces: the step face's top face (z = 0.95 H) and the lower wall's last face (x = -0.003 H). Both lie at the step's top, convex corner, where snapping left a sliver lower-wall face 6.3 mm long (0.07 of a 91 mm cell) beside a 146 mm one; the faces of the first layer cells in the fan around that corner have weights 0.042-0.050 (measured on the mesh made with the check relaxed). The layer count then tapers away from the unextruded points: 15 layers from the bottom of the step to z = 0.32 H, 14, 12, 10, 8, 6, 4, 0 towards the corner, and 0-8 over the last 0.5 H of the lower wall. The coarse (0.129 H) and fine (0.064 H) levels have no sliver at that corner (one 159 mm face; 60 + 90 mm faces) and no face below 0.05 (minimum 0.095 and 0.109). Cause: where the level's background grid falls against the corner (the surface level is the base cell in this preset, so only through the grid), not the corner feature edge as such nor a D-122 setting: shifting the medium grid by 30 mm in x (a case set-up change) removes the sliver and gives the step its full stack, but 8 other faces fall below 0.05 and the floor next to the step loses 1.6 % of its full faces.
- Decision: the wall-resolved controls' layer-phase relaxed set (`mesh_case.WALL_RESOLVED_RELAXED_QUALITY`, D-122) adds minFaceWeight 0.02 (main value 0.05, used while snapping and in the robust and legacy controls unchanged). The meshing hash of a project with the wall-resolved controls (chosen or automatic) includes `WALL_RESOLVED_CONTROLS_REVISION` (its earlier mesh is out of date); other projects keep their hash. checkMesh's checks and the hard / soft classes (D-100) are unchanged.
- Check: the step's three levels with the app's dictionaries (only the minFaceWeight line differs from round 2): coarse 138,024 and fine 439,456 cells, geometrically identical to round 2's meshes (the same sorted points, 0.0 m apart; only the parallel numbering differs), so their round 2 solutions stand; medium 240,423 cells, every wall 100 % full (step 15 of 15), checkMesh OK, max skewness 3.23 (round 2 3.23); minFaceWeight 0.005 gives the same mesh. The NACA 0012 round 3 15 deg mesh (wall-resolved) meshed with and without: identical (218,711 cells, 0 faces removed either way). Public benchmark (robust controls): dictionaries byte-identical (51 files), `python -m vortexcfd.bench --no-solve --compare benchmarks/baseline.json` (serial, nice 15, at 2ae8c05 plus this entry): sphere 233,534 cells, 46.46 degrees, coverage 99.59 %; half wing 300,025, 64.91, 94.37 %, trailing edge 38.36 %; Ahmed 231,606, 64.28, 96.55 %, slant edges 56.01 %; fidelity unchanged; "no regressions".
- Alternatives considered: the background grid moved for the step (a case set-up fix: it moves the defect to the floor); minFaceWeight 0.005 (the same mesh here, less margin against a really degenerate face); relaxing it for the robust controls too (D-106 unchanged by the owner's rule).
- Consequences: a wall-resolved stack no longer loses its extrusion at faces whose weight is 0.02-0.05 (the first layer cells beside a sliver face); such faces interpolate with weights down to 0.02.

## D-136 Report tables, line plots, the streamlines' scalar bar and the report's sections (schema v21)
- Status: Accepted (owner review, 2026-10-23). Was Proposed (task w4-w5, Part A).
- Decision: (1) The HTML report (D-134) gets three sections before the saved views: the mesh study (the record's levels with cells, h and stop, then per quantity the value per level, the convergence, the apparent order, the extrapolated value and the GCI fine, or why there is none: `mesh_study.quantity_rows`' note; a 3D study adds that its GCI is indicative, D-127), the polar sweep (CL, CD, CM pitch, L/D, iterations, stop and cells per angle, and a matplotlib plot of CL, CD and CM against the angle, unconverged points hollow) and the line plots. (2) A "Line plot" results scene object (`LinePlotObject`: two points, 2 to 10,000 samples, 1 to 8 volume fields): the fields interpolated from the latest results' point fields at evenly spaced points (`workflow/line_plot.py`, PyVista `sample`, NaN outside the fluid); the viewer draws the line in the accent colour (so saved views and image exports show it); the properties rows show the chart (matplotlib embedded with the Qt Agg canvas, one axes per field against the distance) with Export PNG and Export CSV; the report draws the same chart. (3) Streamlines carry their own colormap (None: the scene's), range mode (auto / fixed / symmetric) with fixed limits, and a scalar bar (on by default) on the left, titled "<object>: <field>"; the scene's bar is drawn only for the walls, section, clips and isosurfaces. (4) File > Create Report first shows a check box per section; the sections left out are saved in the project (`ResultsScene.report_omit`, one undo step) and are not in the file at all. Schema v21: v20 -> v21 fills nothing (the defaults: the scene's colormap, auto range, every section).
- Check: tests/test_report_tables.py (the real NACA 0012 round 3 study record: the CL GCI and CD viscous "oscillatory, GCI not applicable"; a sweep record of the round 3 results: the table and the plot; line plots inside and outside the fluid; omitted sections; the migration; the window's dialog and its memory); tests/test_line_plot.py (samples on tests/data/results_case, CSV and PNG, the Results node's chart and exports, the streamlines' bar with the fixed range and colormap and its image export).
- Alternatives considered: the line plot's chart in a separate window like the study results (a window per plot; the properties panel already shows the selected object); report section choice in the application settings (the owner asked for per project).

## D-137 Installation: the start-up OpenFOAM check, scripts/install.sh, the version in one place
- Status: Accepted (owner review, 2026-10-23; the owner ran scripts/install.sh in a clean WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 distribution and it worked). Was Proposed (task w4-w5, Part B).
- Decision: (1) At start-up (`foam/installation.check_openfoam`, Qt-free) the app looks for OpenFOAM v2606 in this order: the `VORTEXCFD_OPENFOAM_BASHRC` environment variable, the bashrc the user configured (`<XDG config>/VortexCFD/openfoam.json`, a plain file so `vortexcfd --check-openfoam` needs no Qt), then the standard ESI places (/usr/lib/openfoam/openfoam2606, /opt/openfoam2606, /opt/OpenFOAM/OpenFOAM-v2606, ~/OpenFOAM/OpenFOAM-v2606). A bashrc counts when it declares WM_PROJECT_VERSION=v2606 and platforms/*/bin holds simpleFoam, snappyHexMesh and blockMesh (nothing is run). The runner then sources that bashrc (`runner.set_bashrc`; the standard path is still the default). Other versions found are reported. Without it the app still opens and shows projects, meshes and results; Generate Mesh, Run Solver, the mesh study and the polar sweep are disabled with "OpenFOAM v2606 was not found ...: Help > OpenFOAM Installation." as the reason; a dialog at start-up (and Help > OpenFOAM Installation) lists what was looked for, shows the official ESI commands as text (`curl https://dl.openfoam.com/add-debian-repo.sh | sudo bash`, `sudo apt-get update`, `sudo apt-get install openfoam2606-default`; never run by the app) and takes a bashrc path, checked before it is accepted and remembered. (2) `scripts/install.sh` for a fresh WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04: checks Ubuntu 24.04 (else stops; `--ignore-os`), Python 3.12 with venv and OpenFOAM (prints the ESI commands when missing, installs the app anyway and ends with exit code 3), creates or reuses ~/.local/share/vortexcfd/venv (`--prefix`), installs the pinned runtime dependencies (`scripts/requirements-v1.txt`, the development venv's versions) and the package from the checkout, checks the imports, `vortexcfd --version` and `pip check`, copies docs/manual next to the venv, writes ~/.local/bin/vortexcfd and ~/.local/share/applications/vortexcfd.desktop, and runs `vortexcfd --check-openfoam`. It never uses sudo: a missing system package stops it with the command for the user. (3) The version is set once, `vortexcfd.__version__` = "1.0.0rc1" (pyproject.toml reads it); the About dialog and `vortexcfd --version` show it. The console script is `vortexcfd.cli:main` (`--version`, `--check-openfoam`, `--help` without Qt; otherwise the GUI as before).
- Check: tests/test_openfoam_check.py (fake installations: the standard place, another version, an incomplete one, the configured path and the environment first, the runner's bashrc, the command line without Qt, the window without OpenFOAM and with a path set later, the start-up dialog only without OpenFOAM, the dialog's path check, the About text); tests/test_install_script.py (syntax, no sudo run, --check-only on fake environments). The script run in isolation (a temporary HOME and prefix; pip's cache pointed at the user's existing cache): exit 0, `vortexcfd 1.0.0rc1`, the OpenFOAM check found /usr/lib/openfoam/openfoam2606; run again: the environment reused, exit 0. Not yet run on a second machine (owed by the owner).
- Version 1.0.0 (task release-1.0.0): `__version__` from "1.0.0rc1" to "1.0.0", nothing else changed for it.
- The user manual (task w4-w5, Part C): docs/manual/ (English Markdown, images exported by the app with `scripts/manual_images.py`); Help > User Manual opens the local README.md: the copy the install script puts in <prefix>/venv/share/vortexcfd/manual, else the checkout's docs/manual; disabled with a tooltip when neither exists.
- Alternatives considered: running OpenFOAM's `foamVersion` at start-up (a shell per launch; the file checks are enough to say where to look); QSettings for the configured path (the command line check would need Qt); an editable install (ties the installed app to the checkout's working tree).

## D-138 ParaView is optional: detection order, the disabled button's install tip, the install script's report
