- Status: Accepted (owner review of rc1-polish, 2026-10-23) with the QUESTIONS 220 change below. Was Proposed (task rc1-polish, item 1).
- Context: D-055 found only a Windows ParaView under C:\Program Files. ParaView is not part of the install and need not be on a user's machine; the owner asked that "Open in ParaView" find it where it is and say how to get it when it is not.
- Decision: (1) `gui/paraview.executable()` uses, in order: the executable set in Edit > Preferences (when that file does not exist: detection below, and the tooltip says the configured path was not found; QUESTIONS 220, task release-1.0.0. Before: none, the button off); `paraview` on PATH (`shutil.which`, the Ubuntu package); the newest /mnt/c/Program Files/ParaView*/bin/paraview.exe (D-055's rule: only that folder is listed, nothing is run, so it is safe where /mnt/c is absent or slow to list nothing). The Linux ParaView gets the case.foam path as is, a .exe gets `wslpath -w` of it. (2) Without any, the button is disabled and its tooltip says ParaView is optional, `sudo apt install paraview`, or ParaView for Windows opening the case through \\wsl$\<WSL_DISTRO_NAME>\... (case.foam, created if missing), or a path in Preferences. (3) scripts/install.sh has a "ParaView (optional)" step (also with --check-only): found on PATH, found under the Windows Program Files, or "not found: ParaView is optional" with the same two ways; it never changes the exit code. Manual chapter 1.4.
- Check: tests/test_results_scene.py (fake environments: nothing anywhere and the tip, PATH before Windows, the configured path and its fallback when missing, the Linux launch without wslpath, the window's button); tests/test_install_script.py (the three cases of the script's report).
- Alternatives considered: asking Windows for ParaView through cmd.exe or the registry (runs Windows programs from the app; slow; not "safe" detection); PATH before the configured path (a user's explicit choice should win); a missing configured path disabling the button (D-138 as first proposed; the owner chose the fallback, QUESTIONS 220).
- Note: the development machine has Ubuntu's ParaView (/usr/bin/paraview, package paraview); the tests replace PATH lookup so they do not depend on it.


## D-139 Qt: PySide6-Essentials only (pinned), not the PySide6 meta-package
- Status: Accepted (owner review of rc1-polish, 2026-10-23). Was Proposed (task rc1-polish, item 2; QUESTIONS 213).
- Context: the PySide6 6.12 meta-package installs PySide6_Addons, PySide6_WebEngine (Chromium) and PySide6_Pdf beside PySide6_Essentials.
- Finding: static scan: the sources import QtCore, QtGui and QtWidgets only; pyvistaqt, VTK's Qt support and matplotlib's Qt Agg backend use QtOpenGL, QtOpenGLWidgets and QtSvg (all in Essentials). Runtime: after the main window showed a project with results, a line plot's chart, an image export and a report (offscreen), sys.modules held QtCore, QtGui, QtOpenGL, QtOpenGLWidgets, QtSvg, QtWidgets (QtTest under pytest-qt), and QtDataVisualization (Addons) only because qtpy tries it inside suppress(ImportError) at import; in an Essentials-only install it is skipped and nothing fails. Nothing uses Qt WebEngine, Qt Pdf or any Addons module.
- Decision: pyproject.toml depends on `PySide6-Essentials==6.12.0`; scripts/requirements-v1.txt pins PySide6_Essentials==6.12.0 and shiboken6==6.12.0 and drops PySide6, PySide6_Addons, PySide6_Pdf, PySide6_WebEngine. THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md follows.
- Check: tests/test_qt_modules.py (the static scan; the window's runtime modules within Essentials; the GUI imported and a QtInteractor created with every non-Essentials PySide6 module unimportable). scripts/install.sh run in isolation (a temporary HOME and prefix, pip's cache the user's): before 1870 MB (PySide6 673 MB), after 1442 MB (-428 MB); exit 0 both; the Essentials-only install opened the window with results, a line plot, an image export and a report offscreen.
- Note: the development .venv still has the meta-package installed (nothing removed from it); it satisfies the new requirement.

## D-140 The 2D view convention: look along +y, flow left to right [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Status: Accepted (owner review of rc1-polish, 2026-10-23). Was Proposed (task rc1-polish, item 6). Tagged because it changes a default (the viewer's camera), not the numerics.
- Context: a 2D section (D-121) lies in the x-z plane with the free stream along +x. Looking along -y mirrors x (the flow runs right to left and a C-type domain's round inlet appears on the right). Before this decision a 2D project kept the viewer's start-up isometric view from (+1, +1, +1) in the geometry scene, which looks partly along -y (+x pointing left), and the results scene's upstream ISO view (oblique); the mesh scene's section camera already looked along +y. Image export and the report's views copy the viewer's camera exactly (D-133), so they follow the viewer.
- Decision: for a 2D project the default camera is PyVista's XZ view (camera on the -y side looking along +y, +z up; the top bar's XZ, now shown active): when a 2D project's parts are first shown or the analysis is set to 2D (`_sync_scene`), on every switch to the geometry or results scene, and in the mesh scene when no section plane is shown (the section camera, along +y for the default y-normal section, is unchanged). A user's rotation is kept on later edits; 3D projects are unchanged (the start-up ISO view, the upstream ISO results view).
- Check: tests/test_view_2d.py (the camera along +y with +z up in the geometry and results scenes and after switching to 2D; a red marker at the scene's +x end lands in the right half of the exported image, and in the left half for the mirrored view; 3D unchanged). 4 of its 5 tests failed before the change.
- Note (item 5 of the task): the NACA 0012 round 3 case is not reversed: its constant/triSurface/domain.stl, snappyHexMeshDict regions and polyMesh/boundary put the round half-disc inlet at x < 0 (299 faces, face centres x -99.48 to -0.06) and the flat outlet at x = +100.97 (149 faces), with U = (90 0 0); the D shape with a flat face on the left seen in an offscreen render is the view along -y. D-078 holds; no fix.

## D-141 Settings isolation for tests and scripts; the Recent list skips temporary folders and is pruned
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part A).
- Context: the owner's Recent list held /tmp paths from tests and agents' scratch scripts (/tmp/tmp.../proj/p.json, /tmp/claude-1000/...). tests/conftest.py redirected QSettings in-process only, so a subprocess started by a test, or a script that forgot its own redirection, wrote to the user's real settings.
- Decision: (1) `core/user_dirs.py` (Qt-free): the environment variable `VORTEXCFD_SETTINGS_DIR`; when set, `gui/settings_store.settings()` points every QSettings format and scope at that folder, `config_dir()` (the presets) is `<folder>/VortexCFD` and the OpenFOAM path file (`foam/installation.config_path`) lives there too. `isolate_settings()` sets it to a fresh temporary folder unless already set (a child process keeps its parent's). (2) tests/conftest.py sets it for every test (the same folder as XDG_CONFIG_HOME and the QSettings redirection), so subprocesses inherit it; the benchmark (`vortexcfd.bench.cli.main`), every scripts/validation driver, scripts/manual_images.py and scripts/screenshots.py call `isolate_settings()` before anything else. (3) The Recent list never records a project inside a system temporary folder (`tempfile.gettempdir()`, /tmp, /var/tmp; resolved paths too): `RecentProjects.add` returns False and stores nothing. (4) At start-up (`main_window.start_window`) `RecentProjects.prune()` drops the entries whose file no longer exists and those inside temporary folders; at most 10 entries are kept (as before). File > Open Recent lists only existing projects (D-077 greyed missing ones with "(not found)").
- Check: tests/test_settings_isolation.py (the isolation in every test, a subprocess inheriting it, the scripts calling it, temporary folders detected and refused, the start-up prune, ten entries). Tests that need a temporary project in Recent use the `allow_temporary_recent` fixture.
- Alternatives considered: a separate application name for tests (scripts started outside pytest would still hit the real settings); keeping missing entries greyed (the owner asked for them to be pruned).

## D-142 The welcome screen: two large buttons and an open panel with the recent projects
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part B). Amends the Start dialog of D-077.
- Context: the owner found the Start dialog hard to use: small buttons, a recent list of "name -- full path" lines with low contrast, a separate Open button that duplicated "Open project..." and a "Remove from list" button.
- Decision: the Start dialog is a welcome screen with two pages. Page 1: the app name and version, and two large primary buttons (accent-filled tiles, each with a one-line description): "New simulation" and "Open project". "Open project" shows page 2, the open panel: "Back", the title, "Browse..." (any .vcfd file: the window's file dialog, as File > Open), and the recent projects list (elevated background with a border, so it stands out from the dialog): per entry the project name in bold with the last-opened date on the right and the folder dimmed and elided in the middle. Missing projects are not listed (D-141). A double-click (or Enter) opens a project; the right-click menu offers "Open" and "Remove from list" (the list only; nothing on disk). There is no other Open or Remove button. The last-opened date is recorded by `RecentProjects.add` (QSettings key recent/opened, JSON); entries recorded before show the project file's modification time.
- Check: tests/test_start_screen.py (the two buttons and no duplicates, the panel listing only existing projects with a date, double-click, Browse, the right-click menu's Open and Remove). Screenshots: run/gui_review/B_start/.
- Alternatives considered: the recent list on the welcome page itself (two "open" paths again); a separate window for Browse (the File > Open dialog already exists).

## D-143 The New simulation dialog: a preset list with descriptions, three sections, inline validation
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part C). Amends the New simulation dialog of D-077 and D-121.
- Decision: the dialog has three titled sections with right-aligned labels centred on their fields. Project: the name and the parent folder (as before) and a dimmed one-line "Creates <folder>/" (elided in the middle). Analysis: the presets as a LIST (not a drop-down): each entry its name in bold over a one-line description (built-in: a fixed text, `core/presets.BUILTIN_SUMMARIES`; user presets: "User preset: ..." from the analysis type, dimension and domain shape, `preset_summary`); the selected preset's full description in a box below the list (`preset_description`: what the analysis generates, the dimension, the domain shape and sizes in L, the mesh's background cells, surface size and layer sizing); the separate "Analysis type" row is gone. The dimension is one segmented row (3D / 2D section (x-z plane)) set from the preset. Solver: "Run in parallel" and a spin box shown as "N / <physical cores>"; the words "physical cores" are only in the tooltip. Validation is inline: the field the problem concerns (the name, or the parent folder when it is empty or not a folder) gets the `invalid` border (the error token) and the reason as its tooltip; Create is disabled with the same tooltip; there is no footer message. An empty parent folder is now refused ("Choose the parent folder."; before, it meant the working directory). Save As (duplicate project, D-079) uses the same inline check.
- Check: tests/test_start_screen.py (the list and its descriptions, the description box, "N / 8" with "physical cores" in the tooltip only, the inline name and parent errors with no footer label, the sections, no combo box, the dimension from the preset). Screenshots: run/gui_review/C_new_simulation/.

## D-144 The Zinc & Teal theme (replaces Tungsten & Amber)
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part D; the palette is the owner's decision).
- Decision: owner values: background #18181B (`canvas`: the viewer and the gaps between panels), panel #27272A (`base`: every panel, the top bar, dialogs), accent #2DD4BF (teal), secondary #F59E0B (amber), primary text #FAFAFA. Chosen here (zinc steps and the accents' lighter steps): `elevated` #1C1C20 (inputs, inset darker than the panel; row hover), `floating` #303036 (menus, tooltips), `border` #3F3F46, `border_active` #52525B, `muted_text` #A1A1AA, `disabled_text` #71717A (disabled controls and placeholders only), `accent_hover` #5EEAD4, `accent_secondary_hover` #FBBF24, `accent_text` #18181B (dark text on teal), `success` #22C55E, `warning` = the secondary #F59E0B, `error` #FB7185, `part_surface` #9F9FA9 (neutral), `part_edge` #3F3F46; the axis colours are unchanged. Usage: the accent is selection and primary action: the selected tree row is a low-alpha teal fill (`selection` = the accent at 0.15 over the panel) with a teal marker and teal text; Generate Mesh and Run Solver are filled teal with dark text; the active scene tab, checked view buttons, segmented buttons, toggles, sliders, focus borders, menu highlight, the selected part and the selected domain face are teal. The secondary (amber) is for highlights and warnings only: warnings, the unsaved dot, the telemetry title, the y+ histogram, the residual chart's p. The viewer background is the background colour; the ground grid uses the neutral border tokens. The residual chart's weights over the new canvas are recomputed with the same OKLCH L <= 0.66 rule (D-049): p amber 0.78, U blue 0.80, k / omega green 0.82 (p moved from the accent to the secondary: teal is too close to the green of k).
- WCAG contrast (tests/test_theme.py, every pair >= 4.5): text on canvas 16.97, on base 14.27, on elevated 16.27, on floating 12.56, on selection 10.48; muted_text on canvas 6.91, on base 5.81, on elevated 6.63, on floating 5.12; accent_text on accent 9.52, on accent_hover 11.98; on base: success 6.54, warning / secondary 6.94, error 5.53, accent 8.00, accent_hover 10.07, secondary hover 8.92; on canvas: success 7.78, warning 8.25, error 6.58, accent 9.52; accent on selection 5.87, on elevated 9.12; error on elevated 6.31.
- Check: tests/test_theme.py (the owner values, the contrast pairs, no colour literals outside theme.py); tests that name a token follow the new roles. Screenshots: run/gui_review/theme/.

## D-145 Tree context menus and keys; refinement regions from imported geometry (schema v22) [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part E). Tagged: the snappyHexMeshDict gets a new kind of refinementRegions entry; no default changes, and projects without imported regions write byte-identical dictionaries.
- Decision (menus and keys, `gui/tree_actions.py`): every tree node has a right-click menu (`MainWindow.tree_menu_for`): Geometry / Parts: Import Geometry, Import Airfoil, Show all, Hide all, Prepare Surfaces; a part: Hide/Show, Isolate, Rename, Delete, Use as refinement region, Repair, Properties; Surface Preparation: Prepare Surfaces, Remove preparation; Mesh: Generate mesh, Clear mesh (deletes constant/polyMesh, the mesh record and the solver results after a confirmation; `foam/mesh_case.clear_mesh`), Mesh report (the checkMesh summary as text), the refinement volume additions; Refinement volumes: Add (by shape), Add from imported geometry, Add Wake Volume; a volume: Enable/Disable, Rename, Duplicate, Delete, Properties; Fluid Domain: Generate Domain, Edit; a domain face: Boundary type (editable from D-147 on); Results: Add results object, Save current view, Export Image, Create Report (and Open in ParaView, Part K); a results object: Show/Hide, Rename, Delete, Properties; a saved view: Apply, Delete; the studies: Run, Properties; any other node: Properties. Delete (tree focused) removes the selected part, volume, results object or saved view after a yes/no confirmation (before, Delete removed a part at once and did nothing else); F2 focuses and selects the node's name field; a double-click selects the node and brings its properties forward.
- Decision (imported regions): `SurfaceShape` (schema v22: `file` relative to the project, `units`, `mode` "inside" or "distance", `distance` in metres) is a fourth refinement volume shape. "Add from imported geometry" copies a STL / OBJ / PLY (units asked) or STEP / IGES / BREP (tessellated on the geometry worker like a CAD part) into geometry/; "Use as refinement region" turns a part into such a volume in one undo step (the part is removed, so it is no longer a wall; its file stays). The surface must be closed (`workflow/refinement.load_region`; an inward-oriented surface is flipped on a copy); the default target is the part surface cell size; distance mode starts at one target cell. Meshing writes the surface (turned with the parts at an angle of attack for a body-frame volume, D-120) to constant/triSurface/volume_<name>.stl, the geometry entry `volume_<name>.stl { type triSurfaceMesh; name volume_<name>; }` and the refinementRegions entry `volume_<name> { mode inside; levels ((1e15 level)); }` or `{ mode distance; levels ((d level)); }` (2D: directional as D-121). It is never a refinementSurfaces entry, never in surfaceFeatureExtract and never a patch. The estimate's staircase (D-103) works on the loaded surface: the region's own volume in inside mode, otherwise sampled on a 40^3 grid over its bounding box (signed distance to the surface), grown by the buffers; the share inside the domain sampled as for the other shapes. The viewer draws the surface like the other volumes. The mesh hash adds the region file's content hash; a missing or open region file blocks meshing with its reason.
- Check: tests/test_surface_regions.py (model and migration, part to region and undo, containment, sampled volumes against the exact Minkowski sum within 4 %, distance shells, inward and open surfaces, the estimate, the snappyHexMeshDict entries in both modes, the written STL and its removal, the rotation, the hash and readiness; ONE real mesh, marked slow: a 1 m sphere with an imported box STL behind it, inside mode, 20,000 background cells: the cells well inside the box have the volume of the region's level within 2 %, checkMesh OK, no volume_* patch; 38 s); tests/test_tree_menus.py (a menu for every node, the required actions, show / hide / isolate, Delete with confirmation, F2, double-click, the region from a part and from a file, an open file refused, Clear mesh and Mesh report). Screenshots: run/gui_review/E_tree/.
- Alternatives considered: keeping the part and flagging it (every part list, patch name, force group and the readiness would need to skip it); a STEP region only through a prior part import (two steps for the user).

## D-146 Surface preparation: one action, explained
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part F). Amends the GUI of D-093.
- Context: the tree had the "Surface Preparation" node and, under it, a "Prepare surfaces..." entry that did the same as Edit > Prepare Surfaces and the node's button; nothing said what the operation is for.
- Decision: one action, `act_prepare_surfaces`, now "Surface Preparation...": in the Edit menu and in the context menus of Geometry, Parts and the Surface Preparation node (the node's "Run..." button calls it too). The "Prepare surfaces..." tree entry is gone. The node's tooltip, its properties and the strategy dialog explain it (`workflow/surface_prep.EXPLANATION`): it makes several parts mesh as one clean outer surface; it is needed when parts cross or touch (a wing through a fuselage, a mirror on a body) or come from CAD that was not sewn; a single closed part does not need it; run it after the parts change. The dialog (`gui/dialogs.SurfacePrepDialog`) shows each strategy with what it does (`STRATEGY_HELP`): intersection features (parts kept, crossing curves as feature edges at the finest surface level; fast; buried walls stay) and CAD union (sewn and fused with OpenCASCADE, buried walls removed, faces keep their part and patch; needs CAD or clean closed surfaces; falls back per part; the default). No behaviour of the operation changed.
- Check: tests/test_surface_prep.py (no tree entry, the node's tooltip, the dialog's texts and choice); tests/test_layout.py (one node fewer). Screenshots: run/gui_review/F_surface_prep/.

## D-148 The properties panel: interface size, compact rows, collapsible sections, help, detachable; units and explanations
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part G; built up over the Part G commits, each listed below).
- Context: the owner found the panel hard to read: tall rows, large empty boxes, unexplained terms and values without units.
- Decision (G1-G2, the panel itself): View > Interface size: Small (9 pt, the new default), Medium (10 pt), Large (12 pt), applied to the application font and every size derived from it (rows, fields, tree rows, headers); remembered (the existing appearance/point_size key; the zoom actions still step the size). Inputs and tree rows are 1.6 u high (were 2 u and 1.8 u) and the property rows have 0.25 u padding (was 0.5 u). Every section of the panel is a header with a chevron that collapses its rows; the collapsed sections are remembered per node type (QSettings properties/collapsed/<kind>). Section headers covered by the manual carry a "?" that opens docs/manual/09_properties_reference.md at the matching heading (`gui/help_texts.py`). The "Checks" and "Last mesh" titles are such sections too. The panel can be detached into its own window (the button at the right of the Properties header) and docked back (the same button, or closing the window); it keeps following the selection. Field widgets (`gui/panel_widgets.py`): `AutoField` (an "Auto" checkbox; checked, the computed value in use is shown read-only and greyed), `VectorField` (one "(x, y, z)" field with a unit, pasted text accepted, inline error), `ReferenceLengthField` (a length stored in multiples of L, entered in L, m or mm, the other form shown live).
- Decision (G3, Mesh): every Mesh field has its unit and a tooltip saying what it does, its default and why (`MESH_SETTINGS`). The optional values (background cell size, surface levels min and max, meshing cores) are `AutoField`s showing the computed value while Auto is checked; the "Base size used", "Levels used" and "Cores used" rows are gone (they repeated those values; the cores' recommendation is in the field's tooltip). Surface cell size: the suffix names its length (" x l_ref" or " x L") and a line under it shows the size in metres and the length with its value ("= 0.005 m (l_ref = 0.5 m)"), updated live while typing; the Estimate's "Surface cell (achieved)" gives the size at the level used. Curvature refinement ("Curvature: extra levels"): explained under the row, its tooltip states the cost (up to 4 x surface, 8 x volume cells per level), committed on every change so the estimate follows live, and editable up to 4 (MAX_CURVATURE_LEVELS was 2 in D-085; the default stays 2) [NUMERICS-REVIEW]. The layer section is "Prism layers (walls)" with an explanation; the sizing is a choice between "Sized from a target y+" and "Thickness relative to the surface cell size" (the model values absolute_yplus / relative are unchanged); the computed first-layer height, layer count, expansion used and total / final thickness are shown with units for both sizings (relative: from the final-layer fraction of the surface cell, marked approximate); the final-layer thickness is disabled with y+ sizing (it is not used there).
- Decision (G4-G5, free stream and body attitude): "Inflow" is "Free stream" in the tree, the undo labels ("Edit free stream (...)"), the reference speed's note and the report (the model section keeps its name, `Simulation.inflow`, so no schema change). Its rows: "Free-stream speed" (m/s), "Turbulence intensity" entered and shown in percent (the model keeps the fraction), "Viscosity ratio" (\u03bd\u209c/\u03bd), each with a tooltip (what, default, why); the Physics and Solvers rows got tooltips and units too (iterations "it.", cores "N / physical"). The angle of attack left the free stream: it is the "Body attitude" group of the Geometry (and Parts) node, external flow only: "Angle of attack \u03b1: rotation of the parts about the y axis, nose up positive, relative to the free stream", with the rotation centre in use shown below it (the Reference Values' CofR, or auto); its undo label is "Edit body attitude (alpha_deg)".
- Decision (G6, domain size): the Size section starts with the reference length L in use and its definition ("1 m: the parts' largest extent"; its tooltip: the largest extent of the box around the parts the domain is sized around). Each extent (upstream, downstream, lateral, vertical, radius) is a `ReferenceLengthField`: entered in L, m or mm (a button cycles the unit; the choice is remembered per extent, a display preference), the other form shown live under it ("= 5 m" or "= 5 L"). The model keeps every extent in multiples of L (D-078): a value entered in metres is converted with the current L, so the domain still follows the parts when they change (QUESTIONS 226). The computed dimensions are "Dimensions x \u00d7 y \u00d7 z" in metres. Each extent has a tooltip with what it measures and its default.
- Decision (G8-G11): Probes: the location is one `VectorField` "(x, y, z)" with the unit m, accepting pasted "1.2 0 -0.5", "(1.2, 0, -0.5)", commas, semicolons, tabs or brackets; bad text shows on the field (the invalid border and the reason as its tooltip) and is not committed (no status-bar message). Reference Values: the area, l_ref and the CofR show the values in use while automatic (the area follows its basis: editable only when custom; l_ref an `AutoField`; the CofR a `VectorField` with an Auto box); the "Used" rows that repeated them are gone and where an automatic value comes from is in the field's tooltip. Part surface levels are `AutoField`s showing the part's levels in use. The "Mesh Study" is the "Mesh independence study" (tree, section, Run button) with an explanation (three meshes, the ratio r and the cells it costs, what the GCI is and when it applies, the 3D GCI indicative) and a tooltip on every value. The polar sweep is opt-in (`PolarSweepSettings.enabled`, schema v22): off for new projects ("Polar sweep (off)" in the tree, only its explanation, "for lifting bodies", and an "Enable the polar sweep" box shown; it cannot run while off), on for every project saved before v22 that has its studies. Stopping rows have their units (it., %). Every visible input row of every node has a tooltip (a test sweeps them).
- Alternatives considered: a QDockWidget (the left column is part of the central widget under the top bar; a dock area would sit beside the top bar and change the window layout the owner accepted in D-041); per-row "?" buttons (one per section is enough and keeps the rows compact); storing an extent in metres when entered in metres (a schema change; the domain would no longer follow the parts); hiding the polar sweep node while off (the user would not find where to enable it).

## D-147 Editable boundary types of the domain faces (schema v22) [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part G7). Tagged: it changes the patch types and boundary conditions written for a face whose type the user changes; the defaults write byte-identical dictionaries (proved on the public benchmark cases, task report).
- Context: every domain face had a fixed type (D-019, D-078): a velocity inlet, a pressure outlet and slip far-field faces; only the box's bottom face could become the ground plane (D-116). The owner asked for every face to be editable, e.g. for ground-effect studies on any face.
- Decision: `Domain.boundary_types` (schema v22) maps a face (inlet, outlet, sides, top, bottom, farfield) to one of: velocity_inlet "Velocity inlet", pressure_outlet "Pressure outlet", slip "Slip (far field)", symmetry, wall "Wall" (no-slip, stationary), moving_wall "Moving wall" (with the free stream). Defaults (`DEFAULT_FACE_KINDS`) are dropped on validation, so an unchanged domain stores nothing, writes the dictionaries it always wrote and keeps its mesh and results hashes. The symmetry plane (Domain.symmetry) and the 2D front and back are not editable; the ground plane stays the D-116 option, offered as a type of the box's bottom face ("Ground plane") and shown as the type of the ground face (choosing another type there turns it off and gives the bottom face that type). Written conditions (`foam/physics_case._field`, `patch_kinds`): a face takes the conditions of its type exactly as the default faces of that type have them (velocity inlet: U fixedValue (U, 0, 0), p zeroGradient, k and omega fixedValue; pressure outlet: p fixedValue 0, U, k, omega inletOutlet; slip; symmetry: symmetryPlane on the box's flat faces, symmetry on a rounded domain's; wall: the parts' wall functions, U noSlip; moving wall: U fixedValue (U, 0, 0), the ground's moving condition). Patch types (`foam/mesh_case.FACE_KIND_PATCH_TYPES`, blockMeshDict and a rounded domain's domain.stl regions): wall for both walls (wall functions need it), symmetryPlane / symmetry, patch otherwise. Domain walls get no prism layers. The mesh hash adds the faces' patch-type class (so wall to moving wall keeps the mesh), the results hash the types. Run Solver needs at least one velocity inlet and one pressure outlet (`workflow/meshing.boundary_problem`). Presets carry the types too (`PresetDomain.boundary_types`; none in the built-ins).
- GUI: a face node has a "Boundary condition" section with the type (a combo) and the conditions written; the tree names a changed face's type ("top (wall)"); the face's context menu offers the same types (D-145).
- Check: tests/test_boundary_types.py (validation and normalisation, the edit and the ground option, the default dictionaries identical to an explicit default set, wall / symmetry / moving faces in blockMeshDict and 0/, inlet and outlet moved, a bullet's symmetry far field, readiness, hashes, the GUI). Public benchmark dictionaries byte-identical to main (scripts/validation/public_dicts.py, diff -r).
- Alternatives considered: one type per role (D-077's per-role preset types; the owner asked for faces); a symmetryPlane on rounded faces (OpenFOAM requires a planar patch for it).

## D-149 Results snapshots and the Compare tab
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part I; a user's request, Carla).
- Context: a new mesh or run deletes the previous results (D-051), so runs could not be compared.
- Decision: "Save results snapshot" (Compare tab) writes <project>/snapshots/<name>.json (`workflow/snapshots.py`, format 1; the folder gets a .gitignore with "*": data, not sources): the residual history (decimated as the live chart, at most 2000 points per field), every report the run wrote (`core/reports.available_reports`) with its history, final value and windowed mean (the mean-stable criterion's window, else the oscillation window, as the Forces table, D-118), the line plots' samples the Results Scene computed (none before the results were shown), the mesh summary (cells, faces, points, the worst non-orthogonality and skewness, failed checks, layers) and the set-up summary (the key inputs flattened to text: analysis, parts, physics, free stream, domain, mesh, refinement volumes, solver, reference values, internal-flow boundaries). "Load from project" adds the snapshots of another project read-only. The Compare tab (a fourth tab beside the scenes; the viewer's place shows it) lists the current run, this project's snapshots and the loaded ones with check boxes; with two or more ticked (the first is the reference) it shows: Plots (the residuals, any report's history or any line plot field overlaid, one colour per snapshot, the legend naming them; PNG export), Values (each quantity's windowed mean, else its final value, and the mesh cells, per snapshot, with the absolute and relative differences to the reference; CSV export) and Set-up differences (only the inputs that differ; CSV export). File > Create Report adds a "Comparison" section (the values with their differences, the set-up differences, the residual and first report overlays) when two or more runs are ticked; it is a report section the user can leave out (REPORT_SECTIONS "comparison", no schema change: report_omit accepts it).
- Also: the report's and the study window's "Mesh study" is "Mesh independence study" (D-148).
- Check: tests/test_snapshots.py (capture on the fixture case of tests/data with a line plot, save / load / delete and the .gitignore, another project's read-only snapshots, the difference tables on two snapshots with altered values, the overlays' legends, the PNG and CSV exports, the report section only with two runs, the Compare tab in the window). No OpenFOAM run.
- Alternatives considered: keeping whole result folders per run (hundreds of MB per snapshot); a snapshot per run automatically (the user chooses what is worth keeping).

## D-150 Show in OpenFOAM file
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part J; a user's request, Carla).
- Decision: every properties row whose value the app writes to the case (`workflow/foam_entries.PROPERTY_FIELDS`: the Mesh settings, physics, free stream, body attitude, solver, domain extents and symmetry, reference values, the ground's settings; a domain face's boundary type; the selected refinement volume's target size and enabled flag) has a right-click menu with "Show in OpenFOAM file". The map from a property to its entries is found with the writers themselves, not written by hand: the project is copied, that property alone is changed to another valid value (a number doubled or +1, a choice to the next option, a flag flipped, an automatic value set), both case file sets are built with foam/mesh_case.build_mesh_files and foam/physics_case.build_solver_files, and the keyword paths whose values differ are the property's entries (a path only the changed files have is reported at the part the current files have); the first one shown is the most specific (a short table of preferred entries picks among the found ones, e.g. 0/U internalField for the free-stream speed). A property that changes nothing written with the current settings (e.g. the radius of a box domain) says so. The viewer is read-only: a choice of the entries, the file's text with the entry's line selected and highlighted (located by its nested keyword names), "Open in external editor", and the warning that the app writes these files again on Generate Mesh and Run Solver, so manual edits are overwritten. When the case has no such file yet, the dictionaries are written to <project>/case_preview/ (no meshing, no solving, the case untouched) and the viewer says so and how the real ones are written.
- Check: tests/test_foam_entries.py (mesh, physics, free stream, body attitude, boundary types, solver, reference values, domain, ground and refinement volume properties all mapped and every entry present in a case written by prepare_job and the solver writer; specific entries; the line located; the viewer scrolled to and highlighting the entry, Open in external editor; the not-yet-written case previewed without touching case/; the row menus and the window).
- Alternatives considered: a hand-written map (it would drift from the writers); writing the case itself when missing (prepare_job removes a mesh and Run Solver's writer removes results: never as a side effect of looking).

## D-151 Open in ParaView in the top bar and the Results menu
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, Part K). Amends where D-055 / D-138 put the button.
- Decision: "Open in ParaView" is a top bar button beside the scene controls (after Wireframe) and an action of the Results node's context menu (`act_paraview`, the same action); the Results Scene's strip no longer has its own. It finds ParaView as D-138 does (looked for once, and again after Edit > Preferences: listing the Windows Program Files can be slow under WSL); without one it is disabled with D-138's install tooltip. It opens the current case (case.foam written if missing) as soon as the case has a mesh, not only once results exist; without a mesh it is disabled with "Generate the mesh first". The Results node's action row wraps in a narrow panel.
- Check: tests/test_results_scene.py (the button in the top bar next to Wireframe and in the Results menu, disabled with the install tip when not found, enabled after Preferences with a meshed case, both launching the case; disabled without a mesh; case.foam written by the launch).

## D-152 Results selectors apply after their popup has closed
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up item 10; the owner's report: after choosing a colormap the selector stopped responding until the scene was left and entered again, on the real display).
- Finding: offscreen the selectors never stopped responding (the colormap, field, range and display selectors and the results objects' selectors, each changed many times through their popups; the lookup tables followed every change; no selector was rebuilt, disconnected or left with its signals blocked; no widget kept a mouse grab). What the real display adds is the native OpenGL viewer under the strip: every choice ran the whole redraw (actors removed and added, a new scalar bar, a synchronous OpenGL render) inside the selector's own signal, that is while its popup (a separate window that overlaps the viewer) was still closing. Leaving and entering the scene hides and shows the strip and renders again, which is what cleared the state. The cause is therefore attributed to that synchronous redraw during the popup's close; it could not be reproduced without the real display, which agents do not use.
- Decision: the Results Scene strip's selectors (field, colormap, range, its limits, display, separation) only start a zero-delay single-shot timer; the redraw runs from the event loop once the popup has closed, and several changes in a row are coalesced into one redraw. The results objects' selectors in the properties (colormap, range, direction, seed, field ...) commit the same way. No selector is rebuilt on refresh (the field list is refilled only when the fields change, with its signals blocked and unblocked around it).
- Check: tests/test_results_scene.py (every strip selector and the streamlines' selectors chosen several times in a row through their popups: each choice applied, the selector enabled, visible, not blocked and the same widget, no mouse grabber left, the lookup table following the colormap). To confirm on the real display (QUESTIONS 236).

## D-153 Text roles: the only text sizes
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up item 2).
- Decision: gui/theme.py defines the text roles (`TEXT_ROLES`): display (2.4 x the interface point size), page title and tile title (1.3), section title (0.85, bold, letter-spaced), subsection title (0.77, bold), label and body (1.0), strong (1.0 bold), caption (0.85), the list item's title (1.0 bold) and subtitle (1.0), and the monospace value (0.9), control (0.85), badge (0.8), data (0.8), small data (0.75), metric (1.5 bold) and code (1.0) roles. They keep the sizes the widgets had, so the look does not change, except the status bar (now the caption role, 0.85 instead of 0.9) and the y+ histogram labels (data role kept at 0.75 as small data). Every widget takes its font with `set_text_role(widget, role)` (painters with `role_font(role)`, the charts' notes with `role_point_size(role)`); the stylesheet's sizes come from the same roles. No font size, weight or QFont is set outside theme.py.
- Also (a bug found by the new test): after an interface size change, some text kept the previous size until a restart, because Qt does not pass a spin box's font to its line edit and because Qt's stylesheet re-polish (after a state change such as a warning colour) puts back the font a widget had when first polished. The window now re-applies every widget's role and the spin boxes' line edits after a size change, and `repolish()` re-applies the role after a state change.
- Check: tests/test_typography.py (a static scan of gui/*.py for font sizes, weights and QFont outside theme.py, and that it catches them; the roles' sizes at each interface size and the stylesheet's sizes all role sizes; every widget of the main window, the start screen and the New simulation dialog at each interface size uses a role size; a state change after a size change keeps the role).

## D-154 New simulation: sizes and a default name
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up item 1). Amends D-143.
- Decision: the dialog is one grid (the labels share a column). Its default size fits the content: every preset of the list (up to six; more scroll) and the selected preset's description, which sits directly below the list, word-wrapped, top-left aligned and exactly as tall as its text at its width. The preset list is the only part that gives way when the dialog is made short: it scrolls, down to two rows; the labels, Dimension, Solver and the buttons keep their size and stay visible. A tall dialog's spare height goes above the buttons, not into an empty list. The minimum width is 32 font heights.
- Decision: the project name is pre-filled with the first of "simulation_1", "simulation_2" ... that does not exist in the parent folder, and follows the parent folder for as long as the user has not typed a name of their own. Why: an empty name made Create disabled on opening for no visible reason, and "wing_study" as a placeholder suggested a name that was not there; a free default lets Create work at once, and never collides with an existing folder. The disabled Create button's tooltip gives the reason (the same as the field's).
- Check: tests/test_start_screen.py (the unique default name, following the parent and kept once typed; the disabled Create's tooltip; the default size showing every preset and the fitted description directly below the list; at the minimum height only the list shrinking, to two rows, with the other rows unchanged and inside the dialog; a tall dialog not stretching the list). Screenshots: run/gui_review/fix/new_sim/ (minimum, default and 1920x1080 at Small, Medium and Large; scripts/gui_review_sizes.py).

## D-155 The top bar never squeezes its contents; the main window's minimum size
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up item 11; item 4's layout checks start here).
- Finding: the status badge's height came from a squeezed layout row (20 px at Small for 13 px of text and 10 px of padding and border), so descenders were cut; and with both out-of-date badges shown, the top bar needed 1257 px at Small and squeezed every control. The main window's minimum size was 975 x 142 px: the splitters do not pass their regions' minimum heights on.
- Decision: a badge's height is fixed from the badge text role's font metrics (ascent and descent) plus its stylesheet padding and border; badges are never squeezed. When the top bar is narrower than its contents it compacts in steps, the least needed: the out-of-date badges show short texts ("Mesh", "Results"; "Mesh invalid" for an error), then only their coloured dot, then "ParaView" for "Open in ParaView", then the brand text is hidden (the logo stays), and last the project name is elided in the middle (never below 4 font heights); a compacted item's full text is its tooltip. The main window's minimum size is 66 x 36 font heights (`MIN_WINDOW_U`), where the most compact top bar fits at every interface size.
- Check: tests/test_screen_layouts.py (every state text of the status badge, and the out-of-date badges, at Small, Medium and Large, at the minimum window width: the text's full height inside the badge, no label clipped in the top bar, the bar's contents within its width; full texts again when wide), with tests/layout_check.py (overlapping widgets and clipped labels).

## D-156 The residual chart and the convergence criteria in their own windows
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up item 9). Reuses D-148's detached panel (FloatingPanel).
- Decision: the residual chart (with the turbulence stage line) and the convergence criteria panel each have a ↗ button in their header (the criteria now have their own "Convergence criteria" header) that opens them in a window of their own, and docks them back on a second click (the tooltip says which). The same widgets move, so the live updates go on while detached, during a run too; closing the window docks it. A detached criteria window shows even with no criteria, saying so. Each window's state (docked or detached) and geometry are remembered per user (QSettings "monitors/windows/<key>/detached", "geometry" and "frame"); at the next start a detached window opens where it was, unless its saved place is on no screen now (the title bar strip outside every screen's available area), when it stays docked. On exit the windows are remembered and then closed, and a detached properties window is docked, so no window of its own keeps the application open. The windows take the application's theme (the panel background) and follow the interface size (their widgets' text roles are re-applied with the rest).
- Check: tests/test_monitor_windows.py (detach and dock with the buttons and their tooltips, closing docks, the empty criteria window; the chart and criteria updated while detached during a fake run; the interface size followed; detached state and size restored after a restart, docked after docking; a saved place off every screen opens docked).

## D-157 Domain extents in m or mm are stored in that unit
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up item 5; QUESTIONS 226). Replaces D-148's remembered display unit.
- Decision: an extent of the far-field domain (upstream, downstream, lateral, vertical, radius) is stored with its unit: `Domain.units` maps an extent to "m" or "mm" and its value is then in that unit, a fixed length when L (the parts' largest extent) changes; an extent without an entry is in multiples of L and follows the parts as before ("L" entries are dropped). The geometry reads every extent through `Domain.extent_m`. In the properties the unit button converts the value to the same length in the next unit and commits value and unit together (one undo step); without parts there is no L to convert with, so the button is disabled with a tooltip saying why. The display unit remembered per user in QSettings ("properties/domain_units/<extent>") is no longer read: the unit belongs to the project. Presets carry the units (`PresetDomain.units`), and their descriptions and the snapshots' set-up rows name each extent's unit ("5 L", "2 m").
- Migration: schema v23. A v22 project has every extent in L (the old field stored L whatever unit was shown), so the migration fills nothing; an empty `units` is left out of the mesh hash, so existing meshes stay up to date.
- Check: tests/test_domain_units.py (m, mm and L extents and their metres; validation; a length in metres unchanged when L doubles while an L extent doubles; v22 migrated with every extent in L, saved and loaded with its units; preset descriptions and presets keeping the units), tests/test_properties_compact.py (the unit button converting and storing value and unit, mm, back to L, undo), the schema version and mesh-hash tests updated to v23 (the hash payloads leave `units` out, as before).

## D-158 Every screen checked at three sizes; automated layout checks
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up items 3 and 4). Builds on D-155.
- Review: the start screen (both pages), New simulation, the main window (tree, viewer, properties, top bar, monitors), the detached properties window, the residual chart and criteria windows, the Compare tab and the Show in OpenFOAM file viewer, each at its minimum size, its default size and 1920 x 1080, at Small, Medium and Large (scripts/gui_review_sizes.py; run/gui_review/fix/<screen>/<window size>_<interface size>.png).
- Fixes: the monitors column scrolls under its header when short, with the residual chart never below 10 font heights (it was squeezed to nothing and the criteria banner cut); its header is "Residuals", and the criteria's name column, the report slots' titles and their lines elide (full text in the tooltip) instead of being cut; the column's minimum width is 16 font heights. The residual chart's legend wraps onto more rows when narrow. The detached properties window (18 x 16) and the chart and criteria windows (16 x 12, 16 x 8) have a minimum size in font heights that follows the interface size (they could be shrunk to nothing). Rows rebuilt after an edit (the mesh's layer rows, the internal-flow and report rows) are hidden as they are removed: until the event loop deleted them they were drawn over their replacements. The Show in OpenFOAM file viewer keeps at least 8 lines of the file and its wrapped notes as tall as their text at any width, and opens at 60 x 42 font heights. The New simulation dialog's fields keep their full height (only the preset list gives way).
- Check: tests/test_screen_layouts.py with tests/layout_check.py, at each interface size and the minimum size of each screen (the main window on each kind of tree node, with the longest convergence texts, after rows are rebuilt, and on the Compare tab; the detached windows; the viewer; the start screen and New simulation): no two visible leaf widgets overlap (containers ignored, 1 px tolerance), no label's text is clipped (its one-line size or its wrapped text height, descenders included, inside its geometry; an elided label passes), and no widget is cut by a squeezed parent or collapsed to nothing (a scroll area's content excepted); the checks are themselves checked on a broken layout; the legend wraps; the chart keeps its minimum height.

## D-159 An imported refinement region must be closed only to refine inside it
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up item 6; QUESTIONS 230). Amends D-145.
- Decision: an imported region's surface must be closed (watertight) only in the "inside" mode; the "within a distance" mode takes an open surface too (a sheet, a wake surface), as snappyHexMesh's distance refinement does, and the estimate counts the cells within the distance on either side (the unsigned distance). A new region from an imported file or from a part starts inside a closed surface and within a distance (one surface cell) of an open one, and the console says which; switching an open region to the inside mode is refused with the reason, and an open region left in the inside mode (its file replaced) is reported before meshing, as before. A missing or unreadable file is an error in both modes. Inward normals are flipped only for a closed surface (an open one has no inside).
- Check: tests/test_surface_regions.py (an open sheet refused inside, accepted within a distance: the points within the distance on both sides, the sampled volume against the sheet's exact Minkowski sum; closed or open told apart, an unreadable file not taken as open; an open region reported in the inside mode and meshable in the distance mode), tests/test_tree_menus.py (a closed file added inside, an open one added within a distance, the switch to inside refused).

## D-160 A snapshot samples every line plot itself
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up item 7; QUESTIONS 231). Amends D-149.
- Decision: a results snapshot (and the Compare tab's current run) samples every line plot of the project (Results > Line plot) on the case's latest results itself, as the Results Scene and the report do (the same sampling, speed and flow direction), instead of keeping only the line plots the Results Scene had already computed: a snapshot saved without ever opening the Results Scene has every line plot. A line plot that cannot be sampled (outside the fluid, or none of its fields in the results) is left out. When the Results Scene has already read the results, the snapshot samples on those (not read twice).
- Check: tests/test_snapshots.py (capture with a line plot through the fluid and one outside it: the first kept with the Results Scene's samples, the second left out; a project without line plots keeps none; the Compare tab's snapshot saved with no Results Scene shown has the line plot).

## D-161 Prism layers on a wall domain face (optional, off by default) [NUMERICS-REVIEW]
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up item 8; QUESTIONS 227). Flagged for review: it changes the snappyHexMeshDict layers when used.
- Decision: a domain face whose boundary type is a wall or a moving wall (D-147) has a **Prism layers** check box, off by default. Ticked, the face's patch is added to snappyHexMeshDict's addLayersControls/layers with the parts' layer count (the same stack, sizing and controls as the parts' walls, as the ground plane's option does, D-116). `Domain.wall_layers` lists the ticked faces (schema v23, with D-157: one unreleased version); a ticked face whose type is no longer a wall writes nothing (the tick is kept for when it is a wall again). The mesh hash carries the faces that are written, so ticking a slip face does not put the mesh out of date and a project without any keeps its hash. Presets and the snapshots' set-up rows carry the faces (a row only when written, so older snapshots compare as before). Show in OpenFOAM file maps it to addLayersControls/layers.
- Proof that nothing else changes: the public benchmarks' meshing and solver dictionaries (sphere, wing, ahmed; scripts/validation/public_dicts.py) written by this branch and by main are byte-identical (`diff -r`, 51 files, exit status 0).
- Check: tests/test_wall_layers.py (off by default, known faces once; a ticked wall or moving-wall face gets the parts' layer entry, a wall face unticked or a ticked slip face none; the hash; Show in OpenFOAM file; the check box only on wall faces, one undo step; presets and snapshot rows), the hash payload tests (tests/test_patches.py, tests/test_ground_plane.py) leaving the new field out as before.

## D-162 Public repository housekeeping
- Status: Proposed (task gui-polish, follow-up item 12).
- Decision: LICENSE holds the full GPL-3.0 text (it already did: identical to the FSF text); the README states that VortexCFD's own code is GPL-3.0-or-later and that the combined distribution is GPL-3.0 because foamlib is GPL-3.0-only. CONTRIBUTING.md gives the setup, the tests (changed modules while working, the fast suite once before a pull request), the branch and review workflow (task branches, "Task <id>: <summary>" commits, the maintainer merges) and the [NUMERICS-REVIEW] rule: a change to numerics, boundary conditions or turbulence is marked and comes with the public benchmarks' dictionary diff against main, and with before/after benchmark runs when it is meant to change results. GitHub issue templates (bug report, feature request) and a pull request template ask for the same.
- .gitignore audit: added the projects and case folders written inside the checkout (*.vcfd, case/, case_preview/, processor*/; tests/data excepted), benchmark logs (benchmarks/results/ and benchmarks/local_cases.json were already ignored; the baselines and the written-up results stay tracked), a linked or copied ~/vortexcfd-geometry, and scratch, editor and tool files. No tracked file is ignored by the new rules (`git ls-files -ci --exclude-standard` lists none).
